> **Read `TDD_02_Conventions.md` before writing any code.**
>

> must not be built, and the "MVP trim" notes remove SaaS pieces from steps that stay. Full

> the steps that name it, test changes for every step that writes tests).

---

## How to use this plan

Steps are ordered by dependency. Later steps assume earlier ones exist.
Do not jump ahead.

Each step lists:
- **Files** to create
- **TDD reference** for the specification
- **Done when** — the verifiable completion criterion

Mark a step complete only when its "done when" criterion passes.

Versions for every dependency come from `TDD_01 §2.4` and nowhere else. Before installing
a package, resolve it with Context7; if the pinned version does not exist or is
incompatible, fix the table first and say why in the commit.

---

## CI rollout and the `main` ruleset

CI is not written at the end. It starts in Step 0.1 and each step adds the job (or the check)
that the step makes possible, so GitHub verifies every step as it lands. A GitHub ruleset on
`main` grows in lockstep: **a status check is added as required only after its job has run
once on GitHub** (GitHub lists a check only after it has reported), and never before, or
every PR would be blocked.

Ruleset on `main` (created in Step 0.1): pull request required with **0 approvals** (a sole
owner cannot approve their own PR, `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7, D7), force pushes and deletion
blocked, conversations resolved, branch up to date. Job names are the ones of `TDD_03 §4`.

> **The ruleset exists (owner, 2026-10-31).** The repository became public, so rulesets work on the free
> plan. `main` has the active ruleset (id 26241021): pull request required, squash only, branch up to date,
> no bypass, and eight required checks (`lint-and-types`, `unit-tests`, `integration-tests`,
> `security-tests`, `e2e-tests`, `security-scan` for api, web and worker). `lighthouse` and `changes` are not
> required. A job joins the ruleset only after it has run once. A task below that calls its job advisory
> because there is no ruleset was written before this date.

| Step | CI work | Add to the ruleset as required |
|---|---|---|
| 0.1 | `ci.yml` with `lint-and-types` (Nx affected lint and type-check, and `format:check`); create the ruleset | `lint-and-types` |
| 0.4 | `integration-tests` job born with Postgres and Redis services: `prisma migrate deploy` on an empty database and `check-rollbacks` | `integration-tests` |
| 0.5 | Dockerfiles for `api` and `worker`; `security-scan` job born: build both images, Trivy (CRITICAL/HIGH fails; unfixed ignored); `nx affected --target=build` joins `lint-and-types` | `security-scan` |
| 0.6 | add gitleaks to `security-scan`; add commitlint, `validate:translations` and `validate:deprecations` to `lint-and-types` | none new |
| 1.1 | `unit-tests` job with Nx affected and coverage thresholds (only for paths that exist so far) | `unit-tests` |
| 1.3 | `integration-tests` now runs the integration suites; `security-tests` job born with the tenant isolation suite | `security-tests` (no bypass) |
| 2.5 | `integration-tests` also runs `pnpm test:contracts` | none new |
| 3.1 | Dockerfile for `web` added to `security-scan`; `e2e-tests` job born with Postgres, Redis, SeaweedFS and Mailpit (magic-link login); `lighthouse` job (PRs only; created in Step 3.2, PR B3c) | `e2e-tests` (`lighthouse` stays optional, as in `TDD_14 §8`) |
| 7.3 | Railway deployment config; nothing new in the ruleset | none new |

From Step 0.1 on, a step is not done until **its PR is green on GitHub** with every job that
exists at that point. Thresholds, services and secrets (for example `SNYK_TOKEN`) are added
to a job when that job is created.

### Local gates and CI performance

**What runs where.** Local gates are a convenience that catches problems in seconds; CI is the authority
and cannot be skipped (`--no-verify` is forbidden by `CLAUDE.md`, and the ruleset blocks the merge anyway).
Nothing heavy runs at commit time, so committing often stays cheap.

| Moment | What runs | Budget |
|---|---|---|
| Editor | ESLint and Prettier on save (optional, not enforced) | instant |
| `pre-commit` | `lint-staged` (ESLint `--fix` with `--max-warnings=0`, Prettier, `prisma format`) which fixes and re-stages instead of rejecting; `gitleaks` on the staged files; the translations and rollbacks validators only when such files are staged | under 10 s |
| `commit-msg` | `commitlint`, showing the exact rule that failed | under 1 s |
| `pre-push` | `pnpm validate:affected`: Nx affected `lint`, `type-check` and `test`, `format:check`, `validate:deprecations`, `validate:isolation`, translations and rollbacks; no Docker needed; skipped when only `docs/` changed | 30 to 90 s |
| Before opening a PR (manual) | `pnpm validate:ci`: the full local mirror of CI with the compose services up (integration, contracts, security tests) | minutes |
| CI | every job of `TDD_03 §4`, required by the ruleset as they are born | 5 to 15 min |
| After the merge | staging deploy from `main`, production from a version tag with manual approval (Step 7.3) | n/a |

`TDD_14 §2` calls `validate:all` the pre-push check but defines no hook for it and runs the whole
repository; this plan makes the hook real and affected-only (`validate:affected`), and keeps `validate:all`
as the full local run. `gitleaks` is a local binary (`brew install gitleaks`): the hook fails with a message that
says so, and the README lists it as a prerequisite. Husky is disabled in CI (`HUSKY=0`).

**CI performance and cost.** The repository is private, and a free GitHub plan includes a limited number of
CI minutes per month, so speed and cost are the same goal. Rules for `ci.yml` and every later workflow
(versions of the actions are resolved through Context7 when the file is written and kept current by Dependabot,
always pinned to SHAs):

- **One run per branch:** workflow-level `concurrency` (`workflow` plus `ref`) with `cancel-in-progress: true` for
  pull requests, so a new push cancels the superseded run. Never for `main` or tag runs, which must finish.
- **Least privilege and a ceiling:** `permissions: contents: read` by default, widened per job (for example
  `security-events: write` for the Trivy upload), and a `timeout-minutes` on every job.
- **Affected only:** `actions/checkout` with `fetch-depth: 0` and `filter: tree:0`, then `nrwl/nx-set-shas`, then
  `nx affected`. A docs-only change finishes in seconds.
- **Dependency cache:** `actions/setup-node` with `cache: pnpm`, and `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --prefer-offline`.
- **Nx task cache, locally only:** `lint`, `type-check`, `test`, `build` and `prisma generate` are cacheable in `nx.json`
  (`targetDefaults` with `cache: true` and `namedInputs` where `production` excludes tests and config of tests), so on a
  developer machine a task whose inputs did not change is replayed, not run. **CI does not persist this cache:** Nx 23
  refuses local cache artifacts generated on another machine ("Unrecognized Cache Artifacts"; it is a cache-poisoning
  safeguard), and every runner is a different machine, so restoring `.nx/cache` with `actions/cache` never produces a hit
  (verified on Step 0.1). CI saves time with `nx affected` and the pnpm store cache. A hosted remote cache (Nx Cloud) is
  the supported way to share task results between runs; it is a later option, not an MVP dependency.
- **Docker:** `docker/build-push-action` with the GitHub cache backend (`cache-from` and `cache-to` of `type=gha`,
  one scope per image) in `security-scan`, and the Trivy vulnerability database cached.
- **Playwright:** the browsers directory cached by Playwright version in `e2e-tests`; system dependencies installed on every run.
- **Jobs in parallel:** no `needs` chain between the test jobs, so the wall time is the slowest job, and each job
  that is not needed for a change is skipped with a **job-level `if`** (a `changes` job decides). A job skipped by
  `if` reports success to a required check; a workflow-level `paths` filter would leave the check pending and
  block the merge, so it is never used.
- **Full run as a safety net:** pull requests run affected only; pushes to `main` and a nightly `schedule` run the
  suites in full, to catch anything the project graph missed.
- **Coverage upload** runs on pull requests (for the diff) and on `main`; the heavy jobs (`e2e-tests`, `lighthouse`,
  image scans) run only when the changed projects can affect them (`lighthouse` on pull requests only).

## Milestone 0 — Foundation

No product functionality. This milestone exists so that everything after it
is fast, safe and consistent.

- [x] Configure project tags (`scope:core-domain`, `scope:core`, …) and module boundaries per `TDD_14 §6`, `§9`
- [x] `nx.json` with `namedInputs` (`default`, `production` excluding test files and test config) and `targetDefaults` that make `lint`, `type-check`, `test`, `build` and `prisma generate` cacheable, with their inputs and outputs, as described in "Local gates and CI performance"
- [x] Root `package.json` scripts as in `TDD_14 §2` (`lint`, `lint:fix`, `type-check`, `format`, `format:check`, `test` and its variants), and a `lint`, `type-check` and `test` target in every `project.json` (the `build` target comes with the Dockerfiles in Step 0.5)
- [x] `.prettierrc` per `TDD_14 §6` and a `.prettierignore` that excludes `docs/` (the specifications are maintained by hand with wide tables), generated code, build output, `node_modules` and the lockfile, so that `format:check` covers code and configuration only
- [x] `eslint.config.js` (flat) and Prettier per `TDD_14 §6` (the `.env.example` files are Step 0.2's); include a `no-restricted-syntax` rule that forbids calling `$transaction` on the Prisma client (services use `PrismaService.transaction(fn)`, created in Step 0.4; the rule exempts `PrismaService` itself)
- [x] `.github/workflows/ci.yml` with the `lint-and-types` job (actions pinned to SHAs, Node and pnpm from `package.json`; it runs Nx affected `lint` and `type-check` and `pnpm format:check`; workflow-level `concurrency` that cancels superseded pull-request runs, least-privilege `permissions`, `timeout-minutes`, `fetch-depth: 0` with `nrwl/nx-set-shas`, and the pnpm store cache), and the `main` ruleset described in "CI rollout"; after the first run, add `lint-and-types` as a required check
- [x] `.github/dependabot.yml` (weekly, grouped by ecosystem, as `TDD_03 §4` and `TDD_05 §7` already describe): `npm` for the pnpm workspace root and `github-actions` (it also moves the SHA pins and their version comments); the `docker` entries for the Dockerfiles and the compose file are added in Steps 0.2 and 0.5, when those files exist. Minor and patch updates are grouped, major updates come as separate PRs and are never merged without reading the changelog, because the stack's versions are pinned by the TDD and an upgrade re-fetches its documentation first (`CLAUDE.md`, deprecation discipline). `commit-message` prefixes `chore(deps)` (npm, docker) and `ci(deps)` (actions) so the commits satisfy commitlint, whose `deps` scope already exists; open-pull-requests limit 5
- [ ] Repository security settings, applied once with `gh api` and recorded in the README: enable Dependabot alerts and Dependabot security updates (free on private repositories). **Secret scanning and push protection are tried, not assumed:** for private repositories they belong to GitHub Secret Protection, a paid add-on, and on this account `security_and_analysis` reports nothing; verify when doing the step, enable them if the plan allows, and otherwise rely on gitleaks (pre-commit and `security-scan`, Step 0.6), which is the layer the plan depends on. Until Step 0.6 lands no secret exists in the repository, and nothing may be committed that looks like one

**Files:** `nx.json`, `tsconfig.base.json`, `package.json`, `.nvmrc`, `eslint.config.js`, `.prettierrc`, `.prettierignore`, `apps/*/project.json`, `.github/dependabot.yml`
**TDD:** `TDD_01 §1`, `§2.4`, `TDD_14 §2`, `§6`, `§9`, `TDD_03 §4`, `TDD_05 §7`
**Done when:** `pnpm nx graph` renders, `pnpm lint` passes on the empty workspace, an
import from `libs/core/config` inside `apps/web` fails lint, `cat .nvmrc` matches the
Node version pinned in `TDD_01 §2.4`, `pnpm format:check` passes and fails on a badly formatted file, a second local run of unchanged projects replays their tasks from the Nx cache, a new push cancels the previous pull-request run, `lint-and-types` is green on GitHub for a PR, GitHub reads
`dependabot.yml` without errors (Insights, Dependency graph, Dependabot) and Dependabot alerts are on.

### Step 0.4 — Database schema
- [x] `libs/core/database/prisma/schema.prisma` — the full schema with `@@map`/`@map`, generator `prisma-client`, **without the inventory models of `TDD_16 §2`**, which arrive in Step 4.2 as an additive migration, and without the new shipping tables of `TDD_17 §2` (Step 4.8); the columns that `TDD_17 §2` adds to existing models (`Order.confirmedAt`, `confirmedBy`, `confirmedVia`, `exchangeOfOrderId`, `copiedFromOrderId`, `discountReason` and the price-approval columns, status default `PENDING_CONFIRMATION`; `StoreSettings.returnShippingPayer`, `defaultCarrierConnectionId`, `bulkLabelRetentionDays`, `preferences`; `OrderItem` override and `freeShipping` columns; `Order.freeShipping`; `Product.freeShipping`; `Order.carrierConnectionId` (a plain column until Step 4.8 adds the foreign key) and `priceApprovedShippingCents`; `Customer.isCaution`, `cautionReason`, `cautionSetBy`, `cautionSetAt`) are in the initial schema. The `Shipment` COD columns and the `CarrierConnection` fee columns (`remittanceDeductsFee`, `defaultShippingFeeCents`, `minShippingFeeCents`, `maxShippingFeeCents`) belong to tables that do not exist yet: they arrive with those tables in Step 4.8

- [x] **Database roles** (decision D16, `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7): the raw SQL migration creates `platform_app` and `platform_worker` idempotently as `NOLOGIN` (the worker `BYPASSRLS`, neither a superuser), grants `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE` on all tables and sequences and sets `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` so tables created by later migrations are covered, then revokes `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on `audit_logs` from `platform_app` (`TDD_05 §9.3`; the worker keeps `DELETE` for the retention export). `scripts/set-db-role-passwords.ts` gives both roles `LOGIN` and their passwords from `DB_APP_PASSWORD` and `DB_WORKER_PASSWORD`; it runs after `migrate deploy` in `bootstrap.sh`, CI and the Railway release. Passwords are never in a migration. The script validates them with its own Zod schema, and `scripts/validate-env-example.ts` imports that schema in place of its `TOOLING_KEYS` list (Step 0.3)
- [x] Connections: the api uses `DATABASE_URL` as `platform_app`, the worker as `platform_worker`, migrations, the owner bootstrap and the dev fixtures use `DIRECT_DATABASE_URL` (owner). RLS policies are NOT enabled yet: they arrive in Step 7.1, so until then both roles behave like a normal application user
- [x] Rollback scripts in `libs/core/database/rollbacks/` + `scripts/check-rollbacks.ts`
- [x] `PrismaService` with `@prisma/adapter-pg` and the read-replica extension. Shaped from the start so Step 7.1 only changes its internals: `client` is a getter that returns the current tenant transaction when one exists and the base client otherwise (the mechanism of `TDD_01 §3.2`, verified by the 2026-10-24 spike, so Step 7.1 only adds the interceptor and the policies), services never call `client.$transaction` directly but `PrismaService.transaction(fn)`, which joins the tenant transaction when there is one (a lint rule forbids the direct call), and the replica is reached through `PrismaService.replica()`, not `client.$replica()`. In the MVP there is no replica, so `replica()` returns the same database
- [x] `libs/core/crypto` (AES-256-GCM helpers) and the seed
- [x] **MVP trim:** the schema has no `Subscription`, plan, `PlanLimits`, `OrganizationOverride`, `AiCreditBalance`, usage/billable-window, dunning, referral or staff/admin tables. They return later as additive migrations
- [x] **Owner bootstrap (closed signup):** idempotent and production-safe. Creates the system roles from `RolePresets`, the owner `User` (email from `SEED_OWNER_EMAIL`, marked verified, no password: users have none), the `Organization`, the first `Store` with `StoreSettings` and `RetentionSettings`, and the owner `Membership`. This replaces the password-based seed with test logins in `TDD_03 §5.5`: no `passwordHash`, no shared test password, no `faker`

shows every table with snake_case names (except `_CustomerTags` and `_ConversationTags`, the implicit join tables of Prisma, whose names cannot be mapped), the seed runs twice without duplicates and the

- [x] In `integration-tests`: build `api` and `worker`, start them against the CI database and check them with `scripts/smoke-http.ts` (probes, metrics, the error contract)

**TDD:** `TDD_04 §1`, `§2`, `§3`, `§4`, `TDD_02 §7`
**Done when:** `GET /health/ready` returns 200 and `GET /metrics` exposes metrics, both
**without** the `/api/v1` prefix, a request to `/api/v1/unknown` returns the error contract,
both images build and pass Trivy, and `security-scan` is green on GitHub.

### Step 0.6 — Tooling
- [x] Husky pre-commit (lint-staged + `gitleaks git --pre-commit`, failing with a clear message when `gitleaks` is not installed), commit-msg and **pre-push** hooks; the pre-push hook runs `pnpm validate:affected` (Nx affected `lint`, `type-check` and `test`, `format:check`, the validators) and is skipped when only `docs/` changed; `HUSKY=0` in CI; commitlint `ignores` commits authored by `dependabot[bot]`, whose generated titles and body lines can exceed the length rules
- [x] `scripts/validate-translations.ts`, `validate-deprecations.ts`, `check-isolation-coverage.ts`, and the `validate:affected` script (affected-only version of `validate:all`). Until their inputs exist they report and pass: translations until `apps/web/messages` exists (Step 3.1), isolation until `tests/security/tenant-isolation.spec.ts` exists (Step 1.3, which also writes the exemptions with their reasons). `validate:rollbacks` is `scripts/check-rollbacks.ts` under the name of `TDD_14 §2`, and `validate:ci` is defined when `test:contracts` and `test:security` exist
- [x] CI: add gitleaks to `security-scan` (the pinned release, checked by checksum, over the whole history), and commitlint (the commits of the pull request and its title, except the title of a Dependabot pull request), `validate:translations`, `validate:deprecations`, `validate:env` and `validate:isolation` to `lint-and-types`, which also runs the `test` targets until the `unit-tests` job of Step 1.1 (with the coverage thresholds) replaces that. The remaining jobs of `TDD_03 §4` are created by the steps listed in "CI rollout" (unit tests 1.1, security tests 1.3, e2e and Lighthouse 3.1)

**TDD:** `TDD_14 §6`, `§7`, `TDD_03 §4`
**Done when:** a commit violating conventions is rejected locally and in CI, a push with a failing affected test is stopped by the pre-push hook, a docs-only push is not slowed by it, and every job that
exists so far is green on GitHub.

## Milestone 1 — Auth and tenancy

The security foundation. Everything after this depends on it being correct.

### Step 1.0 — Queues, workers and email
Placed first in this milestone because Step 1.1 sends magic links through the email queue.
- [x] `libs/core/queues/queues.module.ts` — `@nestjs/bullmq`, seven queues plus DLQ (the `ai` queue is DEFERRED)
- [x] `apps/worker`: `LeaderLockService`; the bootstrap, the health routes and the graceful shutdown exist since Step 0.5. Readiness gains the Redis check (`RedisHealthIndicator`, `PING`, with the `ioredis` client of the cache and the queues) in the api and the worker, and in the worker also that at least one BullMQ worker is running (`TDD_04 §4`)
- [x] Email worker + `ResendProvider` (+ Mailpit dev provider) + React Email template mechanism + server-side i18n. The mechanism ships with the `MAGIC_LINK` template, because it needs one to be tested; Step 1.1 only sends it
- [x] Resend webhook with transactional idempotency. A bounce or complaint is logged (address hashed) and counted, not stored: the suppression list that replaces `markEmailInvalid` is a later step (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` → "Email build details (Step 1.0)")

**TDD:** `TDD_07 §1`, `§4`, `§5`
**Done when:** a `SEND_EMAIL` job enqueued from a test is delivered and visible in Mailpit, and
killing the worker mid-job lets the job complete or retry without loss.

### Step 1.1 — Authentication
- [x] `apps/api/src/modules/auth/` — service, controller, JWT strategy. **MVP trim — magic link only:** no password, no Google/OAuth strategy (both DEFERRED)
- [x] Access token 15 min; refresh token 7 days, hashed, family-tracked, rotated with reuse detection
- [x] Magic link login via `VerificationToken` (single-use, short expiry, hashed at rest): request link and consume link (requesting again is the resend; there is no separate resend route). Consuming is a `POST` with the token in the body, never a `GET` with the token in the URL. It issues the access/refresh pair and marks the email verified. Requesting a link for an unknown email returns the same response as for a known one (no user enumeration). Expiry and throttle values per `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7, D1, D2, held in constants
- [x] Rate limits per `TDD_05 §3.1` (Redis storage), applied to magic-link request and magic-link consume (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7, D2; the invitation accept is limited when its endpoint exists, Step 1.4) instead of the login, signup, forgot-password and resend endpoints named there
- [x] `ApiRoutes.auth` and `AuditActions` carry the magic-link request/consume routes and events; the password login, `forgotPassword`, reset and `PASSWORD_*` entries from `TDD_02`/`TDD_05` are not created
- [x] `MAGIC_LINK` email template, sent through the `SEND_EMAIL` job of Step 1.0
- [x] Bull Board at `/admin/queues`, mounted only when `BULL_BOARD_ENABLED=true`; access requires a valid JWT whose `email` claim is in `BULL_BOARD_ADMIN_EMAILS` (empty allowlist = nobody). Applied as middleware, not through `@RequirePermissions()` or `TenantGuard`, because it is cross-tenant. It lives here because it needs this step's JWT strategy. Replay of a dead-lettered job = retry the **failed job in its original queue** from Bull Board (kept by `removeOnFail`); the dead-letter queue is a triage index (see `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §3)
- [x] **MVP trim — closed signup:** there is no public signup endpoint and no Organization, Subscription or `AiCreditBalance` created here. Users come from the seed (Step 0.4) or from invitation acceptance (Step 1.4, which needs the `Invitation` model). The session issuing code (access/refresh pair) is a reusable service so Step 1.4 can call it
- [x] CI: create the `unit-tests` job (Nx affected, coverage thresholds from `TDD_13 §9` only for paths that exist so far). Adding `unit-tests` as a required check is not possible: the repository has no ruleset by the owner's decision (free plan), so the job is advisory

**TDD:** `TDD_01 §7`, `TDD_05 §3`, `TDD_07 §3`, `§5.3`
**Follow-ups found while building** (cleanup of sessions and tokens, `lower(email)` index, timing of the magic-link request, rate limit per store): `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §10. The decisions of this step that differ from the TDD: §3, "Auth build details (Step 1.1)".
**Refresh grace (owner, 2026-11-07, PR fix/refresh-grace-window):** a consumed refresh token is accepted once more within `REFRESH_REUSE_GRACE_SECONDS` (10 s) while its successor is unused (`TDD_01 §7`), because a reload with `POST /auth/refresh` in flight lost the answer and revoked the login. Known limits, accepted and tracked in Step 7.7 ("Refresh grace follow-ups"): a third simultaneous request with the same token still revokes the login (the web serializes refreshes with a Web Lock, so the browser does not produce it); the grace leaves two live tokens in the family until it ends (the list of sessions shows one); a grace can race with the use of the successor and leave two branches, as a normal grace does.
**Done when:** the seeded owner can request a magic link (visible in Mailpit), consume it,
and refresh; a magic link cannot be used twice or after expiry; a link requested for an
unknown email returns the same response as for a known one; and refresh token reuse revokes
the session family; `/admin/queues` returns 404 with the flag off, 401 without a token, 403 for an email outside the allowlist and 200 for one inside it; and `unit-tests` is green on GitHub.

### Step 1.2 — Tenancy
- [x] `TenantGuard` reading `X-Store-Id` (cached context: store, organization, membership; no subscription). Global and fail closed, with `@SkipTenant()` for the routes of a person; the cache is a `CacheService` of its own (`libs/core/redis`). `AccessLevelGuard` is DEFERRED with dunning
- [x] `@CurrentStore()`, `@CurrentUser()`, `@CurrentOrganization()` decorators
- [x] `PermissionGuard` + `@RequirePermissions()`
- [x] `AuditService` + `AuditInterceptor` + `@Audit()` (`libs/core/audit`); the refusals of both guards are audited, with a budget per user
- [x] System roles come from the owner bootstrap of Step 0.4; `seedTestContext` reuses the same function when it is created in Step 1.3 (the suites of this step seed with `apps/api/src/testing/tenant-fixtures.ts`)

**TDD:** `TDD_01 §3.2`, `TDD_02 §4`, `TDD_05 §9`
**Done when:** the tenant isolation test suite passes for a first resource, a request with
`storeId` in the body/query but no header returns `TENANT_HEADER_MISSING`, and a request to a
store the user has no membership in is rejected.

### Step 1.3 — Tenant isolation test suite
- [x] `tests/security/tenant-isolation.spec.ts` — parameterized
- [x] `tests/helpers/` — `createTestApp` (the real `AppModule`), `api()`, the clean-up of what a run created (**no truncate**, see `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §3), `seedTestContext`, token minting (user; staff DEFERRED). The app under test connects as `platform_app`, but the helpers that set up and inspect data (`seedTestContext`, builders, the clean-up, assertions that count rows) use a second client on `DIRECT_DATABASE_URL` (the owner), because `platform_app` cannot write before any user exists and, from Step 7.1, cannot see other stores' rows
- [x] `tests/builders/` — entity builders
- [x] CI: `integration-tests` now runs the integration suites; create the `security-tests` job (Postgres and Redis; SeaweedFS joins with the first test of media, Step 3.4) with the isolation suite. Making it a required check with no bypass is not possible: the repository has no ruleset by the owner's decision, so the job is advisory

**TDD:** `TDD_13 §3`, `§6`
**Done when:** the suite runs in CI as the `security-tests` job (advisory: the repository has no ruleset, so it
cannot be a required check), and `check-isolation-coverage` lists every `store_id` model.
**Every subsequent resource must be added to this suite.**

### Step 1.4 — Organizations, stores, memberships
- [x] Organization read/update and stores CRUD (`ApiRoutes.organizations.*`); there is no create-organization endpoint (the bootstrap creates it). `StoreSettings` and `RetentionSettings` are created with each store
- [x] `Invitation` flow with mandatory acceptance; `Membership` created on accept. Invitation token: hashed at rest, single-use, expiring, valid only for the invited email
- [x] `GET /invitations/:token` (public): preview only (store, role, inviter, invited email), no other data
- [x] Accept as `POST`, three cases: (a) **no account:** signup with the email fixed to the invited one, creating the `User` (email verified, since the link reached that inbox), the `Membership` and the session in one transaction; (b) **existing account:** requires a session for the invited email (magic link, then back to the invitation page), then creates the `Membership`; (c) **session for another email:** rejected with a mismatch error. Rate limits per Step 1.1
- [x] Web page `/invitations/[token]` (built in Step 3.1) handling the three cases, plus a "no access" screen for users with no membership (revoked invitation, removed from every store). **Not in this step's PR:** Step 3.1 builds and verifies it (its "Done when" lists it); this step ships the API it calls
- [x] Team management endpoints, custom roles
- [x] **MVP trim:** no `EffectivePlanLimitsService` and no `QuotaGuard(SEATS|STORES)`; seats and stores are unlimited

**TDD:** `TDD_01 §4`, `TDD_07 §5.3` (TDD_11 §1.1 effective plan limits is DEFERRED)
**Done when:** the owner can create a store, invite a member by email (no account yet), and
the invitee gets no access until the explicit accept. Accept with no account creates user,
membership and session atomically (a failure leaves no user behind); a reused, expired or
revoked token is rejected; a token cannot be accepted for a different email; signup without
a valid invitation token is rejected; and an authenticated user with no membership is
never sent to `/onboarding`.

## Milestone 2 — Core messaging

The product's reason to exist. (Queues, workers and email moved to Step 1.0, because the magic-link login of Step 1.1 sends its emails through them; this milestone therefore starts at 2.2.)

### Step 2.2 — Customers
- [x] Customers module with store-scoped `CustomerIdentity` resolution
- [x] `findOrCreateByIdentity` (with `phoneNormalized`), link identity
- [x] Addresses, notes, customer tags
- [x] Merge with `CustomerMerge` snapshot and unmerge within 30 days
- [x] Every write of data of a store takes a shared lock on the store row (`FOR KEY SHARE`, see the checklist), because deleting a store takes the exclusive one: a test writes and deletes at the same moment (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §10 item 23)
- Not in this step, and where each one goes (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` "Customers build details (Step 2.2)"): `/customers/:id/caution` Step 4.12; `/customers/:id/media` Step 3.4; `/search/customers` Step 5.1; `/customers/:id/timeline` and `/conversations` Step 3.3, `/customers/:id/orders` and the orders of the timeline Step 4.3 (new tasks there); `/customers/export` Step 3.4 (new task there; the columns and rules are decided, in §10 item 25)

**TDD:** `TDD_01 §4`, `TDD_09 §6`
**Done when:** two identities on different channels can be merged into one customer and
unmerged; the same phone number exists as two customers in two stores.

### Step 2.3 — Conversations and messages
- [x] Conversations module with the status state machine (`updateStatus`, reasons, actors) and read state; a conversation is created under `FOR KEY SHARE` on its customer (the same lock as the store's, owner's decision in Step 2.2), using the survivor if the customer was absorbed by a merge (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §10 item 27)
- [x] Messages module: `POST /messages` with `idempotencyKey`, the thread, internal notes with mentions; a reply is stored `queued` and nothing sends it until Step 2.6 (`mediaIds` moved to Step 3.4, `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` "Messages build details")
- [x] `MessagingWindowService` (`GET /conversations/:id/messaging-window`), and `MessagesService.create` calls it before it stores a reply: a closed window answers `CHANNEL_WINDOW_EXPIRED` (`TDD_10 §4`). Channels have no policy until Step 2.5 binds the registry (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` "Messaging window and routing build details")
- [x] Cursor pagination per `TDD_08 §2.4`, list view model per `TDD_09 §2` (with the counts of the filters, `countsOnly`)
- [x] Tags, snippets, routing rules, business hours, store settings endpoints (tags, snippets, business hours and inbox settings done in #45; conversations, read state, cursor and counts in #46; routing rules and `RoutingService.apply` in PR 4)

**TDD:** `TDD_08 §2.4`, `TDD_09 §2`, `§3`, `§4`, `TDD_02 §5`, `TDD_06 §4`, `TDD_10 §4`, `TDD_11 §7.2`
**Done when:** conversations list under 300ms p95 with 10k seeded rows, invalid status
transitions are rejected, and `POST /messages` with a repeated `idempotencyKey` returns the
existing message.

### Step 2.4 — Realtime
Two pull requests (owner, 2026-10-26): (1) domain events, (2) gateway, `RealtimeService` and presence. The browser side of the step (connection manager, `useRealtimeSync`, offline queue) moved to Step 3.1, where the web app gets its auth and `apiClient`: it cannot be built or shown working before that.
- [x] Install `@nestjs/event-emitter` (12.0.1, the line of Nest 12; `TDD_01` said 3.0.1) and the `DomainEvents` service in `libs/core/event-bus`: a service announces with `publish(tx, event, payload)` and the event leaves after the transaction commits (`PrismaService.afterCommit`), never for one that rolls back, and a failing listener cannot fail the request
- [x] Emit the `Events.CUSTOMER_*` of `CustomersService` (created, updated, merged, unmerged, identity linked), which Step 2.2 left out because nothing listened to them yet
- [x] Emit from `MessagesService` `Events.MESSAGE_QUEUED` (new: `Events.MESSAGE_SENT` stays for a message the channel took) for a stored reply and `Events.MESSAGE_NOTE_ADDED` (new, without the text of the note) for a note, and `Events.MESSAGE_MENTION` for each mention, which Step 3.5 turns into `NotificationTypes.CONVERSATION_MENTIONED`; from `ConversationsService` the `Events.CONVERSATION_STATUS_CHANGED`, `CONVERSATION_ASSIGNED` (an unassignment is the same event with `assignedTo: null`) and `CONVERSATION_READ`. `CONVERSATION_CREATED` is emitted by Step 2.6, because its payload carries the first message and a conversation is born with it; the unassignment that removing a member does is not announced yet
- [x] Counts of the inbox filters: not cached (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` item 31). Measured by `conversations-performance.spec.ts` over 10 000 conversations: under the 300 ms limit (about 17 ms by hand in Step 2.3). The realtime client refetches them debounced, so the cache would add an invalidation on every write for a query that costs that little
- [x] Socket.io gateway with the ioredis adapter, typed events, a store room and a user room; a conversation room is joined only for a conversation of that store that the user sees (the same visibility as the REST route), and relays typing
- [x] `RealtimeService` + event listeners + `EVENTS_BRIDGE`. What goes out carries ids and no text, and an event about a conversation reaches only the sockets whose user may see it (a note, only who may read notes)
- [x] Per-socket presence with heartbeat and away detection

**TDD:** `TDD_06 §1`–`§5`
**Done when:** two sockets of one store receive each other's events, a socket of store B receives nothing of store A and cannot join its conversations, and closing one of two sockets keeps the user online.

### Step 2.5 — Plugin SDK and registry
Two pull requests (owner, 2026-10-27): (1) the SDK, the registries, the module and the contract suite; (2) the open channel ids and the binding of the messaging window (the checkpoints of PR 1 are checked below, the rest wait for PR 2). Build decisions: `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` row "Plugin SDK build details (Step 2.5)".
- [x] `libs/plugins/sdk` — `ChannelPlugin`, `ChannelEvent` (with `connectionId`), `ChannelContext`, types, `PluginError`, `createPluginLogger`, and (owner, 2026-10-27, `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §9) `CarrierPlugin` with its types and typed errors (the `httpClient` helper waits for Step 2.7, its first user)
- [x] `libs/plugins/runtime` — `PluginRegistry` with contract validation, a `CarrierRegistry` beside it with the same fail-fast capability check, `PluginsModule.forRoot`, keyed by string ids (`TDD_10 §3`, §6.7.3: per process, `list(storeId)`; the Redis `plugins:changed` refresh and the 60 s re-read arrive with the remote plugins in Step 5.6) — PR 1
- [x] Bind `MESSAGING_WINDOW_POLICIES` (`apps/api/src/modules/messaging-window`) to `plugin.messagingWindow` and test it with a plugin of the suite (the policies of WhatsApp, Instagram and Messenger arrive with those plugins in Steps 2.7 and 2.8, each with its own)
- [x] Open channel ids (owner, 2026-10-26; `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` "Open channel ids"): a channel is the id of a registered plugin, validated against the registry, and not a value of the closed enum `ChannelType`. `ChannelType` stays as an alias of the list of the official ids (`ChannelIds`, with `CHANNEL_ID_PATTERN`, `officialChannelSchema` and `registeredChannelSchema` of `TDD_10 §6.7.1`), and the code that takes a channel (the `channelType` columns, the filters of the list and of the routing conditions, `channelOf`, the labels and icons) takes a string that the registry vouches for; the texts to bring along are `TDD_01` (schema), `TDD_02` (constants), `TDD_03`, `TDD_07` (job types), `TDD_09` (filters), `TDD_11` (`AvailableChannels`, `ChannelGuard`), `TDD_13` and `TDD_14`. The label, the icon, the capabilities and the messaging window of a channel come from its plugin. `TDD_10 §3` and §10 are already written for string ids (`TDD_10 §6.7`)
- [x] `tests/contracts/channel-plugin.contract.ts` with per-plugin fixtures (PR 1; the carrier suite and the fake carrier are Step 4.8)
- [x] CI: `integration-tests` also runs `pnpm test:contracts` (PR 1)

**TDD:** `TDD_10 §2`, `§3`, `§6.7.1`, `§9`
**Done when:** the contract suite exists, runs in `integration-tests`, and the registry rejects a plugin that
declares a capability it does not implement.

### Step 2.6 — Inbound pipeline
- [x] (PR 1/4) First, move what the worker needs out of `apps/api/src/modules` into `libs/core/messaging` (an Nx library both apps import; the api keeps its controllers): `ConversationsService`, the identity part of `CustomersService`, `RoutingService`, `MessagingWindowService` and the messages writer. `TDD_10 §4` already puts `InboundMessageService` there, and the worker cannot import from `apps/api`; the isolation suite and the tests that import them move with them
- [x] (PR 2/4, with the `reopenWindowDays` migration: `reopen_window_days` is nullable, `null` by default, and `TDD_01`, `TDD_09 §3.3` and the inbox settings API say so) System lines in the thread: `ConversationsService.applyStatus` writes a `MessageType.SYSTEM` message in the same transaction for each change of status that people care about (resolved, reopened, archived, marked as spam or not, snoozed and woken) and `assign` / `assignIfFree` one for each assignment, authored by `system`, with the event, the people and the dates in `metadata` and no text. They do not count as the last message of the list, do not move `lastMessageAt`, do not raise `unreadCount`, are left out of the exports of the customer's messages, and a bulk action writes one for each conversation. Step 3.2 only draws them
- [x] (PR 3/4) `ChannelWebhookController` (verify, normalize, enqueue) + `InboundProcessor`
- [x] (PR 3/4; the reading of the connection is `ChannelConnectionResolver`, the management comes in PR 4/4) `InboundMessageService`: connection → store resolution, identity, reopen/wake/create, dedupe, media job, routing (`RoutingService.apply(storeId, conversationId, { text, receivedAt })`, for a conversation it creates and for one it reopens that has no assignee or whose assignee left; the channel and the customer are read from the conversation), `CustomerIdentity.lastInboundAt` (it opens the messaging window), events (billing window DEFERRED). A message of the customer continues the conversation that customer already has on that channel (owner, 2026-10-26: one continuous thread per customer and channel, like a chat, with the whole history in it): it wakes a snoozed one and reopens a resolved or an archived one through `ConversationsService.applyStatus` (actor `'system'`, reason `CUSTOMER_REPLIED`), the previous assignee keeping it. `StoreSettings.reopenWindowDays` changes with it: it becomes optional, `null` (the default) meaning a new conversation is never started and a number meaning one is started after that many quiet days; the migration with its rollback, the inbox settings API and the text of `TDD_09 §3.3` change in the first PR of this step, and a spam one stays spam (stored, no reopening); it moves `lastMessageAt` and raises `unreadCount` and `lastInboundAt`, and does not touch `lastOutboundAt` or `firstResponseAt` (those are the agent's, set by `recordReply`)
- [x] (PR 5/5) Outbound: the queue and job constants in `libs/core`, and a processor that takes a `queued` reply of `MessagesService`, calls the plugin's `sendMessage` and writes `sent` or `failed` (`deliveryError` holds an `ErrorCode`), with the provider's `delivered`/`read` status callbacks written by the inbound path; the channel limits of the text (WhatsApp 4096, Messenger 2000, Instagram 1000) are checked here, because `POST /messages` only caps at 4096
- [x] (PR 4/5; the outbound processor is PR 5/5) `ChannelConnection` management + `libs/core/crypto` encryption + health scheduler
- [ ] **MVP trim:** no `UsageMeteringService`; inbound conversations are never metered or blocked

**TDD:** `TDD_10 §4`, `§10`
**Done when:** a `queued` reply is sent through a test plugin and becomes `sent`, a simulated webhook addressed to store B's connection creates customer,
conversation and message in store B only, a replayed webhook creates nothing, and the
endpoint answers in under 300ms p99 while the worker does the work.

- [x] Add each plugin (`instagram`, `messenger`, `web_widget`) to `OFFICIAL_CHANNEL_PLUGINS`, each running its contract suite with `{ official: true }` (Instagram and Messenger in PR 1c, the web widget in PR 2a)
- [x] `MetaChannelPlugin` base class; Instagram and Messenger plugins with capability declarations (PR 1c: `libs/channels/meta` base, `libs/channels/instagram`, `libs/channels/messenger`, both in `OFFICIAL_CHANNEL_PLUGINS`; text, media by url, `is_echo`, reactions both ways, ad `referral`, 24 h window with 7 days after an ad)
- [x] **Web widget, server side (owner, 2026-10-29; PR 2a):** `libs/channels/web-widget` (`WebWidgetPlugin`, declares `TEXT` only and has no messaging window; `connect({ siteOrigins })` makes a site key `pk_widget_…` that is the `connectionId`, keeps the normalized origins in `metadata.allowedOrigins` and no credentials). The visitor token is an HMAC-SHA256 over `{siteKey, visitorId, exp}` (1 hour) whose key is derived from `JWT_SECRET` with a purpose label, so no new variable exists. The token is to the widget what the signature is to a provider: `validateWebhookSignature` reads it from the header `x-widget-token`, and `handleWebhook` reads `{visitorToken, clientMessageId, content}` and emits `message_received` with `externalId` `<visitorId>:<clientMessageId>`, so that sending twice stores once and two visitors cannot collide. Api module `widget`: `POST /widget/bootstrap` (token, store name, `away` from the business hours, the last 50 messages of that visitor: text that reached them, not notes, system lines, queued or failed replies, nor deleted messages), `POST /widget/messages` (queued through the new `InboundEventQueue`, shared with the provider webhook, so the worker stores, routes and announces it like any other), per-request CORS against `allowedOrigins` (the preflight, which carries no key, is answered for the origin that asks and grants nothing; the request that follows checks the list and only then lets the browser read the answer), limits of 30 and 60 a minute per address, and the `/widget` socket namespace (token in `auth`, a listed origin, 5 sockets per visitor, closed when the token expires) with `WidgetDeliveryListener`, which pushes a reply the channel took (`MESSAGE_SENT`) to the room of its visitor as `widget.message` (id, `from` visitor or team, text, date; nothing about agents). Cases in `tests/api/widget.spec.ts`, and "a widget site key" in `tenant-isolation.spec.ts` (a key from the site of another store, and a visitor id that exists in two stores). **Left for later:** files from the visitor and `POST /widget/upload-url` (Step 3.4), typing and read receipts (the typing task of Step 3.2: `TYPING_INDICATOR`, `CUSTOMER_TYPING` and `READ_RECEIPTS` are declared only then), the pre-chat form and linking by email (Step 3.3), and the bundle (PR 2b). **Known limits:** the site-key lookup and the history read run with no store context, so Step 7.1 turns them into the `SECURITY DEFINER` function it already lists; the connection is read from the database on every call (cache it if it shows in the P99); a visitor past 5 tabs is refused, not rotated, and the count is looked at in the handshake, so a burst of connects at once can pass it by a few; a visitor token cannot be revoked, so after a disconnect only the replies stop at once (the listener asks for an active connection), while a removed origin (also by `PUT .../widget/origins`, which refuses new calls at once but does not close the sockets already open) or a copied token lives until the token expires (1 hour); the listener reads one message for every `MESSAGE_SENT` of any channel, which is cheap (one row by id) and can be narrowed with a marker in the bridged payload if outbound volume grows; the generic webhook answers 404 for `web_widget` by id, so a second channel with a door of its own would need a flag on the plugin instead of another `!==`; the token key is derived from `JWT_SECRET`, so rotating it ends every open widget session and the `.env.example` placeholder is accepted outside staging and production exactly as it is for the access tokens
- [x] `apps/widget` bundle (PR 2b): vanilla TS built by Vite under 40 KB gzipped, launcher, panel with the store name, message list, composer, the "we're away" banner from `away`, i18n from `Store.defaultLocale`, no inline script or style (it must work under a strict CSP of the merchant), the `visitorId` in `localStorage`, bootstrap at load, a socket on `/widget` that asks for a new token and reconnects when the old one expires, the embed snippet in Settings → Channels, and a way to change the origins of a connection without changing its site key (`connect` makes a new key each time, so today adding a domain would break the snippets already published; an update of `metadata.allowedOrigins` for the connection of a store is needed, with the error of the bad entry named by its position). **Built in PR 2b (2026-10-29):** Vite 8.3.5 library mode, one IIFE `widget.js` of about 25 KB gzipped (`scripts/check-widget-size.ts` fails the build over `WidgetLimits.BUNDLE_MAX_GZIP_BYTES`; `treeshake.moduleSideEffects: false` keeps the zod schemas of the `@platform/core/*` barrels out of it). The snippet is a script tag with `data-site-key`, `data-api-url` and an optional `data-locale`; the language is the snippet's, then `Store.defaultLocale` (now in the bootstrap as `store.locale`), then `navigator.language`, in the two languages the widget has (es, en), kept in `apps/widget/src/i18n.ts`. Styles are a constructable stylesheet in a shadow root, so no `<style>` or style attribute exists; a browser without constructable stylesheets gets no widget. `GET /channels/connections/:id/widget` returns the site key, the origins and the snippet (`WIDGET_SCRIPT_URL` in the env), and `PUT /channels/connections/:id/widget/origins` replaces the origins and keeps the key (the bad entry is named by its position, audited as `channel.widget_origins_changed`); both are drawn by Step 3.6. Seen working in Chrome under `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; connect-src <api>` against a stand-in server with the contract of the api; the api and the widget together in a browser are covered by `tests/api/widget.spec.ts` only. **Known limits:** a visitor who sends from two tabs sees the other tab's message after the next history read, not at once (the socket carries replies of the team only); the unread dot counts nothing and clears on opening
- [x] Origin of a conversation, channel-agnostic (owner's idea, 2026-10-28): an optional `origin` on the message of `ChannelEvent` (`kind` ad, post or other; `sourceId`, `sourceUrl`, `title`, `body`, `media` with its type and either a provider media reference or a url, plus a thumbnail; `clickId`) that each plugin fills from what its provider sends (the `referral` of a Click-to-WhatsApp ad on WhatsApp; `referral.ads_context_data` on Instagram and Messenger, whose page must also subscribe to `messaging_referrals`). It lands in the first PR of this step so that Instagram and Messenger are born with it; WhatsApp is mapped there too, and the webhook schema keeps validating each part alone. The core stores it as `ConversationOrigin` (conversation, message, the fields above), because one conversation can come from several ads: the same `sourceId` in a row is one entry, the first is the first contact and the latest is the one the thread shows. The provider's raw `referral` is kept in a JSON column. A new tenant-scoped table: migration with its rollback, a case in `tenant-isolation.spec.ts`. Open questions that the docs and a real ad must answer first: `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md`, "WhatsApp plugin build details", item 14
- [x] Several attachments in one inbound message (owner, 2026-10-28): `ChannelEvent.message.media` becomes an array `media[]` (SDK, `channelEventSchema`, `insertInboundMessage`, one `fetch-media` job per media, WhatsApp emits an array of one). The core stores one message with N `Media`
- [ ] Messages written in the provider's own app (owner, 2026-10-28): a new SDK event `message_echo` (customer's `channelIdentity`, the message with its `media[]`, `externalId`) that the core stores as a sent message of an external author (`AuthorType.EXTERNAL`, no `authorId`). It is deduplicated against our own sends by `channelMessageId` (the provider echoes those too), and it neither opens the messaging window nor raises the unread count nor is routed. A conversation that is resolved, snoozed or archived is reopened, as a reply of an agent does (owner, 2026-10-28), never spam; a customer with no conversation gets one, unassigned and read, announced as created. It retries while one of our own replies to that customer has no provider id yet, and stores it as the owner's on the last attempt. This replaces "an echo is dropped and counted" of `TDD_10 §6.7`. **Built in PR 1b:** the core, the SDK and schema, the worker case, and the WhatsApp mapping of `smb_message_echoes` (Meta's reference, read 2026-10-28: `message_echoes[]`, `from` the business, `to` the customer; `revoke` and `edit` are skipped; a reaction becomes an external reaction). That field only exists for a number in Coexistence, which needs Embedded Signup (Phase 2), so it is proved by unit tests until a real number does (a box in Step 7.3). **Built in PR 1c:** Instagram and Messenger map `is_echo` (an echo whose sender is not the account is skipped). **Still open:** the app must be subscribed to the `smb_message_echoes` field
- [ ] Reactions (moved here from an orphan decision of `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md`, "WhatsApp plugin build details", item 11, which no step carried): the `message_reactions` table, the SDK event `message_reaction`, `message.reaction` in real time, reactions returned with each message, WhatsApp emitting them instead of dropping them, Instagram (`message_reactions`) and Messenger mapping them, and the `REACTIONS` capability declared by the plugins that map them. **Built in PR 1b:** the table with its rollback (a customer by identity, an agent by user, the owner of the provider's app as one `external` reactor), the event and its schema, `MessageReactionsService`, `message.reaction`, `reactions` in each message of the thread, and WhatsApp emitting them and declaring `REACTIONS`. **Owner, 2026-10-28: an agent reacting is in the MVP too, built in PR 1b-2:** `PUT /messages/:id/reaction` (`AuthGuard` + `TenantGuard` + `MESSAGES_SEND`; body `{ emoji }`, null takes it back), `ChannelPlugin.sendReaction` (required by `REACTIONS`, implemented by WhatsApp), the job `SEND_OUTBOUND_REACTION` on the outbound queue, `MessageReaction.deliveryStatus` / `deliveryError` (queued, sent or failed, with a code of ours; shown in each reaction of the thread so that nobody believes the customer saw what the channel refused), and the `messageReaction` case in `tenant-isolation.spec.ts`, which removes the exemption. The core accepts any message that has a `channelMessageId` and a channel that declares `REACTIONS`; the plugin refuses what its provider does not allow. **Known limits:** a reaction taken back that the channel refuses is logged, not shown (its row is gone); a reaction left `queued` because Redis was down is queued again by the same sweep as the replies (`OutboundRequeueScheduler`, with the version it had) and marked failed after an hour, so it never stays "sending". two jobs of the same reaction that run at the same time (an emoji and the next one, a few milliseconds apart) are not ordered, so the provider can end with the older emoji while the thread shows the newer one as sent; the version guard only stops the older job from settling. **Built in PR 1c:** Instagram and Messenger map the customer's reactions (`reaction{action, emoji, mid}`; `unreact` takes it back) and send an agent's (`sender_action` `react` / `unreact`), both declaring `REACTIONS`. The reaction of the account itself is not read: it comes back as the echo of our own send
- [x] **Delete and edit a message (owner, 2026-10-28; PR 1d, after 1c):** the core stays channel-agnostic and the plugin decides what its API allows. (1) **Delete is for everyone only:** there is no "delete for me", because in a team inbox it would show the agent a thread the customer does not have (a junk or abusive inbound message is handled with the `spam` or `archived` status of the conversation, a wrong reply with a correction message). Capability `DELETE_MESSAGE`, optional `ChannelPlugin.deleteMessage`, the same queued, sent or failed state as a reaction, shown in the thread; a channel without the capability answers 409 and the inbox hides the action. The permission `MESSAGES_DELETE` exists and has no handler on purpose until now. (2) **Edit reaches the customer or does not happen:** capability `EDIT_MESSAGE`, optional `editMessage`; without it the core answers 409, never an edit that only the inbox shows, and the inbox offers "send a correction" (a new message). Revisions are kept (`MessageRevision`, tenant-scoped, with its isolation case and rollback). (3) **Internal notes** are working data shared by the team, so anyone who can see notes (`CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` and `MESSAGES_INTERNAL_NOTE`) can edit one; the author stays the author, every edit keeps a revision (who, when, the text before) and the note shows "edited by"; an edit carries the version it saw and a stale one answers 409 `RESOURCE_CONFLICT` with the current text, so nobody overwrites a change they did not see. Deleting a note is for its author or `MESSAGES_DELETE`. Edits and deletes are pushed in real time (`message.edited`, `message.deleted`, as `message.reaction` is), so a teammate sees the change without reloading. (4) **Inbound:** SDK events `message_deleted` and `message_edited` for the customer and for the provider's own app (the echo of WhatsApp that carries `revoke` and `edit` is skipped today), applied by the core whatever the author. (5) **A message the customer deleted** shows "the customer deleted this message" to the team; the original text is kept for a short time (store preference, 30 days by default) for disputes, shown only with an explicit permission and every view audited, then a job purges it. (6) **Permissions (owner, 2026-10-28):** no `MESSAGES_EDIT`. A user with `MESSAGES_SEND` edits and deletes their own message to the customer (when the channel can); `MESSAGES_DELETE` (only `admin` has it) deletes the message of another team member; nobody edits the message of another person, a correction is a new message; nobody deletes a message of the customer, it becomes the tombstone of (5). Every delete is audited with who and when **Built in PR 1d (2026-10-29):** the capabilities `DELETE_MESSAGE` and `EDIT_MESSAGE` and the optional hooks `deleteMessage` / `editMessage` (the registry requires the hook of a declared capability); no official plugin declares them, because Meta offers no API to delete or edit a sent message (WhatsApp Cloud API, Messenger and Instagram: nothing in Meta's list of webhook events or send endpoints, read 2026-10-29), so the outbound path is proved with the contract plugin of the suite and a plugin that can (a remote one) needs no change in the core. `PATCH /messages/:id` and `DELETE /messages/:id`, `GET /messages/:id/deleted-content`; `MessageRevision` (migration with its rollback, which also gives the new permission to the Admin roles that exist); `Message.version`, `editedAt/By`, `pendingContent` + `editStatus`, `deletedAt/By`, `deleteStatus`, `changeRequestedAt`, `contentPurgedAt`; jobs `SEND_OUTBOUND_EDIT` and `SEND_OUTBOUND_DELETE` on the outbound queue (a sweep queues again after 2 minutes and fails after an hour, as for reactions); `message.edited` and `message.deleted` in real time (a note only to who may read notes); permission `MESSAGES_VIEW_DELETED` (Admin only) for the kept text of a deleted message, every read audited (`message.deleted_content_viewed`) and every delete audited (`message.deleted`); the purge is an hourly cron (`PURGE_DELETED_MESSAGES`) with `DeletedMessageRetention.DAYS` = 30, which also clears the text of the revisions; inbound SDK events `message_edited` and `message_deleted` applied by `InboundMessageChangesService` whatever the author (the lookup of the target message is shared with reactions in `InboundMessageTargets`); Messenger maps `message_edits` and the `is_deleted` flag of `messages` (both directions: the customer's, and the owner's own echo). **Decisions made while building:** (a) an edit of a reply the customer has changes nothing in the thread until the channel takes it (`pendingContent`, `pendingEdit` in the view), and one the channel refuses leaves the text as it was; (b) a deletion the channel has not taken leaves the message shown with `deleteStatus` queued; (c) the retention is a constant, not a store preference (Phase 2, as the profile staleness); (d) `deletedContent` answers 404 for a message that was not deleted or whose text is purged, and for a note to who may not read notes; (e) an agent's deletion of their own reply needs `MESSAGES_SEND`, the deletion of another person's `MESSAGES_DELETE`. **Bulk deletion measured (2026-10-29):** a customer who deletes many messages at once is one `message_deleted` event and one job for each message; 100 messages of one customer with 20 jobs at a time take about 0.5 s in `tests/api/inbound-message-changes.spec.ts` and leave 100 tombstones and 100 `message.deleted` events, so no batching was built; the cap of 100 events per request only splits the queueing in batches, it drops nothing. The client of Step 3.1 must debounce its invalidation of the thread. **Not verified with a real payload:** the `message_edits` shape of Messenger (`message_edit{mid, text, num_edit}`, taken from Meta's reference) and the `is_deleted` of `messages` (a box in Step 7.3); Instagram's `message_edit` event (changelog of 2026-09) carries no text as far as known, so the plugin skips it, and Instagram's unsend arrives through `is_deleted`. **Follow-ups, none blocking:** (1) the search over message text (`search_vector`) is generated from `content`, so a deleted message stays searchable until it is purged: the search of Step 5.1 must filter `deleted_at IS NULL`; (2) the media of a deleted message stays until Step 3.4 decides what the purge does with files; (3) the thread of Step 3.2 draws the tombstone, "edited", the pending state and the failed one, and the `version` it sends back when editing a note; (4) `editedById` and `deletedById` have no foreign key to `users`, like an audit trail: the user may be removed and the name goes with the member list; (5) editing a note keeps its `mentions` as they were (no one is notified again, and a removed mention stays): decide with the thread of Step 3.2 whether an edit recomputes them; (6) one request at a time for a message: asking to delete a reply whose edit waits answers 409 (and the other way round), so each settles on its own; (7) the purge takes the oldest due rows first across all stores, which is fair enough for one owner and becomes a per-store batch in Phase 2
- [x] 7-day window after an ad (Instagram and Messenger; Meta, changelog of 2026-09-28: up to 7 days from the first message after a Click-to-Messenger or Click-to-Direct ad, 24 h from any other entry point): `CustomerIdentity.adWindowOpenedAt` (migration with rollback) set by `InboundMessageService` when that first message carries an ad `origin`; `MessagingWindowPolicy` gains `extendedHoursAfterAd` (168); `stateOf` takes the later of `lastInboundAt + hours` and `adWindowOpenedAt + extendedHoursAfterAd`. Meta says "up to", so a send refused for a closed window must map to `CHANNEL_WINDOW_EXPIRED`; the exact error code and subcode need a real ad (Step 7.3)
- [x] Contract tests for all three (`tests/contracts`: `instagram-`, `messenger-` and `web-widget-plugin.contract.spec.ts`)
- [ ] **PR split (owner, 2026-10-28; 1c-2 added 2026-10-29; 2 split into 2a and 2b 2026-10-29), eight PRs, one per session:** 1a (merged, #65) = `media[]`, `origin` (`ConversationOrigin`), `adWindowOpenedAt` and WhatsApp adapted to them; 1b (merged, #66) = `message_echo` and `message_reaction` with WhatsApp mapping both; 1b-2 (merged, #67) = an agent reacts (endpoint, plugin send, isolation case); 1c (merged, #69) = `MetaChannelPlugin`, Instagram and Messenger (text, media, `is_echo`, reactions both ways, ad `referral`, capabilities in the connection views, contract tests; Instagram through Instagram Login on `graph.instagram.com`, checklist item 12 (moved to the unified login on `graph.facebook.com` on 2026-11-05, same item); typing and read receipts wait for the typing task of the interface milestone); 1c-2 (merged, #70) = name and photo of an Instagram or Messenger customer (below); 1d (merged, #71) = delete and edit a message (above); 2a (merged, #72) = web widget, server side (above); 2b (#73) = web widget bundle
- [x] Channel capabilities to the browser (owner, 2026-10-28; built in PR 1c, `capabilities` in `ChannelConnectionView` from `PluginRegistry.capabilitiesOf`): the connection views (`GET /channels/connections` and `/:id`) return the `capabilities` that the plugin of each connection declares (`ChannelCapability` constants), so the web app decides which actions to draw (react, edit, delete, templates, typing) without knowing any channel by name; a remote plugin declares its own through its manifest. They are static per plugin: if a capability one day depends on the connection (a permission granted), the view widens then
- [ ] **For a future third-party plugin that answers customers who come from an ad (owner, 2026-10-28; nothing of it is built now):** (1) the `origin` of the message travels in the `message.received` webhook to plugins (`TDD_10 §6.5`, not built yet: only the `TRIGGER_PLUGIN_WEBHOOK` queue name exists) together with `messageId` and `conversationId`; it is per message, not per conversation, because one conversation can receive several ads. (2) Scope: the origin goes in `messages:read` (owner decision), with `kind`, `sourceId`, `title`, `body` and `media`, which identify the ad; `clickId` and `sourceUrl` stay out of the payload until a plugin needs them, with a scope of their own. The step that builds `§6.5` carries this. (3) Note: `Events.MESSAGE_SENT` carries only `storeId`, `conversationId`, `messageId` and `visibility` (`MessageStatusPayload`), not `authorType` or `createdAt`, so a plugin cannot tell that a human took over after a given message. Enrich that payload in the step that builds `§6.5`; do not build it now
- [x] **Name and photo of an Instagram or Messenger customer (owner, 2026-10-29; built in PR 1c-2):** the webhooks carry only the scoped id, so the customer shows as a number. Optional `ChannelPlugin.fetchProfile(ctx, channelIdentity)` returning `{ name?, username?, avatarUrl? }`, required by a new capability `CUSTOMER_PROFILE`. Instagram: `GET graph.instagram.com/<IGSID>?fields=name,username,profile_pic` (name may be null). Messenger: `GET /<PSID>?fields=first_name,last_name,profile_pic`, which needs the "Business Asset User Profile Access" feature requested in the Page settings (an owner step, documented in the connect view). A worker job `FETCH_CUSTOMER_PROFILE` on a queue of its own, `customer-profiles` (5 at a time, 3 attempts, exponential 60 s; a provider that answers slowly fills it and delays no customer message; the heavy queue has no consumer until Step 3.4 and one added now would swallow the `fetch-inbound-media` jobs that wait there, and the inbound queue is where messages wait), one per identity (job id), queued when the identity is created and again when `profileFetchedAt` is older than 7 days (a constant in `libs/core`), always after the message is stored, never inside the webhook and never blocking it. **Stored in new columns of `CustomerIdentity`** (migration with its rollback; the table is already covered by the isolation spec): `username`, `avatarUrl`, `profileFetchedAt`, `profileRetryAfter`, `profileFetchError` (a code of ours). No index: staleness is decided from the identity row an inbound message already reads, and no sweep exists. `displayName` takes the name; a read that finds something replaces name, username and photo link together (the stored link expires), and one that finds nothing keeps what was stored; `Customer.name` is set only while it is null, because the customer's own name is the agents'. A failure retries three times with backoff; when they run out, `profileFetchError` is written, and `profileFetchedAt` too only if the failure was a refusal, so that a refused read is not asked again until the profile is stale; an outage is no answer, so it sets `profileRetryAfter` (one hour ahead) and leaves the date of the last real read alone, and the connection view can tell the owner what is missing. **The photo is a reference only:** no storage exists until Step 3.4 and a plugin never stores anything (the core stores what `fetchMedia` and `fetchProfile` hand over); Meta's urls are signed and expire, so Step 3.4 carries the avatar download through the SSRF-safe fetch. **Built:** `fetchProfile` returns null when Meta has no profile to show (an empty object, or error 2018218 of an account made with a phone number), which is stamped like a read and never retried; `ProfileRequester` (after a stored customer message) and `ProfileFetcher` (the job) in `apps/worker/src/inbound`, `CustomerProfileService` in `libs/core/messaging`, and the `CUSTOMER_PROFILE` capability mapped to Step 3.2 in `scripts/frontend-coverage-map.ts`. Display fallback, drawn in Step 3.2: name, then `@username`, then "Instagram customer ····1234", with an initials avatar
- [ ] Follow-ups of the profile fetch found in the review of PR 1c-2, none blocking: (1) the requester repeats the connection and identity lookups that `handleMessageReceived` already did, two indexed queries per Instagram or Messenger message; return the identity from the service and check `needsProfileRead` on it first; (2) the plugin call is not aborted when the 10 s timeout fires, because `ChannelContext` carries no `AbortSignal`: add one to the SDK and to `MetaGraphClient`; (3) Step 3.4, when it stores the avatar, must fetch it through the SSRF-safe fetch with an https-only, public-host check, and Step 3.2 must draw the stored link with the CSP `img-src` of the app (a link the plugin chose is untrusted); (4) a username the channel stops giving stays until a read gives another (the photo link is the only field replaced as a whole)
- [ ] Phase 2, SaaS follow-ups of the profile fetch, recorded so they are not lost: (1) the 7-day staleness becomes a store preference (`libs/core/platform/config/store-preferences.ts`, default 7) once stores are not all the owner's; (2) a budget for profile calls per Meta app across tenants (Graph rate limits count per app), with the job spreading its calls instead of bursting when many tenants import a backlog; (3) the Messenger "Business Asset User Profile Access" step disappears for stores that connect through Facebook Login for Business, which grants it in the flow; (4) an erased customer (`Customer.erasedAt`) clears `username` and `avatarUrl` too, and the job skips it
- [ ] Phase 2, recorded so it is not lost: WhatsApp Embedded Signup (it needs the platform to be a Tech Provider, business verification and App Review) and Facebook Login for Business for accounts of other people. **Facebook Login (classic) for Messenger and Instagram, for the owner, moved to Step 7.8 (owner, 2026-11-05).** Messenger buttons and templates (`sendButtons`, `BUTTONS`; `TDD_10 §5.2` lists them, but no screen of the MVP sends them, so 1c does not declare them). The `HUMAN_AGENT` message tag (a human answers up to 7 days after the last customer message; never for bots or promotion; needs the "Human Agent" permission from App Review)

**TDD:** `TDD_10 §5.2`, `§5.4`, `§5.5`
**Done when:** all four channels deliver into the same unified inbox, and the widget works
on a page with a strict CSP.

## Milestone 3 — Inbox interface

### Step 3.1 — Frontend foundation
- [x] **Stabilize `tests/api/realtime.spec.ts` first, in a fix PR of its own (found in PR 1c-2, 2026-10-29):** in the whole integration run it fails one or two tests, a different pair each run, and it does so on a clean `main` too (four runs at `d186cff`); alone it sometimes passes 69 of 69. The ones seen: `hears an event of the bridge whose ids come in capitals`, `hears an event of the worker, which holds no sockets, through the bridge`, `does not take a user out of a room on the word of an event that the conversation does not bear out`. All three publish to `EVENTS_BRIDGE_CHANNEL` and wait for a socket to hear it. **Hypothesis, not proved:** (1) `EventsBridge.onModuleInit` subscribes inside the `ready` handler and nothing waits for it, so `createTestApp` can return before the api listens; (2) an event is passed on by the one instance that claims it in Redis, so an instance left open by an earlier test of "across instances" can claim it and the socket of the test never hears it. **How to close it:** reproduce in a loop (the spec alone, 20 runs) with a log of which instance claims each event; if (1), make the bridge expose a promise that settles when the subscription is acknowledged (it also fixes the order of a real boot) and have the suite await it; if (2), close every extra instance in a `finally`; if it is neither, say what it was. Do not widen waits or add retries to hide it. **Done when:** `realtime.spec.ts` passes 20 runs in a row alone and the whole integration suite passes three runs in a row. Step 3.1 relies on realtime, so it starts after this. **Closed:** it was both. (1) The bridge subscribed in the `ready` handler and nothing waited for it: `EventsBridge.subscribed` settles when Redis acknowledges, and `createTestApp` awaits it. (2) An api left running on the laptop (`dist/apps/api/main.js`) listened to the same channel and claimed events: `assertNoOtherBridgeListener` stops the suite and names the cause. Proved with 20 runs of the spec alone and three whole integration runs (1057 tests each)
- [x] Next.js App Router structure with `/s/[storeId]`, `/org`, `/account`, `/invitations/[token]`, `proxy.ts`. A user with no membership is redirected to the "no access" screen, not `/onboarding` (DEFERRED). Covered by unit tests (a real socket.io server for the connection) and by `tests/api/realtime-client.spec.ts` against the real api: a message queued while the network is down is sent once on reconnect, two sockets of a store hear each other, the presence is read. **The `e2e-tests` cases of "Done when" that drive this in a browser are in the PR E2b (below)**

### Step 3.2 — Inbox
Tasks 2 to 7 were added by the TDD_09 pass of the frontend alignment audit (owner, 2026-10-29): each names a view or a route that the interface of this step reads and the server does not give yet. Build the server side of each one first, with its test in `tests/api`, then draw it.
- [x] **Delete the test page of Step 3.1 and create the `lighthouse` job (owner, 2026-10-30; the deletion is PR B3a, the `lighthouse` job is PR B3c, owner 2026-10-30: the cases of `realtime.spec.ts` need the offline queue, which only the composer drives). Done in PR B3a:** the cases of `realtime.spec.ts` drive the inbox (offline queue, two sockets) and the test page, its harness, the `E2E_HARNESS` branch and `web:build-e2e` are gone. **Done in PR B3c:** the `lighthouse` job (pull requests that can affect the web app; optional, not a required check) runs `nx run web-e2e:lighthouse`: a Playwright config of its own (`playwright.lighthouse.config.ts`, the servers shared with the e2e one through `playwright.shared.ts`) whose spec opens Playwright's Chromium with a debugging port and gives it to the `lighthouse` module (13.6.0, a root dev dependency), in place of the `lhci autorun` that `TDD_03 §4` used to name, because the inbox is behind the login: `/login` signed out, and `/s/{store}/inbox` after a magic link read from Mailpit, with a new sign-in before each run, because the session was lost in some runs (the likely cause is the rotation of the refresh token with Lighthouse cutting the load short; not confirmed in the api logs), and a new sign-in before each run removed it. Lighthouse's own run wipes the cache, so each run is a cold load; the budgets are asserted on the median of three (`lighthouse/budgets.ts`). The budgets of `TDD_08 §4.4`: FCP, LCP, TTI, CLS as written; **Total Blocking Time < 200 ms stands in for INP**, which needs a person; the initial script transfer is held at a **ceiling per page** (login 260 KB, inbox 340 KB, owner 2026-10-30) because the app weighs 244 KB and 322 KB today against the 200 KB of the TDD: the ceilings come down in Step 7.7. The per-route 100 KB budget is not measured (a lab run cannot tell the shared chunks from the route's own). The steps that install Node, the dependencies, the migrated database and Chromium moved to the composite action `.github/actions/web-stack`, used by `e2e-tests` and `lighthouse`; the services stay in each job, since an action cannot declare them.
