Instagram and Messenger are left out of PR 1c on purpose (owner, 2026-10-28) and implement both here: Meta's `sender_action` `typing_on` / `typing_off` / `mark_seen` on `POST /<id>/messages` (Messenger `message_reads`, Instagram `messaging_seen`), declaring `TYPING_INDICATOR` and `READ_RECEIPTS` only then. The web widget (Step 2.8, PR 2a) declares neither `TYPING_INDICATOR`, `CUSTOMER_TYPING` nor `READ_RECEIPTS` for the same reason: it adds them here, with the events on its `/widget` namespace **Server side done (PR 3.2-A3, owner 2026-10-30; the interface that draws and sends them is B4):** Instagram and Messenger declare `TYPING_INDICATOR` and `READ_RECEIPTS` (`sender_action` `typing_on` / `typing_off` / `mark_seen`, the last one for the whole conversation, so the message id is not used). The typing of an agent leaves the agents' gateway to `AgentTypingRelay`, which does the first start of each 5 s slot and a stop that follows a start that went (calls for one conversation run in order, a start first asks the database whether the agent may still send, and only an active connection is told): a channel with the plugin capability gets `SEND_OUTBOUND_TYPING` on the outbound queue (one attempt, not kept and with no job id, carrying the time of the event: `TypingSender` in the worker drops a job older than 10 s or overtaken by a newer one of its conversation, and only logs a failure), and the widget visitor gets `widget.agent_typing` `{ active }` on the socket. **Meta sends no typing of the customer** (Messenger and Instagram have no such webhook; checked with the docs of both), so `CUSTOMER_TYPING` is declared by the widget alone: the visitor's socket sends `widget.typing`, once in 2 s for each visitor (all their tabs) and 600 a minute for each site, and the api emits `customer.typing` (`{ conversationId }`) to the room of the conversation (not for an archived or spam one), api only, nothing stored. **Before running more than one api or worker instance:** the thinning of typing and the two limits of the widget live in the memory of each process, so with several instances each lets a signal through; move them to Redis (`SET NX PX`) then. The MVP runs one of each. **Left for PR B5, on purpose (owner, 2026-10-30):** the widget bundle does not send or draw typing yet, and the widget declares no `READ_RECEIPTS`; both are the next task below. **The widget side is PR B6b; the inbox side is PR B7:** the composer announces a reply being typed (`useAgentTyping`, `TypingAnnouncer`: a start at most once in `TypingTiming.AGENT_START_GAP_MS`, a stop when the agent is idle, empties the box, sends or leaves; never for a note, a slash command or a closed window), and the line under the thread (`typing-line.tsx`, `useTyping`, `TypingTracker`) draws "the customer is typing" from `customer.typing` (it lapses after `TypingTiming.CUSTOMER_LAPSE_MS`, or when the customer's message arrives) and the teammates from `typing.start` / `typing.stop`, except the person on another tab. The read state toward the channel was already sent by `POST /conversations/:id/read`, which `useMarkRead` calls (`ReadReceipts.queueFor`, `widgetRead.notify`)
- [x] **WhatsApp shows "typing" to the customer (owner, 2026-11-04, real test with Meta; PR `feat/whatsapp-typing-indicator`):** `WhatsAppPlugin` declares `TYPING_INDICATOR` and implements `sendTypingIndicator` as Meta documents it (<https://developers.facebook.com/documentation/business-messaging/whatsapp/typing-indicators>): the read receipt of the newest message of the customer plus `typing_indicator: { type: 'text' }`. Meta ends it when the agent answers or after about 25 seconds and has no call to take it away, so a stop (`active: false`) calls nothing. `TypingParams.lastInbound` (provider id and ISO time) is read by the worker (`typing-inbound.ts`, inside the store, the newest live message of the customer); the plugin shows nothing without it, nor once the 24 hour window is closed. Because the indicator also marks that message as read, the worker offers no message when the store switched `sendReadReceipts` off. A refusal of Graph is logged by `providerCode` and `providerSubcode` only. Known limit, accepted: while the indicator is on, the customer sees the message as read even if the agent never opened the conversation (the same effect as the blue ticks the agent would send by opening it); a store that does not want that switches the receipts off and WhatsApp then shows no indicator. Known limits, accepted: `TypingSender.deliver` itself has no unit spec because its module imports the environment (CI has no `DATABASE_URL`); the pieces that decide (`typing-inbound.ts`, the plugin) are tested and the wiring is covered by the real test with Meta; `receivedAt` is the time the message was stored, not the one Meta sent, so a delayed webhook can cost one refused call (logged by provider code).
- [x] The link of something the customer shared on Instagram or Messenger (a post, a reel, a story mention, a link preview) is stored in `metadata.attachmentUrl` as it came (PR 1c): draw it as an untrusted link, with its host visible and `rel="noopener noreferrer"`, never fetch it from the server, and do not count on it lasting: a story mention is a signed CDN link that expires within about a day, and a permanent copy would need the storage of Step 3.4 **Done in PR B2:** `SharedLink` draws it with its host, `rel="noopener noreferrer"`, in a tab of its own; a link that is not http(s) is not drawn
- [x] The list of conversations when a quiet message arrives (owner, 2026-10-29): the preview of each row is the newest message of the thread by date, so the server already answers it; the client reads `message.queued` and changes the text of that row in place, without moving it (the order of the list is `lastMessageAt`, which a quiet message does not change) **Done (PR B2):** `message.queued` already makes the `conversations` key stale (`invalidation.ts`, covered by `invalidation.spec.ts`), and the refetched row carries the new preview in the same place, because the order is `lastMessageAt`; nothing else was needed

- [x] **End to end test of the web widget (PR B6c; owner, 2026-10-29; the widget of Step 2.8 was seen in a browser only against a stand-in server):** a Playwright case in the `e2e-tests` job of Step 3.1 that serves a page with a strict CSP (`default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; connect-src <api>`, no `style-src`) holding the snippet that `GET /channels/connections/:id/widget` returns, with the real api and worker: the launcher appears, a visitor writes and the message reaches the inbox of an agent in a second browser context, the agent replies and the visitor sees it through the socket, and the same page after the site is removed with `PUT .../widget/origins` gets no new session. The widget is not served by the Next.js app, so the job builds it (`nx build widget`) and serves `dist/apps/widget`
- [x] **Typing and read receipts in the web widget bundle (owner, 2026-10-30; PR B6b, and the end to end case above is B6c; the server side of typing is built in PR 3.2-A3):** `apps/widget` sends `widget.typing` (`WidgetClientEvents.TYPING`) while the visitor types, at most once in `WidgetLimits.CUSTOMER_TYPING_GAP_MS`, and draws "the team is typing" on `widget.agent_typing` `{ active }`, letting it lapse after a time of its own (about 25 s) if no stop arrives, a constant added where it is read; the text is an i18n key of the widget's own catalog and the style respects the strict CSP of the task above. Read receipts: the worker cannot reach a visitor's socket, so the read route of the api (where `ReadReceipts.queueFor` is called) also emits a new `widget.read` to the visitor's room when the conversation belongs to the widget, the widget draws the "seen" mark on the messages of the visitor, and the plugin declares `READ_RECEIPTS` only then (with a `markAsRead` that does nothing, as `sendTypingIndicator` does). Each piece gets its case in `tests/api/widget.spec.ts` **Done in PR B6b (#101):** `Session.typing()` sends `widget.typing` at most once in `WidgetLimits.CUSTOMER_TYPING_GAP_MS` and only on a live socket; `Session.onAgentTyping` drives the "the team is typing" line of the panel (a polite `role=status` line under the list), cleared by a stop, by a team reply, by the socket going down, or after `WidgetLimits.AGENT_TYPING_LAPSE_MS` (25 s, in core with the other widget limits). `WidgetSocketEvents.READ` (`widget.read`, `{ messageId }`) is emitted by the new `WidgetReadNotifier` (api, `WidgetDeliveryModule`) from the read route and from the bulk `mark_read`, next to `ReadReceipts.queueFor`; it names the latest text message of the visitor, honors the store's `sendReadReceipts` switch, and only a conversation of an active widget connection is looked into. The widget marks that message and every earlier one of the visitor as "Seen" (`ChatLog.markSeenUpTo`; the marks survive a re-read of the history). The plugin declares `READ_RECEIPTS` with a `markAsRead` that does nothing (the queued job of `ReadReceipts` reaches it and ends there). Cases in `tests/api/widget.spec.ts` ("read receipts") and unit cases in `apps/widget`. **Left on purpose:** a visitor who reloads the page loses the "Seen" marks until the next read; the task right below closes it. Also known: `widget.read` names the latest message of the visitor when the notifier runs, not at the instant of the read, so a message that lands in between can be marked seen early; it closes with the task below by reading `lastReadAt`
- [x] **"Seen" marks in the bootstrap of the web widget (left by PR B6b of this step, none a data risk; owner of the rest: PR B6c, the next one that touches the widget):** `widget.read` reaches only a socket that is open at the moment of the read, so a visitor who reloads, or was offline, sees no "Seen" mark on messages an agent already read. `Conversation.lastReadAt` already holds the moment of the last read: the history of the bootstrap (`WidgetHistory`) adds `readUpToMessageId` (the latest text message of the visitor with `createdAt <= lastReadAt`, `null` when the store turned `sendReadReceipts` off or nothing was read), `isBootstrap` in `apps/widget/src/api.ts` checks it, and `Session` passes it to `ChatLog.markSeenUpTo` after `loadHistory`. A case in `tests/api/widget.spec.ts` (read, then bootstrap again) and one in `apps/widget`
- [x] Reactions in the thread (owner, 2026-10-28; the data is built in Step 2.8, PR 1b): the emoji sits in the corner of the message it was left on, as chips grouped with a count (👍 2), like WhatsApp; the detail of who reacted (`authorType` tells customer, agent or the owner of the provider's app; `authorId` is the user or the customer identity, null for the owner) shows on hover or tap. No line in the thread for a reaction. `message.reaction` carries only ids, so the client invalidates the thread; if a notice such as "Ana reacted" is wanted, widen that event first (`action`, `emoji`, `authorType`, `authorId`) **Drawn read-only in PR B2** (`reaction-chips.tsx`, with who reacted when the chip is opened and the pending or failed state of an agent's reaction); the actions are PR B4 **Done in PR B4:** the picker is the six quick emoji of `QUICK_REACTIONS` plus a dialog for any other single emoji (checked with `isWholeEmoji`, the rule of the api), taking back one's own reaction from the same menu or by choosing it again; offered only when the channel declares `REACTIONS`, the viewer holds `MESSAGES_SEND` and the message has a copy in the channel (`lib/inbox/message-actions.ts`, the same rules as the api, covered in its spec)
- [x] The actions of an agent on a message (owner, 2026-10-28; the server side is built in Step 2.8): react with an emoji picker and take it back (`PUT /messages/:id/reaction`), showing the reaction as waiting or failed with the reason until the channel answers; "send again" on a reply that failed (`message.failed`; the route `POST /messages/:id/resend` is built, PR 3.2-A2: it puts the same message back on its way as `queued`, so the bubble and its `clientMessageId` stay, and a text needs the window open again while a template does not; `Message.resentAt` is when the sweep starts counting its age); and edit and delete (built in PR 1d of Step 2.8). Every action appears only if the channel of the conversation declares its capability (from the connection view) and the user holds the permission. **No official plugin declares `EDIT_MESSAGE` or `DELETE_MESSAGE`** (Meta offers no such API for WhatsApp, Messenger or Instagram, Step 2.8), so these two actions stay hidden for every official channel until a plugin that can is installed; the interface is still built and tested with the contract plugin. For a note the actions need no capability. **Draw what the thread receives from PR 1d:** the tombstone of a message the customer deleted ("the customer deleted this message") with the kept text behind `MESSAGES_VIEW_DELETED` (`GET /messages/:id/deleted-content`, every read audited), "edited" with `pendingEdit` and `editStatus`, `deleteStatus` queued or failed with its reason, and the `version` that an edit of a note sends back (a stale one answers 409 with the current text). Decide here whether an edit of a note recomputes its `mentions` (Step 2.8, follow-up 5) **Drawn in PR B2, read-only:** the tombstone (without the kept text), "edited", `pendingEdit` and `editStatus`, `deleteStatus` queued or failed with its reason, and the failed ticks; the actions, the capability checks and `deleted-content` are PR B4 **Done in PR B4:** a bar beside the bubble (hover, keyboard focus, always on a touch screen) with react and a menu of resend, edit, edit history, view deleted text and delete (`message-actions-bar.tsx`, dialogs in `message-action-dialogs.tsx`); the capability comes from the channel directory (`ChannelCapability` moved to `libs/core/domain` so the browser can ask for it by name, and the SDK re-exports it), delete asks first, the kept text is read when its dialog opens and never cached (every read is audited), and a stale save of a note shows the current text and its version instead of overwriting. **Decision, edit of a note and `mentions`:** `PATCH /messages/:id` takes no mentions, so an edit neither notifies nor changes who the note names; the highlight marks only the names still written in the text. The cases with a channel that declares the capabilities (react, edit and delete of a reply, resend) are for PR B5 with the fixtures of the composer cases below, because the e2e fixtures have no such channel; the note case runs in `thread.spec.ts`
- [x] Internal notes in the thread (owner, 2026-10-28; the data is built in Step 2.3, and the edit with its revisions in Step 2.8, PR 1d): a note is not on the left or the right like the messages of the customer and of the agents; it sits on the right, on the side of the team like an agent's message, with the avatar and name of its author and the time, in a soft amber background from theme tokens with a dashed amber border, and the label "internal note" (owner, 2026-10-29, which replaces the centered note without label of 2026-10-28: the color is never the only signal, and the author and the order of the conversation read better on the side of the team). A note written by a plugin shows its nickname and the "automatic" mark (below). It shows "edited by" when a teammate changed it with a link to the revisions, and the `@` mentions highlighted; edit and delete appear on hover, according to the permissions; another agent's edit arrives in real time and a stale save shows the current text. In the composer, the note tab tints the field amber. This replaces "amber left border, Internal label" of `TDD_09 §4`. **The "automatic" mark** (drawn in Step 5.6, when a plugin can write a message) shows on every message whose author is a plugin of integration (`AuthorType.SYSTEM` with `authorPluginId`), next to its nickname, so that a plugin cannot pass as a person; it does not show on an agent's message (also one a plugin sent for them, which shows "sent through <plugin>" on hover), on the lines of the system ("resolved"), or on a message the owner typed in the provider's own app (`external`, a channel event, not a plugin call) **Drawn in PR B2** (amber tokens `--note*` in `globals.css`, dashed border, label, author, time); the edit, the revisions link and the mentions highlight are PR B4, the amber composer tab is PR B3, the "automatic" mark is Step 5.6 **Drawn in PR B4:** the edit and delete from the menu, "edited by <name>" and the edit history dialog (the link to the revisions is the menu entry), and the mentions highlighted **Left: the "automatic" mark,** owned by Step 5.6 (task "Messages from a plugin", below), because nothing can write as a plugin before it.

Two of the twelve `BulkActions` are not built here: `EXPORT_CSV` goes with the exports center of Step 3.4 together with `GET /conversations/export` (it needs the `Export` queue, key and download that Step 3.4 builds, and the route is declared before `GET /conversations/:id` there), and `SEND_TEMPLATE` is Phase 2 (a template has variables and a window for each conversation, and no flow of the MVP sends one in bulk; removed from the constants, decided in PR 3.2-A2). The shortcuts `/` (search) and `Mod+K` (palette) only navigate here and gain search with Step 5.1; `g i`, `g c` and the rest work as soon as their page exists **Bulk actions done in PR B5a (decision of PR B5a, which split B5 in two because the shortcuts act on the selection that the bulk actions build and the two together are more than one review can hold; the shortcuts, the phone focus and the row action `s` are PR B5b):** `bulkConversations` in `libs/api-client`, the ticked rows (`lib/inbox/selection.ts`, a checkbox over the avatar that shows on hover, focus, once something is ticked and on touch screens), the bar of `TDD_09 §10` (`bulk-bar.tsx`: Assign, Status, Tag and More, each shown only with its permission; Export CSV and Send template are not built, see above), the pick dialog for an agent, the team or a tag, and the confirmation (`BulkLimits.CONFIRM_FROM` = 10 or more conversations, or "all matching", gets a dialog with what will change; archiving asks for the typed phrase). The result is one toast with the number changed and the reason of each kind of failure; **Undo** puts every conversation back to what it was (`lib/inbox/bulk.ts`: one step per previous state, only for rows ticked by id, never for a filter, and not for archiving), and a conversation whose old state cannot be set again (snoozed, archived) is left as it is. **Known limit:** the Undo of the toast restores the state read when the action ran and does not check what happened since, so an Undo left on screen can overwrite a later change by another agent or a reply of the customer; accepted for the MVP (a faithful Undo would need the server to refuse a change made over a newer one), to be reconsidered with the visual pass of Step 7.7. "Select all matching this filter" is offered only when a filter is in the address (the server refuses a filter that names nothing) and sends the filter, not the ids; more than `BulkLimits.MAX_ITEMS` ticked by id is refused in the bar. Cases: `bulk.spec.ts`, `selection.spec.ts`, `conversations-api.spec.ts`, and the e2e case that ticks two rows, resolves them and undoes it. **Shortcuts, help sheet, phone focus and row snooze done in PR B5b:** one listener on the document (`components/shortcuts/shortcuts-provider.tsx`, `lib/shortcuts/chords.ts`) reads the key presses, the two-key sequences (`g i`, `* a`, one second between the keys) and the rule of `TDD_09 §9` (nothing works in a text field except Escape and Cmd/Ctrl+Enter, and nothing while a dialog or a menu is open); components register their bindings with `useShortcut`, and when several want one key the highest `ShortcutPriority` that takes it wins (the ticked rows, then the row that has the focus, then the open conversation), so `a`, `e` and `s` act on what is ticked, else on the focused row, else on the open conversation. `j`/`k` (and the arrows, only while the list has the focus) move the focus of the list, `x` ticks, `* a` ticks the loaded rows (up to `BulkLimits.MAX_ITEMS`), `Enter` is the link of the focused row, `?` opens the sheet (`shortcut-help.tsx`, which lists `AvailableShortcuts` only), `g i` goes to the inbox, `Esc` drops the selection and then closes the conversation, `r`, `Shift+N` and Cmd/Ctrl+Enter belong to the composer, and `Shift+A`, `t`, `p`, `e`, `s` and `a` to the open conversation (`conversation-shortcuts.tsx`: the same permissions and states as the header buttons; `p`, `a`, `t` and `Shift+A` go through the bulk runner, so they carry its toast and Undo; `e` keeps the resolve flow of the store). A row has a snooze button (hover, focus, touch) and the key `s`. On a phone the focus goes to the `h1` of the conversation when it opens, and back to its row when the agent leaves it (the back button, Escape; on a desktop too). A row resolved with `e` without opening it is resolved as the bulk bar resolves, with no reason and no closing message. Cases: `chords.spec.ts`, `labels.spec.ts`, `help-labels.spec.ts`, `list-navigation.spec.ts` and `apps/web-e2e/specs/inbox.spec.ts` (the cases named "shortcuts:", in the session of that file: the magic link is limited to `MagicLinkThrottle` per address, and a new spec that signs in as `admin@test.com` went over it). **Not built here, each with the step that owns it (the constants stay in `Shortcuts`, outside `AvailableShortcuts`, until then):** `/` and Cmd/Ctrl+K have nothing to open yet (Step 5.1 registers them with `useShortcut`); `g o` (Step 4.4) and `g c` (Step 3.3) wait for their pages; `Shift+O` (Step 4.4) and `Shift+C` (Step 3.3) wait for the order dialog and the profile drawer. When one of them is built, add its name to `AvailableShortcuts` and its label to both message files (`help-labels.spec.ts` fails otherwise). **Known limits:** `a`, `e` and `s` act on the row that has the focus before the open conversation, so after `j` moves the focus to another row they reach that row while `r` still writes to the conversation on screen (accepted: the focus is the cue, and the toast of an action on one conversation names the customer, "Resolved: Ana", with its Undo; a clearer ring for the focused row waits for the visual pass of Step 7.7); the row snooze button sits over the bottom right of the row and can cover a tag chip on a narrow list, to be looked at in the visual pass of Step 7.7
- [x] Follow-ups of the review of PR B5b, none a data risk (owner: PR B6 of this step, the next one that touches the inbox). **Done in PR B6a: (1)** a `e` on a focused row runs the resolve flow of the store for that conversation (`row-resolve.tsx` mounts `useResolveFlow` for it), **(2)** a key taken while an action is going (`BulkRunner.claim`) or by a selection over the limit says why in a toast. **Moved: (3)** the DOM test of the provider waits for a DOM test environment in the web project, which `apps/web/jest.config.cts` lacks: it is a task of Step 7.7 ("DOM test environment"), below. Original text: (1) `e` on a focused row resolves as the bulk bar does, with no reason and no closing message, while `e` on the open conversation runs the resolve flow of the store, so one key has two effects by focus: make the row key open the flow of that conversation (the flow is per conversation, `useResolveFlow`) or ask the same questions; (2) a key pressed while an action is in flight, or on a selection over `BulkLimits.MAX_ITEMS`, is taken and does nothing, with no sign other than the bar: show a short toast saying why; (3) the provider's listener (overlay and text-field rules, `repeat`) has no unit test beyond `dispatch.spec.ts` and `chords.spec.ts`: cover it with a component test when the web project gets a DOM test environment, today only the "shortcuts:" cases of `inbox.spec.ts` (e2e) reach it
- [x] **Closing message that waits for the Undo (owner, 2026-10-30; PR B3b-2, right after B3b):** B3b sends the closing message first and resolves after, so an Undo reopens the conversation but cannot take back a message the customer already has. Move it to the server so that resolving is instant and durable (closing the tab loses nothing) and the closing message keeps the ten seconds of the Undo. `PATCH /conversations/:id/status` takes an optional `closingSnippetId` (only with `RESOLVED`); the api resolves, then stores the closing message as `queued` and hands it to the `outbound` queue with a delay of `ResolveUndo.DURATION_MS`, without reopening (the status is already set and the later send does not touch it). `OutboundSender` sends it only if the conversation is still `RESOLVED` by the same resolution (`resolvedAt` not after the message); otherwise it deletes the row (never sent, so it leaves no trace; a `failed` bubble after an Undo would be wrong, and a message left `queued` would be requeued by the sweep). The web stops sending the closing message and only asks to resolve. Cases: `tests/api` (resolve with the closing message, Undo before the send, a reply of the customer before it, resolving twice), a worker integration case, and the e2e case of PR B3b changed to wait for the message **Done in PR B3b-2:** `ConversationClosingService` (`libs/core/messaging`), the delay in `OutboundQueue.enqueue`, the check and delete in `OutboundSender`; the web only sends `closingSnippetId`
- [x] **Done in PR B6d:** `apps/web-e2e/specs/templates.spec.ts` (five cases: the banner, the closed state, the template sent end to end, the refusal with no connection, the failed send retried once), the fake Graph of `support/fake-graph.ts` and the new `META_GRAPH_ORIGIN` variable (refused by `env-schema.ts` in `staging` and `production`, because the host would receive the access token of every connection; only Facebook's host is replaced, never Instagram's). Two findings while building it: the WhatsApp connection of the fixtures has no `phoneNumberId` in its credentials, so no real call could use it and the spec owns a connection of its own (`newWhatsAppConversationWrittenAgo`); and Playwright's offline mode does not close a socket that is up, so the refusal case cuts the socket with `page.routeWebSocket`. A laptop that reuses a running api or worker must have started it with `META_GRAPH_ORIGIN=http://127.0.0.1:8099` or the cases fail. Original task: cases of the composer that PR B3a could not test, for PR B6d (moved from B5 by PR B5a, then from B6c by PR B6c, which carried the widget end to end case and the "Seen" marks and left this piece for a PR of its own; the way in, found while reading the code: the fixtures already hold a WhatsApp connection in Main Store, and `WhatsAppPlugin` already declares `MESSAGING_WINDOW` and `TEMPLATES`, so no test plugin is needed in the production bundle; what is missing is a Graph API that the e2e api can reach, a fake server in the spec behind a new env variable for the base url of the Graph client (`libs/channels/meta/src/meta-graph.client.ts` hardcodes `https://graph.facebook.com`), in `env-schema.ts` and `.env.example`, and fixture conversations on that connection with the last customer message at the wanted age) ( they need a test channel plugin in the e2e api, which is a piece of its own, and the order of B4 already puts the end to end cases in B6) (the e2e fixtures have no channel with a messaging window or templates): the amber banner under two hours with the time left, the closed window with the "Choose a template" button, and the template dialog end to end (choose, fill the values, preview, send, the offline refusal, a retry after a failure that sends once). Uses the WhatsApp channel of the fixtures against that fake Graph, not a test plugin. Also check by hand in a real browser, in the visual pass of Step 7.7, that the banner and the closed state read well in light and dark
- [x] **Use `waitForAuditEntries` in the specs that keep their own copy (debt of PR #96; done in PR B5a: `customers`, `routing`, `settings` and `conversations-bulk` use the helper and the local loops are gone):** `tests/helpers/audit.ts` is the one way to wait for a fire-and-forget audit row; `tests/api/customers.spec.ts`, `routing.spec.ts` and `settings.spec.ts` still carry a local `auditEntries` polling loop. Replace each with the helper (mechanical, no behavior change) in the next PR of this step that touches one of those specs, and delete the local loops. Rides with B4 or B5, not a PR of its own. The same PR may also adopt it in `conversations-bulk.spec.ts`, whose `find` reads the table without waiting

Find what the inbox bundle imports statically (a bundle analysis of the inbox route), cut it back under the ceiling, and decide whether `lighthouse` joins the ruleset. Do not raise the ceiling. First task of PR D **Done in PR D1:** the open conversation (header, thread, typing line, composer, with a loading line) and the dialogs of the inbox (template, snooze, bulk pick and confirm, message actions, shortcut help) are loaded with `next/dynamic` the first time they are drawn (`components/inbox/lazy-dialogs.ts`, `lib/use-mounted-once.ts`); the ceiling is unchanged and the job passes. **Kept static on purpose (review of PR D1):** the bulk bar and the row resolve flow (they register shortcuts and a key pressed while the chunk loads would fall through) and the confirmation dialog of `DestructiveActionProvider` (a failed chunk would leave its `openRef` set and every later confirmation dead). Moving the socket out of the first load does not help, because the job counts what loads until the page is quiet. **Decision:** `lighthouse` stays advisory (not in the `main` ruleset): it measures a lab run whose result moves by a few KB between runs, and a required check that flakes blocks every PR. The remaining weight is in the libraries (see the task of Step 7.7)

### Step 3.4 — Media
- [x] **PR E is four PRs (decided 2026-11-01, rule 12: one PR cannot carry the server work, the thread, the gallery with the center and the ad media and be reviewed):** **E1** server surfaces (files in `MessageView`, `MEDIA_*` events, `FILE_UNAVAILABLE`, `missing.txt`, gallery counts, CORS check); **E2** the thread, in two PRs (decided 2026-11-01, rule 12): **E2a** the web side (composer upload with progress, attachments, placeholder, lightbox) and **E2b** the "file unavailable" marker and the caption debts, which need the server and the plugins; **E3**, in three PRs (decided 2026-11-01, rule 12: the gallery, the exports center and the bulk export are three screens with three kinds of test): **E3a** the gallery (a dialog of the customer drawer, not a page: `TDD_09 §7` draws it as a dialog with a close button), its lightbox with arrow keys, "Download all" and the debt of the stale lost-file marker; **E3b** the exports center (the `EXPORT_READY` notification and realtime refresh stay in Step 3.5, as the exports task says) and the link from the toast of "Download all"; **E3c** the bulk `EXPORT_CSV` with `conversationIds` and the CSV buttons; **E4** the media of an ad (fetch with a pinned address, origin panel). Tasks below that say "PR E" belong to the one of these that the line names
- [x] Pre-signed upload with progress, purposes and key prefixes; `StorageHealthIndicator` (`HeadBucket`) joins `/health/ready` with the S3 client (`TDD_04 §4`) **Done in PR A:** `libs/core/storage` (`@platform/core/storage-module`: `StorageService` over the S3 client, `StorageModule`), `StoragePlatformModule` (global, from the env, in `HttpPlatformModule` so the api and the worker both have it), `StorageHealthIndicator` in `/health/ready`, `POST /media/upload-url` and `POST /media/confirm-upload` (`MEDIA_UPLOAD`; the key must be exactly the one signed for that media id and name, a key of another store is `FILE_KEY_OUT_OF_SCOPE`, a repeated confirmation answers with the same media and queues the scan again under the job id `scan-file-<media id>` if the first attempt died before queueing it; the client uploads to a staging key `stores/<id>/uploads/...` and the confirmation copies the file to `StorageKeys.message` and deletes the staging one, because the signed URL outlives the confirmation and the uploader could otherwise overwrite a file after it was scanned), `sanitizeFilename`, and SeaweedFS as a service of every CI job that starts the api (integration, security, security-scan, e2e, lighthouse); the container answers `/healthz` before it creates the bucket, so the integration and security jobs run `scripts/wait-for-bucket.ts` before they need it (security-scan retries the 503 of readiness with `curl --retry`, e2e and lighthouse wait on `/health/live`). The scan job is queued but its consumer is PR B, so a confirmed media stays `pending` until then; the isolation suite has its own cases for the upload routes and `Media` leaves the exemptions when `GET /media/:id` joins the generic list
- [x] Scan-then-process worker chain (ClamAV, sharp variants as keys) **Done in PR B1:** `apps/worker/src/media` (`ClamavClient`: INSTREAM over TCP, a file without a verdict is never taken for a clean one; `MediaPipeline.scan`: infected means the object is deleted first and the row marked `infected`, clean means `clean` and, for an image, `PROCESS_IMAGE` under the job id `process-image-<media id>`; `MediaPipeline.processImage`: three WebP variants of `ImageVariantSpecs`, orientation applied and metadata dropped, `limitInputPixels` against decompression bombs, an image the decoder refuses fails for good and is served as a download without variants; a failure of the bucket is retried; a repeated scan job of a clean image queues the variants again). **Known limits (rule 12):** the variants of an animated GIF are its first frame (the original stays a download; animated WebP is a task for Step 7.x polish); the image is decoded once per variant, so the processor runs 2 jobs at a time to bound memory (a shared decode is a task if the worker memory budget of Step 7.3 needs it) and `HeavyProcessingProcessor` (one processor for the queue, told apart by job name; the fetch of inbound media joins it in PR B2). The worker integration suite runs the chain against a real ClamAV (the EICAR string), SeaweedFS and Postgres, so the `integration-tests` job has a `clamav` service (it waits for its signature database). **The worker image carries `sharp`:** a native binary cannot be bundled, so `tools/rspack/nest-bundle.cjs` takes `nativePackages`, and the Dockerfile installs `sharp` on Alpine in a stage of its own (`native-worker`; the api gets an empty one)
- [x] Sanitize the file name of an inbound media where the file is written and where `Content-Disposition` is built (**Content-Disposition done in PR B1:** `attachmentDisposition` quotes and RFC 5987-encodes the name; **the inbound file done in PR B2:** `InboundMediaFetcher` runs `sanitizeFilename` once, for the key and the row) (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md`, "WhatsApp plugin build details", item 12): the WhatsApp plugin already cuts it to a last path segment, but the name is the customer's and this step owns what is done with it
- [x] **(PR B2)** The `fetch-media` processor (moved from Step 2.7; it also fetches the `url` of an ad's media (`ConversationOrigin.mediaUrl`, `thumbnailUrl`) that Instagram, Messenger and WhatsApp send, so before any server-side fetch of a link that came from outside it must refuse private, loopback and link-local addresses, `httpUrl` does not): `InboundMessageService` already queues `fetch-media-<message id>-<index>` (one job per media of the message); it calls `plugin.fetchMedia`, stores and scans the file, and must skip a message that already has its media **Done in PR B2:** `InboundMediaFetcher` (`apps/worker/src/media`) on `HeavyProcessingProcessor` (job `FETCH_INBOUND_MEDIA`, which now carries `index`). The media id is a hash of the message and the place (`inboundMediaId`), so a retry finds its row (and only queues the scan again) and never makes a second file; the type is the one the file server declared (the declared one only when it says `application/octet-stream`) and must be an allowed type, the size is held to the limit of its type while it is read, and the file enters `pending` and goes to the scan like any upload, with `customerId` set for the gallery. A reference that is a link goes through `assertPublicHttpsUrl` first (https, default port, no credentials, every address its host resolves to public: `isPublicAddress`). A file the core refuses (type, size, empty, unsafe link) is dropped with a warning, a failure the plugin calls retryable is retried and then dropped. **Known limits (rule 12):** the plugins already follow only Meta hosts and no redirects, so the address check before the call is a second layer and cannot see a DNS answer that changes between the check and the call; the fetch of an ad's media, the marker for a file that was dropped and the pinned address are tasks of PR E (two lines below); the file is held in memory while it is stored, a task of Step 7.3
- [x] Signed download with variants **Done in PR B1:** `GET /media/:id` (`MEDIA_VIEW`, store from the tenant context): `FILE_NOT_READY` (409) until clean, `FILE_INFECTED` (403) for an infected file, the signed URL of the original and of each variant (a `variants` value that is not a key is no variant, it does not fail the answer). `Media` joins the generic list of `tenant-isolation.spec.ts` and leaves `scripts/isolation-exemptions.ts`; a resource with no change route has no `modify` (`IsolationResource`). **Done in PR E1:** `MEDIA_PROCESSED` (the scan found it clean, and again when its variants exist) and `MEDIA_INFECTED` are published by `MediaPipeline` for a file that belongs to a message, bridged (`BridgedEvents`, `bridged-payloads.ts`: ids, visibility and `internal`, no text) and sent by `RealtimeEventsListener` to who may read the message (`RealtimeEvents.MEDIA_PROCESSED` and `MEDIA_INFECTED`; the web refreshes the thread scope). A file with no message yet (still in the composer) announces nothing: the composer asks `GET /media/:id` itself (PR E2). **Not done:** the `MEDIA_INFECTED` notification of `TDD_07 §6.5` waits for the notification service (task added to Step 3.5); the worker logs a warning with the media and store ids when a file is infected
- [x] Files from a web widget visitor (owner, 2026-10-29; the widget of Step 2.8 is text only): `POST /widget/upload-url` (`ApiRoutes.widget.upload`, mapped to Step 2.8 in `scripts/frontend-coverage-map.ts` and not built), `mediaId` in `POST /widget/messages`, `ChannelCapability.IMAGE` and `DOCUMENT` declared with a `sendMedia` that works, and the attachment drawn by the bundle. The visitor is anonymous, so the upload is limited by the visitor token, the size and type of `TDD_07 §6`, and a quota per visitor and per site **Done in PR F1 (server, visitor to store):** `POST /widget/upload-url` (`ApiRoutes.widget.upload`, the visitor token authenticates it, `WidgetThrottle.UPLOAD` per address, `WidgetLimits.UPLOAD_URLS_PER_VISITOR_PER_HOUR` per visitor and `UPLOAD_URLS_PER_SITE_PER_HOUR` per site, type and size as any upload; the answer is `{ uploadUrl, uploadId }`; the file goes to the staging key `uploads/<uploadId>/<name>` and the api records in Redis, for `WidgetLimits.UPLOAD_TTL_SECONDS`, the visitor, name, type and size it signed). **There is no confirm call and no `mediaId`** (decision of F1, rule 12; the plan said `mediaId`): a `Media` row needs a message and a customer, and a confirmation without a message would leave rows nobody owns. `POST /widget/messages` takes `uploadIds` (at most `UPLOADS_PER_MESSAGE`; text, files or both); `WidgetUploads.attachmentsOf` checks each one against the record (same visitor, `head` size and type equal to what was signed, claimed by one message only; every failure is `FILE_UPLOAD_FAILED`) and hands the plugin `attachments` built from the record, never from the request (the body schema drops unknown keys). The event carries `media` with the upload id as `ref`; the worker's `InboundMediaFetcher` runs unchanged and `WebWidgetPlugin.fetchMedia` reads the object under `uploads/<uploadId>/` of the store of the connection, so type and size are checked again on the bytes, the file is copied to the messages area, scanned and tied to the customer like any file. The staging object is not deleted by the fetch; the orphan cleanup removes it after `UNCONFIRMED_UPLOAD_TTL_HOURS`. The preflight of `upload` is answered like the other two routes. The capabilities `IMAGE` and `DOCUMENT` are **not** declared yet: they promise that an agent can send a file to the visitor, which F2 builds. **Done in PR F2 (server, store to visitor):** `WebWidgetPlugin` declares `IMAGE` and `DOCUMENT` and its `sendMedia` answers `SENT` with the idempotency key as the id, like `sendMessage`: the file is already clean when `POST /messages` queues it (`requireFiles`), so there is nothing to upload to anyone; `WidgetDeliveryListener` now passes on a reply of any visible kind (text, image, video, audio, document) with its files. `WidgetMessageView` gains `attachments` (`{ id, filename, contentType, size }`, never a link) and `content` is the caption, **an empty string when the message is only files** (rule 12: not `null`, so the bundles already on the stores' sites do not crash; they draw an empty bubble until they are updated). A message with neither text nor a clean file (still scanned, infected) is not shown, in the history or live. The three places that decide what a visitor may read (the history, the live delivery and the download) share one rule, `visitorMessagesWhere` (their own messages, and the store's that the channel took; no note, system line, deleted message or reply still waiting or failed) and `WIDGET_FILES_SELECT` (a file of purpose `message` that is `clean`; the picture of an ad never). **`POST /widget/download-url`** (`ApiRoutes.widget.download`, `WidgetThrottle.DOWNLOAD`, preflight answered like the others): body `visitorToken` and `mediaId`; the token names the site and the visitor, the file must hang from a message `visitorMessagesWhere` lets that visitor read, of the store of the site, and anything else (another visitor's, another store's, a note, a deleted or unsent message, an ad, a missing id) is the same 404. The file must be `clean` (`WIDGET_FILES_SELECT`, the rule of the history), so a pending or infected file is the same 404 and the visitor learns nothing of the scan; the link is signed by the unchanged `MediaService.urlsFor` (`MediaModule` now exports it) and the original is always an attachment download; the answer is `{ url, filename, contentType, previewUrl }`, where `previewUrl` is the medium WebP variant for an image and `null` otherwise. The readers of "Seen" (`readUpTo`) count a message of files. The history query asks for something to show (a text or a clean file) so that files still being scanned do not use the 50 slots of the window, and `sendMedia` refuses video and audio. Cases: `the files of a conversation` and one socket case in `tests/api/widget.spec.ts`, the plugin spec. **Left (PR F3):** the bundle: the paperclip, the upload with progress, the attachment drawn (it asks `download-url` when the visitor opens it), the field error of the phone below, inside the 40 KB gzip budget. **Known limits of F2 (rule 12):** (1) only a file the scan found clean is listed, so a file the visitor just sent appears in the history after its scan, not at once (the bundle shows it from its own state meanwhile); (2) an agent can send images and documents to a visitor, not video or audio (the visitor can send them; declaring them is a task of Step 7.7 with the player); (3) the download throttle (60 a minute) is per address like the other public routes, with no per-visitor counter, and the bundle asks one link per file it opens or previews, so F3 must ask on demand (not one call per history message) and a preview link in the history answer is the fix if that is not enough (Step 7.3, with the other abuse limits of public routes); the media row is read twice per link (the ownership check and `urlsFor`), accepted; (4) a store site that still serves a bundle from before F3 draws a file-only message as an empty bubble. **Known limits of F1 (rule 12, from its review):** (1) the per-visitor cap keys on a `visitorId` the client chooses at the bootstrap, so a script can mint visitors and drain the per-site cap (`UPLOAD_URLS_PER_SITE_PER_HOUR`) for real visitors; the address throttle (`WidgetThrottle`) bounds the rate, and a per-address hourly counter is a task of Step 7.3 with the other abuse limits of public routes; (2) a message must omit `content` when it has only files (`content: ''` is refused as an empty text); the bundle of F3 omits it. (3) the record of an upload and its claim last `UPLOAD_TTL_SECONDS` (1 hour): a resend of an already queued message after that is refused instead of answered again; (4) a message takes its type from its first file, so a PDF followed by an image is a `DOCUMENT` with an image among its media (the inbox draws each file by its own type; the gallery filters by the file); (5) the api checks size and type of the staged object but does not pin its etag as `confirmUpload` does, so bytes swapped through the still-valid signed URL before the fetch are possible; the worker checks type and size again on the bytes it reads and scans the copy, so nothing unscanned is ever served. A file of the visitor shows in the inbox as soon as the worker has it; until the scan ends it is `pending`, like any file **Done in PR F3 (the bundle, 27.2 KB gzip of 40):** a paperclip beside the text field picks files (`accept` is the allowed types; `refuseFile` applies the rule of the api, `refuseUpload`, before any byte leaves, and at most `UPLOADS_PER_MESSAGE` stay staged, each removable), `Session.send(content, files)` writes the message at once and puts the files up one after the other (`POST /widget/upload-url`, then an XHR `PUT` for the progress, shown as a bar that moves in place), then names them in `uploadIds` of `POST /widget/messages`, omitting `content` when there is only files. A retry puts up only what is missing and sends under the same message id; an upload the api no longer vouches for (`FILE_UPLOAD_FAILED`) is put up again. A file is drawn as a name and a size; its link is asked from `POST /widget/download-url` only when the visitor opens it (one call per click, never per history message, so the 60/min throttle is not met) and followed as a download, never shown on the page. The rules `refuseUpload`, `maxBytesFor` and `UploadRefusals` moved to `upload-rules.ts` (re-exported by the same barrel) because they sat beside the zod schemas and importing them put the validation library in the bundle (51 KB gzip). Cases: unit specs of the api client, log, session, panel and `upload.ts`, and two cases of `widget.spec.ts` (e2e, the strict-policy site now lists the bucket host in `connect-src`: a store that sets one must do the same, task in Step 3.6). **Known limits (rule 12, from its review):** (1) a file the visitor just sent is drawn without a link until the history is read again (the answer of `POST /widget/messages` carries no media ids), so it cannot be opened for up to the renewal of the token; (2) the link is followed with a click after an await, which Safari may block (the same debt as the gallery tile, Step 3.5); (3) the `File` objects of a message that never succeeds stay in memory until the page closes. Task in Step 3.5: have the messages answer return the ids of the media, which fixes (1). The e2e job has no ClamAV, so the case checks the upload and the send, not the file reaching the inbox; the scan and the inbox side are covered by the integration suites
- [x] Media gallery with lightbox, bulk ZIP export, and the route it reads, `GET /customers/:id/media` (`ApiRoutes.customers.media`, by `Media.customerId`, which no earlier step builds): filters by type with their counts, a search by file name, and "Download all" that creates the `MEDIA_ZIP` export and needs `MEDIA_DOWNLOAD_ALL` (`TDD_09 §7`) **Server done in PR D2:** `GET /customers/:id/media` (`CUSTOMERS_VIEW` + `MEDIA_VIEW`; `type` = `all | image | video | audio | document`, `q` = a part of the file name with `%` and `_` as text, a cursor page; the answer carries `counts` for every tab under the search, whatever tab is open, and for each file its id, tab, name, type, size, date and the channel of its conversation, never a link or a key: the link is asked from `GET /media/:id`). Only `clean` files are offered (a file not scanned yet appears when its scan ends, an infected one never), and only those of conversations the viewer could open in the inbox (`visibleTo`; nothing archived or spam without `CONVERSATIONS_ARCHIVE`; no `CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` means no file; a file with no message only for who sees it all). The ZIP freezes the requester and their view permissions in the row (`viewer`), as the conversations CSV does, so it holds what that member could see; the assignment of each conversation is read when the worker runs; a customer of another store or absorbed by a merge is a 404 (a merge moves `Media.customerId` to the survivor). `POST /media/download-all` (`CUSTOMERS_VIEW` + `MEDIA_VIEW` + `MEDIA_DOWNLOAD_ALL`, 202 with the export; body `customerId` and the optional `type` and `q` of the gallery) refuses an empty selection (`EXPORT_EMPTY`) and one over `GalleryLimits.ZIP_MAX_FILES` (500) or `ZIP_MAX_BYTES` (1 GiB) (`EXPORT_TOO_LARGE`) before it creates the row; the worker holds the same ceilings, because files can arrive between the request and the ZIP, and fails the row for good with `EXPORT_TOO_LARGE`. The ZIP is made by `ZipExportWriter` (`yazl`): the files are read from the bucket one at a time and the ZIP is uploaded as it is written, so memory does not grow with the files; the names inside are cleaned again and repeated ones get `(2)`, `(3)` (`ZipEntryNames`); only text and PDF are deflated. It holds the gallery **as it is when the worker runs**, not when the member asked (a merge in between moves the files to the survivor and the ZIP of the absorbed customer fails with `EXPORT_EMPTY`). The job is `GENERATE_EXPORT` like every export: the TDD's `GENERATE_MEDIA_ZIP` was never needed and its constant is gone (the row says the kind; `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` wins). A media ZIP is listed and downloaded only by who still holds all three permissions. ****Interface done in PR E3a:** `MediaGallery` (`apps/web/components/customers/media-gallery.tsx`), a dialog opened from a button of the customer drawer for who holds `CUSTOMERS_VIEW` + `MEDIA_VIEW`, loaded on demand (the inbox budget has no room for it): tabs with the counts of the first page, a search by file name (debounced), a grid with download and, for images and videos, the lightbox with arrow keys (`MediaLightbox` takes `onPrevious` and `onNext`), "Load more", and "Download all" for who holds `MEDIA_DOWNLOAD_ALL`, which toasts that the ZIP is being prepared. **Done in PR E3b:** the toast of "Download all" has a "View exports" action that goes to the exports center. **Not done:** each image tile asks its own signed link (`GET /media/:id`), so a page of images makes one call per tile, as the thread does (a thumbnail link in the list answer would avoid it; owner: Step 3.5); the download of a tile opens the signed link in a new tab after an await, which Safari may block (the fix needs the link to be a same-tab attachment download; owner: Step 3.5)
- [x] `mediaIds` on `POST /messages` (the upload comes first, `TDD_09 §4`): the media is checked against the store and the conversation, and linked to the message in the same transaction **Done in PR C:** the body for `IMAGE`, `VIDEO`, `AUDIO` and `DOCUMENT` takes `mediaIds` (exactly one, `MediaMessageLimits.MAX_FILES`) and an optional `content`; `MessagesService.requireFiles` runs under the conversation lock and after the replay check: the file must be of this store, uploaded by the sender (`uploadedBy`; another member's upload, another store's and a missing one are the same 404), `purpose` message, not yet attached (409), `clean` (`FILE_NOT_READY` 409, `FILE_INFECTED` 403) and of the type the message says (`mediaMessageTypeOf`, else 400), and the channel must declare the capability of the type (`CHANNEL_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED` 409, asked of the plugin registry by the route). `linkFiles` sets `Media.messageId` and `customerId` (the gallery) in the same transaction with `messageId: null` in the condition, so two conversations racing for one file give it to the first. A repeated key with other files is 409. Permission: `MESSAGES_SEND`
- [x] A media message is one message with its text under it (owner, 2026-10-28): `POST /messages` with an image, video, audio or document `type`, `mediaIds` and `content` as the caption, in the same `idempotencyKey` and message, never a media message followed by a text one. The sender hands the caption to `sendMedia` (`SendMediaParams.caption`, which the WhatsApp plugin already sends within its limits: 1024 characters, none on audio); a channel that cannot carry a caption on a type is refused for good before the send, as WhatsApp does for audio. Inbound already works that way (WhatsApp stores the caption of the media as the `content` of the same message). `replyToMessageId` applies to a media message as it does to text. **Albums (decided 2026-10-28):** inbound, a channel that brings several attachments in one message (Instagram, Messenger) becomes one message with N media (`Media.messageId` is not unique, so the model needs no change), and WhatsApp keeps one message per media; outbound, the MVP sends one media per message with its caption. **Echo of a caption (found in PR 1c, owner 2026-10-29):** Instagram and Messenger cannot carry a caption in the message of the file, so their plugins send it as a second message and answer `metadata.captionExternalId`; Meta echoes both. The sender must keep that id (the `Message` row has one `channelMessageId`) and the echo check of `InboundMessageService` must know it, so that the caption is not stored again as an `external` message; every other echo still enters as `external`. **Phase 2:** `mediaIds` with several entries, split by the core into consecutive sends when the channel takes one per send, the caption on the first, and a place for the `channelMessageId` of each part (`Message.channelMessageId` is one column today) **Done in PR C (outbound):** `OutboundSender` signs the download of the one clean file (`StorageService.downloadUrl`, 15 minutes, a fresh link per attempt) and calls `plugin.sendMedia` with the text as `caption` (none when blank), the reply target and the message id as the key; a plugin without `sendMedia`, a file that is missing, not clean or not alone fails the message for good (`CHANNEL_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED`, `CHANNEL_MESSAGE_FAILED`); a caption a plugin cannot carry (WhatsApp audio, too long) is refused by the plugin before the call, as before. The `captionExternalId` the plugin returns is read from the result (`ChannelResultMetadata.CAPTION_EXTERNAL_ID`, a constant now) and kept in `Message.metadata` by `MessageStatusWriter`; `InboundMessageService` looks an echo up by `channelMessageId` and then by that key, so the second message of Instagram and Messenger is a duplicate. **Not done, owned by PR E:** the thread does not draw the file yet (see the task below)

A file does **not** go through the offline queue (an upload needs the network); while it uploads or is scanned nothing is sent, so the text cannot leave without its file. `NEXT_PUBLIC_STORAGE_URL` (required, in the web env schema, `.env.example`, the Dockerfile and the CI build) joins `connect-src`, `img-src` and `media-src` of the CSP. **Known limits (rule 12):** (1) **closed in PR E2b:** the paperclip is hidden when the channel declares no kind of file, the picker offers only the kinds it declares (`acceptFor`), and a file of a kind it does not take is refused before the upload with `CHANNEL_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED` (`channelTakesFile`; the api still checks it); (2) the hook has no unit test because `apps/web` has no DOM test setup: its pure parts (`attachment-rules`, `waitUntilClean`) are tested and the refusal path has an e2e case; the real upload path (needs ClamAV in the e2e job) is tested by the api and worker suites; (3) one file per message (`MediaMessageLimits.MAX_FILES`); (4) removing the chip right after the `PUT` cannot cancel the confirmation already in flight, so that file stays a row with no message until the orphan cleanup takes it; (5) a file whose scan died for good answers `FILE_NOT_READY` like a slow one, so the composer waits its minute before failing; both close with the terminal state for an unscannable file of Step 3.5

(2) **Done in PR E2b:** a caption a channel cannot carry (WhatsApp audio, over 1024 characters, the text limit of Instagram and Messenger) is refused by `POST /messages` in `requireCaptionFits` from `ChannelCaptionRules` and `ChannelTextLimits`, before anything is queued. (3) **Done in PR E1:** `fileOf` fails a missing, not clean or multiple file with `FILE_UNAVAILABLE` (en and es text) and logs the statuses it found. (4) **Done in PR E2b:** the id of the message that carried the caption is the column `messages.caption_channel_message_id`, indexed with the conversation (migration `20261103120000_message_caption_channel_id` copies the old `metadata.captionExternalId` and removes it; rollback script included), and the echo lookup reads the column.

Created here because this step builds the first export **Server done in PR D1:** `ExportsModule` (`apps/api/src/modules/exports`): `GET /exports`, `GET /exports/:id`, `GET /exports/:id/download` (`@RequireAnyPermission` of `CONVERSATIONS_EXPORT`, `CUSTOMERS_EXPORT`, `MEDIA_DOWNLOAD_ALL`, a new decorator of the permission guard: one of the list is enough, `@RequirePermissions` still needs all of its own). **A member sees and downloads only the exports they asked for**, and only of the kinds their role may still make; another member's, another store's and a kind the role lost are the same 404 (an export is personal data on its way out, so "every export of the store" was narrowed on purpose). `request()` takes the store lock and refuses a fourth active export (`MAX_ACTIVE_PER_STORE`, 429; a `pending` row older than `ACTIVE_WINDOW_MINUTES` no longer counts), creates the row (`expiresAt` in 7 days: `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §10 item 25 wins over the 14 of `TDD_07 §7`, `DataRetentionPolicies.EXPORTS` is 7 now) and queues `GENERATE_EXPORT` under `generate-export-<id>`; a queue that refuses marks the row `failed`. The worker (`apps/worker/src/exports`): `ExportsProcessor` (queue `exports`, one at a time), `ExportGenerator` (reads the kind, filters and requester from the row, never from the job; a row that is not `pending` is left alone; filters that do not fit fail the row for good), `CsvExportWriter` (`StorageService.putStream`, multipart upload that is aborted when the source fails, so no half file stays), the audit entry with `ExportAuditActions` and the filters, `ExpiredExportsCleaner` + `ExpiredExportsScheduler` (`Schedules.CLEAN_EXPIRED_EXPORTS`, hourly: deletes the object of a `ready` export past its date and marks the row `expired`, gives up on a `pending` row older than `PENDING_GIVE_UP_HOURS`). A kind or state the page does not know draws as "Other" or "Unknown". The export kinds, formats and states moved from `libs/core/platform/queues` to `libs/core/domain/exports` (the web may not import the platform layer; the queues path re-exports them). Cases: `export-state.spec.ts`, `exports-api.spec.ts` and the e2e case of the gallery, which follows the toast link to the center. **Not done, owner Step 3.5:** the polling gives way to the `EXPORT_READY` realtime event; `AppPaths.store.exportDownload` (`/s/:storeId/exports/:id`, the `actionPath` of the notification) has no page yet, so the notification task must add one that opens the center on that row or point `actionPath` at the center; no filter by kind or state and no progress bar (the row carries no progress, only `pending`); **Done in E3c:** the CSV buttons (the customers page, which exports the search it shows, and the inbox bulk bar, in "More"). **Also not done:** the `EXPORT_READY` notification and realtime refresh wait for the notification service of Step 3.5 (task added there); rows of `failed` and `expired` exports stay (they hold no personal data, only the filters), a purge of old rows is a task of Step 7.3
- [x] The conversations export and the bulk action `EXPORT_CSV`, moved here from Step 3.2 because they are exports of this center: `GET /conversations/export` (declared before `GET /conversations/:id`) creates an `Export { type: CONVERSATIONS }` on the same queue, key and download as the ZIP, leaves out `type = SYSTEM` messages (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md`, "Inbound pipeline build details", item 2) and the content of a message the customer deleted, and the bulk action points at it **Route done in PR D1:** `GET /conversations/export` (`CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` + `CONVERSATIONS_EXPORT`, declared before `:id`, the filters of the list: `assigned`, `status`, `channelType`, `tagId`; the history of archived or spam needs `CONVERSATIONS_ARCHIVE`, as in the list) writes one row per message, oldest first inside each conversation. **What the file holds is what the member could see when they asked:** the row freezes the requester and the permissions that decide visibility (`viewPermissionsOf`), and the worker applies the same scope as the list (`conversationFilterWhere`, `visibleTo`, `assignedFilter`, moved to `conversations.support.ts` so both use one rule). Left out (rule 12, security first): `SYSTEM` lines (the checklist) and `INTERNAL_NOTE` messages (only the team reads them and the export permission does not say who may); the text of a deleted or purged message is empty and the row says `deleted`. **Done in PR E3c:** `POST /conversations/bulk` takes `export_csv` (up front: `CONVERSATIONS_EXPORT`, on top of the `CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` of the route). It changes no conversation: `ConversationBulkService.exportSelection` keeps the ids the member may see (`ConversationsService.visibleAmong`, the rule of the single routes), answers the others as `failed` with `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, and queues one `Export` whose filters hold `conversationIds` (no ids that passed, no row). A filter target is resolved to ids first, capped at `BulkLimits.MAX_ITEMS` with `truncated`, as every bulk action is. The file and the up-front check share one rule, `conversationsExportWhere` (`libs/core/messaging/src/exports/conversations-export-scope.ts`): a selection sees the store, what is the requester's or nobody's unless they see all, and archived or spam only when the requester may archive. The interface asks no confirmation (a file changes nothing) and its toast leads to the exports center. Cases: `export-filters.spec.ts`, `conversations-export-scope.spec.ts`, the five cases of "the bulk action Export CSV" in `tests/api/exports.spec.ts`, `bulk.spec.ts` and two e2e cases. **Debts, each with its owner (rule 12):** (1) a selection by filter stops at 500 conversations (the toast says so): Step 3.5, task "Export CSV of a whole filter". (2) The assignment of each conversation is read when the file is made, not when it was asked: Step 7.1, task "Exports leave the platform with personal data". **Decision (E1, rule 12):** the export row freezes filters, not ids, so a selection by ids needs `conversationIds` in the filters (checked against the visibility of the requester, capped at `BulkLimits.MAX_ITEMS`) and the same scope rule in the worker; that is PR E3's first task
- [x] Customers CSV export (`GET /customers/export` creates an `Export { type: CUSTOMERS }` on the same queue, S3 key and download as the ZIP; `CUSTOMERS_EXPORT`; `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §10 item 25 for the columns and rules; register the route before `customers/:id`, or `export` is read as an id) **Done in PR D1:** `GET /customers/export` (`CUSTOMERS_EXPORT`, declared before `:id`, 202 with the export, the `q` of the list through `customerSearchWhere`, now shared by the list and the file) with the columns and rules of item 25: UTF-8 with BOM, cells that start with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or carriage return get a single quote (`csvCell`, `libs/core/domain/exports`), absorbed customers out, the text of a note out (only how many), one row per customer read in pages of `ROW_PAGE_SIZE`, one audit entry with the filters and `rowCount`. **Known limit:** the six order columns are written empty until Milestone 4 (task added to Step 4.3). The "Export CSV" button of the customers page is drawn by PR E
- [x] **Purposes a client may upload for (found building PR A):** `ClientUploadPurposes` (`libs/core/domain/storage/upload-schemas.ts`) holds only `message`. An upload purpose is not added before the code that checks who may attach it: `product` with Step 4.1 and `order` with Step 4.3 (both need a `parentId` that belongs to the store, `TDD_07 §6.3`), `avatar` with Step 3.6 (the key has no store, `StorageKeys.avatar`, so the `confirmUpload` prefix check is by user there)
- [x] **Orphan uploads (PR B2, done):** `OrphanMediaCleaner` and `OrphanMediaScheduler` (`Schedules.CLEAN_ORPHAN_MEDIA`, hourly, one replica) delete the stray objects, requeue the scans that were lost (a finished job with the same id is removed first, or it would swallow the new one), drop a `pending` row whose file is gone once it is past `UNCONFIRMED_UPLOAD_TTL_HOURS`, and delete the file of an `infected` row. It lists `ORPHAN_LIST_PAGES_PER_RUN` pages per run and resumes from a cursor in Redis; the infected rows are looked at for `INFECTED_CLEANUP_WINDOW_HOURS` after their verdict. **Known limits:** a `pending` row whose scan fails for good is queued again every hour (a terminal state for it is a task of Step 3.5, with the notification of an unscannable file; until then 500 such rows can crowd out the rows behind them, the batch is oldest first), and the requester of an upload lives only in Redis (accepted: the member asks again). Speed of a full turn is a task of Step 7.3. The original task: a scheduled job in the worker deletes the objects under `stores/*/uploads/` and under `stores/*/messages/` with no `Media` row (a confirmation that died between the copy and the row leaves one at the final key; deleting it at the time could destroy the file of a concurrent confirmation of the same media) older than `UploadLimits.UNCONFIRMED_UPLOAD_TTL_HOURS` (the signed URL lets a member leave a file in the bucket and never confirm it), and the `pending` rows whose scan never ran, and the object of an `infected` row (the scan marks the row first and deletes the file second, so a failure in between leaves the file)
- [x] **Downloads never trust the name (PR B1, review finding of PR A):** the extension of `Media.filename` is the client's and may not match its declared type (`x.html` declared `image/png`). Every signed download sets `ResponseContentType` from `Media.contentType` and `ResponseContentDisposition: attachment` (inline only for the image variants, which are re-encoded WebP), so that nothing is ever served as active content by its extension
- [x] **Upload quota (PR B2, with the orphan cleanup; done):** 100 signed URLs per user and 300 per store per hour (`SIGNED_URLS_PER_USER_PER_HOUR`, `SIGNED_URLS_PER_STORE_PER_HOUR`), counted in Redis by hour, answered with 429 `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`. `upload-url` records who asked (`CacheKeys.upload.requester`, `PENDING_UPLOAD_TTL_SECONDS`) and `confirm-upload` answers `FILE_UPLOAD_FAILED` to anyone else, also for a media that already exists; a Redis that lost the record makes the member ask again. The original task: `upload-url` persists nothing, so a member with `MEDIA_UPLOAD` can fill the bucket with files that are never confirmed until the cleanup runs. Add a per-store (and per-user) limit of signed URLs per hour in Redis, and `UploadLimits` constants for it. The same step persists who asked for each signed URL (a pending-upload record, or an HMAC in the `mediaId`) so that `confirm-upload` accepts only that user: today any member holding the ids could confirm another's upload and be recorded as its `uploadedBy`
- [x] **Ad media fetched and stored (PR E4; debt of PR B2, which left it out):** `ConversationOrigin.mediaUrl` and `thumbnailUrl` are stored as links only, and nothing fetches them. Fetch them in a job of the heavy queue, store them as `Media` (scan and variants like any file) and draw them in the origin panel. The fetch is done by the core, not by a plugin, so it must call `assertPublicHttpsUrl` (`apps/worker/src/media/public-url.ts`), follow no redirect, and **pin the address it resolved** for the request (an agent with a fixed `lookup`), which closes the DNS change between the check and the call that B2 left open **Done in PR E4:** `Media.originId` and `originSlot` (migration `20261104120000_media_origin`, with its rollback; purpose `MediaPurpose.ORIGIN`; the row goes with its origin, `ON DELETE CASCADE`, and the orphan cleanup removes the object). `InboundMessageService.queueOriginFetches` queues one `FETCH_ORIGIN_MEDIA` job per slot (`OriginMediaSlots`: `thumbnail`, `media`) after the commit, only for an origin row the message created (the same ad again makes no row and no job), under the job id `fetch-origin-media-<origin id>-<slot>`; the job carries ids only and `OriginMediaFetcher` (`apps/worker/src/media`) reads the link from the row. The request is `pinnedHttpsGet`: `resolvePublicHttpsUrl` checks every address of the host, the socket is opened to the checked address through a fixed `lookup` (the certificate is still checked against the name of the link), no redirect is followed (3xx, 4xx are final; 429 and 5xx are retried), identity encoding, and the body is read with the size limit of an image (10 MB) and must declare an image type. The file is stored under the messages area of the bucket with a deterministic media id (`originMediaId`), `pending`, with no message and no customer (so the gallery and the media ZIP never see it), and queued to the scan and the variants like any file. `GET /conversations/:id/origins` carries `mediaFile` and `thumbnailFile` (`{ id, status }` for `pending` and `clean`; an infected file is left out, a file of another store is never matched); the card draws the signed link of the thumbnail (`GET /media/:id`), or the media when it is an image, and **never the address of the ad** (it expires and tells the ad's CDN who is looking), and the open conversation asks the origins again every 3 s while a picture of an origin under 5 minutes old is `pending`. Cases: `origin-media-fetcher.service.spec.ts`, `pinned-https-get.spec.ts`, `origin-picture.spec.ts`, two in `conversation-history.spec.ts`, two in `inbound.spec.ts` and one in `thread.spec.ts` (e2e). **Known limits (rule 12):** (1) a video of an ad is not fetched (it is the largest file that comes in and nothing draws it); the card shows the thumbnail, and a player is a task of Step 7.7 (visual polish); (2) origins recorded before this PR have no picture and are not backfilled here; the one-off script is the task "Backfill of ad pictures" of Step 7.3; (3) the pin covers the fetch the core makes; the fetch of a customer's file by a plugin (`mediaRef` as a link) keeps the address check of B2 and the plugin's own host list; (4) the file is held in memory while it is stored, as every fetch is (Step 7.3); (5) a picture that was dropped (refused type, unsafe link, 404) leaves no marker: the card has no picture, the origin keeps its title and body; (6) no event announces that the picture of an ad is ready, so the open conversation asks again every 3 s for 5 minutes after the origin was recorded (measured on the clock of the browser against the time of the message) and then waits for the next load: a `MEDIA_PROCESSED` for origin files, invalidating the origins, is a task of Step 3.5 (realtime and notifications). A generic `application/octet-stream` answer is accepted when the first bytes are an image; a link that fails its certificate or does not resolve is dropped without retry
- [x] **A customer file that could not be kept (PR E2b; debt of PR B2):** `InboundMediaFetcher` drops a file it refuses or cannot fetch and the message shows nothing. **Done in PR E2b (decision, rule 12):** the worker, and not `InboundMessageService`, records it when the job gives up: `UnavailableFileMarker` adds `{ index, code }` to `metadata.unavailableFiles` of the message (codes `MediaUnavailableCodes`: `refused`, `unsafe_link`, `fetch_failed`, `channel_cannot_fetch`; under the message row lock, because an album is several jobs on one row; one entry per place) and publishes `MEDIA_UNAVAILABLE`, which refreshes the open thread. Writing a "file expected" count when the fetch is queued was rejected: a file still being fetched would read as lost until the job ends, and the count adds nothing the give-up record does not say. A retryable failure is recorded only on the last attempt. `MessageView.unavailableFiles` carries the list (empty for a deleted message) and `MessageMedia` draws one line per entry, with a text for each code (en and es). The message type keeps its icon placeholder out of the way when a line is drawn
- [x] **Bucket CORS (PR E1):** the browser `PUT`s to the signed URL on the bucket host, so the bucket must allow the origin of the web app with `PUT` and the `content-type` header. **Verified in E1:** SeaweedFS 4.48 (development and CI) answers the preflight for `PUT` with `content-type` for any origin and exposes `ETag`, so nothing is configured there. **Left:** the CORS rule of the production S3 bucket (the origin of the web app, `PUT`, `content-type`, expose `ETag`) is a task of Step 7.3. **Added in PR F1:** the visitors of the web widget `PUT` from the sites of the stores, not from the web app, so the rule of the production bucket must also allow those origins (the signed URL is what authorizes the write, so `*` with `PUT` and `content-type` is enough there)
- [x] **A file missing from a media ZIP is told (debt of PR D2 of Step 3.4; done in PR E1):** `ZipExportWriter` adds a `missing.txt` entry (`MEDIA_ZIP_MISSING_ENTRY`, one name per line, no prose so it needs no language) when it leaves out a file that is no longer in the bucket; a ZIP in which every file is gone still fails with `EXPORT_EMPTY`
- [x] **Gallery counts only on the first page (debt of PR D2 of Step 3.4; done in PR E1):** `GET /customers/:id/media` runs the per-tab `groupBy` only without a cursor and answers `counts: null` on the pages that follow; the gallery page (PR E3) keeps the counts of the first page
- [x] **Accepted limits of the media ZIP (PR D2 of Step 3.4, owner not asked, rule 12):** (1) the ZIP holds the gallery as it is when the worker runs, so a merge between the request and the job moves the files to the survivor and the ZIP of the absorbed customer fails with `EXPORT_EMPTY`: the center (PR E) shows that code and the member asks again from the survivor; (2) the assignment of each conversation is read when the worker runs, while the requester's permissions are frozen in the row. No change planned; revisit with the conversations export limits of Step 7.3
- [x] **The phone of the pre-chat form (debt of PR D2 of Step 3.3, owner 2026-11-01; PR F):** the widget form checks the phone by characters only and the api drops a phone it cannot read without saying so. Make the api answer with a field error the bundle shows, cheaper than shipping `libphonenumber-js` in the bundle (26.5 KB gzip of the 40 KB budget) **Server done in PR F1:** with the form on, `POST /widget/messages` answers 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` with `metadata.issues` `[{ path: 'profile.phone', code: 'invalid_phone' }]` (`WidgetFieldIssues`) for a phone `normalizePhone` cannot read, before any file is claimed or anything is queued; a store with the form off still drops the profile as before. **Left (PR F3):** the bundle reads that issue and shows `invalidPhone` under the phone field **Done in PR F3:** the bundle reads `metadata.issues` (`WidgetApiError.phoneRefused`); when the api refuses the phone the message stays (failed), the form comes back with `invalidPhone` under the phone input (`aria-invalid`), and the messages held back are sent when the visitor submits it again (`Session.setProfile`). e2e: a case of `widget.spec.ts`

**TDD:** `TDD_07 §6`, `§7`
**Done when:** an image uploads, is scanned, generates variants, and is served through
signed URLs; a `confirmUpload` with a foreign store prefix returns `FILE_KEY_OUT_OF_SCOPE`; a message sent with `mediaIds` shows the attachment in the thread, and one naming media of another store is refused.

### Step 3.5 — Notifications
- [x] Notification service with `messageKey` + params, preference resolution, quiet hours from `UserNotificationSettings`, and its routes: `notifications.list`, `unreadCount`, `markRead`, `markAllRead` and `preferences` (PR A of Step 3.5; `NotificationsService.send` saves the row and emits `NOTIFICATION` to the sockets of the user in that store). **Decisions of PR A:** (1) these routes belong to the member and not to a role, so they carry the new `@MemberOfStore()` (the permission guard lets in an active member of the store of the header; `route-protection.spec` refuses it next to a permission or an exempt route); (2) `markRead` answers 404 when none of the ids is the caller's, and skips the foreign ones otherwise; (3) quiet hours read the hour with `Intl` (`libs/core/domain/notifications/quiet-hours.ts`), not `date-fns-tz`, which the repository does not have. **Left to later PRs of this step (written here, not only in the PR):** `send` does not queue web push (PR C adds `SEND_WEB_PUSH` and the `pushSubscription` route) nor the email digest (PR D adds `QUEUE_EMAIL_DIGEST`); `crossStoreEnabled` and `crossStoreSupervisorOnly` are stored and returned but nothing reads them yet: the notification types of the MVP are all raised inside one store, so the setting takes effect in the first step that notifies across stores (owner: PR E draws the switches; no step uses them before Phase 2). **Review findings left on purpose (PR A, owner: PR F of this step):** (1) `sendToStore` reads the settings and the business hours of the store once per recipient, and `send` could run the preference and settings reads together: hoist the store's hours out of the loop when a type reaches a large team; (2) the follow-store branch of quiet hours repeats `WidgetAway.isAway` (hours missing or unreadable means open): extract one `isStoreClosed(storeId, at)` for both; (3) `PUT preferences` upserts the type rows one by one inside the transaction: batch them if the page ever sends the whole matrix at once
- [x] **The picture of an ad is ready (debt of PR E4 of Step 3.4):** `MediaPipeline` publishes `MEDIA_PROCESSED` only for a file that belongs to a message, so the origin card polls for 5 minutes instead of being told. Publish it for a file of an origin with the conversation's visibility, bridge it, and have the web invalidate the origins of that conversation; then drop `refetchInterval` from `useOrigins` **Done in PR F2 of Step 3.5:** `MediaPipeline.announce` falls back to the message that carried the ad (`ConversationOrigin.messageId`, unique) when the file has no message of its own, so the existing `MEDIA_PROCESSED`, `MEDIA_INFECTED` and `MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` reach whoever may read that message and the web refreshes the thread scope, which holds `QueryKeys.messages.origins`.
