No new event, payload or bridge entry. `useOrigins` no longer polls (`originsNeedRefresh`, `ORIGIN_REFRESH_MS` and its window are gone). **Decisions (rule 12):** (1) the event is the message's, not a new origin event: the visibility rule (`visibility`, `internal`) is the one of the conversation and nothing widens; (2) an infected picture of an ad now also raises the `MEDIA_INFECTED` notification of PR B1 to who may read the conversation, which is right for a file that came from outside; (3) a picture that was dropped (refused type, unsafe link, 404) still announces nothing and leaves the card as it was. **Known limit:** a client that was offline when the scan ended shows the placeholder until its next load of the conversation. Case: two in `media-pipeline.events.spec.ts`
- [x] **An infected file (debt of PR E1 of Step 3.4, done in PR B1 of Step 3.5):** `MEDIA_INFECTED` reaches the open thread but nobody is notified. Add a `MEDIA_INFECTED` notification type with its constants and send it (`TDD_07 §6.5`) to the members who may read the conversation of the message and add the audit entry, from the same event **Decisions of PR B1:** (1) `NotificationsEventsListener` (api) hears `MESSAGE_MENTION` and `MEDIA_INFECTED` and decides who is told when it runs, not when the event was raised, with the new `conversationReaders` filter (`libs/core/messaging`), which `requireMentionable` now uses too: one rule for the check of a mention, the mention notification and the infected one; (2) `AppPaths` moved to `libs/core/domain` (the api may not import `api-client`) and `api-client` re-exports it, so the web is unchanged; (3) an infected file that belongs to no message has no event and so no notification: the composer shows its state (the worker only announces files of a message); the audit entry `AuditAction.MEDIA_INFECTED` is written by the same listener with a system actor. **Left on purpose (owner: PR F):** the audit entry is one per message, not per file (the event carries no media id); `sendToUsers` writes for all recipients at once, fine for a team of the MVP, to be batched with the PR A findings if a store grows
- [x] **An export that is ready (debt of PR D1 of Step 3.4, done in PR B2a of Step 3.5):** `ExportGenerator` marks the row `ready` and audits it, but sends nothing. Send `NotificationTypes.EXPORT_READY` to `Export.requestedBy` with `{ filename, type }` and the path of the download (`TDD_07 §7`), and publish `Events.EXPORT_READY` (its payload type does not exist yet) so that the exports center refreshes without a reload; do the same for a failed export. **Also here (debt of PR E3b):** drop the polling of `useExportList` (`EXPORT_POLL_MS`) for the event, and give `AppPaths.store.exportDownload` (`/s/:storeId/exports/:id`, the `actionPath` of this notification) a page, or point the `actionPath` at the center `AppPaths.store.exports` **Decisions of PR B2a:** (1) `ExportGenerator` and `ExpiredExportsCleaner` (the give-up of a stale `pending` row) publish `Events.EXPORT_READY` / the new `Events.EXPORT_FAILED` after the row settles, only from the call that settled it, with ids and the kind (`ExportSettledPayload`); both cross the bridge (`bridged-payloads.ts`), and a failure to announce is logged, never fails the job; (2) the api turns them into `RealtimeEvents.EXPORT_UPDATED` (`{ exportId }`, to the tabs of the requester only) and into `NotificationTypes.EXPORT_READY` / the new `EXPORT_FAILED` (`NotificationsEventsListener`), which reads the row, checks it is in the state of the event, belongs to that requester in that store, and that the requester is still an active member whose role makes that kind (`ExportTypePermissions`, moved from `ExportsService` to `libs/core/domain/exports` so the listener and the service share one rule); (3) the `actionPath` is the center, `AppPaths.store.exports`, so `AppPaths.store.exportDownload` was removed instead of given a page; (4) the web drops `EXPORT_POLL_MS` and `hasPendingExport`: `EXPORT_UPDATED` and a reconnect invalidate `QueryKeys.exports.all`. **Left on purpose (review of PR B2a):** settling the row and announcing it are two steps without an outbox, so a worker killed between them loses the notification and the live refresh; the page no longer polls, so such a row shows `pending` until a reload or a socket reconnect (owner: Step 7.3, with the purge of old export rows; the `pending` give-up of the cleaner settles it within 24 h); the texts of `notifications.export.failed` were added to both message files next to `ready`.
- [x] **"Export CSV" of a whole filter (debt of PR E3c of Step 3.4):** the bulk action by filter stops at `BulkLimits.MAX_ITEMS` (500) and the toast says the file was cut. This step already reworks the exports flow (the `EXPORT_READY` event), so it also lets the bulk bar export every conversation of a filter: the export row keeps the filter instead of ids (the plain `GET /conversations/export` already works so), and the "select all matching" text stops promising 500 for this one action. Close it with a case that exports more than 500. **Done in PR F2 of Step 3.5:** `POST /conversations/bulk` with `export_csv` and a `filter` no longer resolves ids: `ConversationBulkService.exportFilter` counts the matches (`ConversationsService.countMatching`, the rule of `idsMatching`, which also refuses the archived history to who may not archive) and writes the row with the filter and the frozen viewer, as `GET /conversations/export` does. The answer is `{ succeeded: [], failed: [], truncated: false, exported: <n> }` (`BulkResult.exported`, optional in `bulkResultSchema`); a filter that matches nothing writes no row (`exported: 0`). A selection by ticked ids is unchanged. The web counts `exported` in `summaryOf`, the toast no longer says the file was cut (`exportTruncated` is gone) and the texts of "select all matching" say that Export CSV is the one action without the limit. **Found by the 501-conversation case and fixed here:** `conversationsExportSource` paged with `id > last` and also with `cursor` + `skip: 1`, which dropped the first conversation of every page after the first (a file of more than 500 conversations lost one per page, also in `GET /conversations/export`); the keyset alone is correct. Cases: `exports every conversation of a filter, past the limit of the other bulk actions` and `writes no file when a filter matches nothing` in `tests/api/exports.spec.ts`, and one in `bulk.spec.ts` (web)
- [x] (PR B1 of Step 3.5) The mention notification (`NotificationTypes.CONVERSATION_MENTIONED`, from `Events.MESSAGE_MENTION`) goes only to who may open the conversation and read its notes; Step 2.3 already refuses a mention of anyone else, but a role may change after the note is written
- [x] Notification center UI rendering keys (PR D of Step 3.5): `NotificationCenter` in the top bar of the store area (bell, `99+` badge, a sheet with the tabs All / Unread / Mentions, "Mark all read", load more); the text is `messageKey + .title/.body` with `notifications.unknown` for a key the page does not have; a click marks the row read and goes to `actionPath` only if it is a path of the app (`safeActionPath`). **Decisions of PR D:** (1) a sheet and not a popover, to reuse the focus trap of the existing primitive; (2) "Mark all read" marks every notification of the store, whatever the tab, and is disabled with nothing unread; (3) the link "Notification settings" of `TDD_06 §8` arrives with the preferences page (PR E), since a link to a page that does not exist would be a 404. **Left on purpose (owner: PR F):** the relative time of an open panel does not tick; a message whose ICU params do not match the server's relies on next-intl's own error handling; a mark-read refetches the cached tabs, not only the active one. **Not run locally:** `notifications.spec.ts` (e2e) could not run on this machine, where the worker fails to render any email (`renderToPipeableStream is not a function`) even on a clean `main`; CI runs it
- [x] **Web Push, the server (PR C1 of Step 3.5; PR C2 below draws the browser side):** `NotificationsService.send` queues `SEND_WEB_PUSH` (one job per notification, `jobId` = name + notification id) when the type's `push` is on and the user is not in quiet hours; the worker's `NotificationsProcessor` hands it to `WebPushSender`, which writes the title and body in the person's locale from the `notifications.<type>` messages (`push-payload.ts`), sends to every registered browser through `WebPushGateway` and drops a subscription the push service answers 404 or 410 for. Routes `POST /notifications/push-subscription` and `POST /notifications/push-subscription/remove` (`@MemberOfStore()`, 204; a POST and not a DELETE with a body, which proxies drop). **Decisions of PR C1:** (1) the endpoint is a url the worker will call, so it is accepted only on the host of a known push service (`PushServiceHosts`, exact hosts and suffixes in `libs/core/domain/notifications/push-constants.ts`), never a host the caller names (SSRF); a browser on a push service that is not listed needs its host added there; (2) the keys of a registration must be base64url of 65 and 16 bytes (`isBase64UrlOfBytes`) and the VAPID pair of 65 and 32, so a bad key stops at the door and not as a retry storm; the wait for the queue is bounded by `PushLimits.ENQUEUE_TIMEOUT_MS`; `PushSubscription` belongs to the person and not to a store (no `storeId`, no RLS), so there is no `tenant-isolation.spec` case; the isolation of one person from another (a foreign endpoint is not removed, the same browser moves to the next person who registers it) is in `tests/api/notifications.spec.ts`; (3) at most `PushLimits.MAX_SUBSCRIPTIONS_PER_USER` browsers per person, the least recently used dropped; `send` queues no job for a person with no browser registered; each push has a `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`; a refusal that will not pass (401, 403) is logged as an error; (4) a failure of the push service that may pass (429, 5xx, network) fails the job, which the queue retries 5 times; the retry repeats the push to the browsers that already got it, and the shared `tag` makes the device replace it instead of showing it twice; a failure that will not pass (400, 401, 403, 413) is logged and not retried; (5) a queue that is down loses the push and never the action that raised the notification (logged); (6) the keys travel with each call (`vapidDetails`), not through the global state of `web-push`, and `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` / `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` are now required together (`env-schema`); without them the worker sends nothing and says nothing. **Left on purpose (owner: PR F):** registering is not one transaction (two registrations at once may leave one row over the cap until the next); an endpoint already registered by another person moves to the caller without proof (the endpoint is an unguessable url only that browser knows); a subscription the push service refuses for good (400, 401, 403, 413) is logged as an error and kept, with nothing that expires it (drop it after N failures, or by `lastUsed`); the VAPID keys are checked for format but not required in a deployed environment (decide with the deploy config of Step 8); each retry of a failed push repeats it to every browser (one job per browser would stop that); `send` reads the subscriptions once in the api and again in the worker; a push is sent to a person who was suspended in the store between the notification and the job (seconds, text of the notification only); nothing yet reads `lastUsed` to expire a subscription that is alive but never used
- [x] **Web Push, the browser (PR C2 of Step 3.5):** Serwist service worker (`apps/web/sw.ts`), the push and notification-click handlers only; the caching rules of `TDD_09 §16.3` and the manifest are Step 8.3, which adds them to the same file **Web image (left by PR E1):** `NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` is read by `env-schema.ts` but is not a build argument of `apps/web/Dockerfile`, and Next.js inlines it at build time, so add it as an `ARG`/`ENV` there and to the `security-scan` leg of `web` in this task, or the image ships without push **Decisions of PR C2:** (1) `apps/web/sw.ts` is built by `@serwist/turbopack` (the route `app/serwist/[path]/route.ts`, served as `/serwist/sw.js` with `Service-Worker-Allowed: /`; Next 16 builds with Turbopack, which the webpack plugin of `@serwist/next` does not serve); `esbuild` is a pinned dev dependency because the route imports it by name; the worker imports nothing from the `serwist` runtime yet (Step 8.3 adds the caching), and `libs/core/domain/notifications/notification-constants.ts` now imports `../status-enums` instead of the barrel `../index`, which had put zod and all of the domain in the worker (455 KB, now 5 KB); (2) the worker is registered by the prompt, not on every page (`lib/push/push-browser.ts`), so no person pays for it until they say yes; `SerwistProvider` is not mounted (its `reloadOnOnline` and cache-on-navigation belong to Step 8.3); (3) `proxy.ts` leaves `/serwist/sw.js` (and its map) out of its matcher: a browser fetches the worker with no page around it and its update check must not be sent to the login; (4) the body of a push is validated before it is shown (`parsePushPayload`), and the click opens only a path of the origin (`targetPathOf`: `//host`, other origins and `javascript:` go to `/`); (5) the prompt (`usePushOffer`, toast with Turn on / Not now) is asked once per browser after a conversation is resolved (`StorageKeys.PUSH_OFFERED`), never when the permission is denied, and a browser that already allows it registers again silently on each resolve (another person may have used it); (6) signing out removes the push registration of this browser (`unsubscribeFromPush`, best effort, with the last store id since the route needs `X-Store-Id`), so a shared computer does not show the next notifications of the person who left; (7) `sw.ts` has its own program, `apps/web/tsconfig.sw.json` (webworker types, no dom), excluded from the one of the app: the `type-check` target of `web` runs both, and `eslint.config.js` points the parser at it for that one file, so worker globals never leak into the pages; (8) `NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` is a build argument of `apps/web/Dockerfile` and of the `web` leg of `security-scan`, optional like its schema. **Left on purpose:** (a) the notification has no `icon` or `badge`: the files arrive with the manifest of Step 8.3 (owner: Step 8.3), the browser draws its own meanwhile; (b) there is no e2e of a real push (a headless browser has no push service): the handlers and the click are unit-tested, and the first real delivery is checked by hand with a VAPID pair (owner: PR F, which also closes the step); (c) a session lost by an expired refresh does not remove the registration (only the sign-out does); the server drops a dead one when the push service answers 410 (owner: PR F with the other subscription expiry debts); (d) the switch "notifications on this device", for a person who pressed Not now or turned it off, is drawn by the preferences page of the task below, with `subscribeToPush` and `unsubscribeFromPush`
- [x] **A file that cannot be scanned (debt of PR B2 of Step 3.4, done in PR B2b of Step 3.5):** `OrphanMediaCleaner` queues again, every hour, a `pending` row whose scan fails for good. Give the row a terminal state after a number of tries (a `MediaStatus` value, with its constants), stop requeuing it, and notify the team of the store (`MEDIA_INFECTED` is the model, `TDD_07 §6.5`) **Decisions of PR B2b:** (1) the number of tries is the attempts of the scan job (`isLastAttempt`): on the last one `MediaPipeline.scan` writes the new `MediaStatus.UNSCANNABLE` (a `status` string, so no migration and no rollback script), deletes the file and still throws, so the job ends in the dead-letter queue; the hourly cleanup only looks at `pending`, so it never queues the row again, and its infected-files pass also deletes the file of an `unscannable` row if the delete in the scan failed; (2) a failure that is the bucket's (`the file to scan is not in the bucket`) is not given up: the row stays `pending` and the cleanup drops it as before; (3) `Events.MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` (bridged), `RealtimeEvents.MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` (refreshes the thread), `NotificationTypes.MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` (in-app only, no push: nothing is unsafe, the sender must send it again), `AuditAction.MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE`, all raised only for a file of a message, exactly as the infected one; (4) `GET /media/:id` and `POST /messages` answer `FILE_UNSCANNABLE` with 410, so the composer stops at once instead of waiting its minute (closes limit (5) of the composer task of Step 3.4), and the thread draws the file as discarded. **Left on purpose:** a daemon that is down for longer than the retries of the job turns every file uploaded in that window into `unscannable` (the member uploads them again); the retry policy is the queue's, to be revisited with the alerts of Step 7.3. **Review findings left on purpose (PR B2b, owner: PR F of this step, with the PR A and B1 findings):** (1) `report.infectedFilesDeleted` of the cleanup also counts the files of `unscannable` rows, and neither kind of row is ever removed: rename the counter and purge old rows; (2) the "tell each person once" check of `MEDIA_INFECTED` and `MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` reads and then writes, so two concurrent deliveries could notify twice: use a unique key or an upsert; (3) the 410/403 status branch of a media is written out in `MediaService.urlsFor` and `MessagesService`: extract one `assertMediaServable(status)`. **Flaky on main, not caused by this PR (owner: its own fix PR, before Step 3.5 closes):** `media-pipeline.integration.spec` ("queues its variants once", "makes the three WebP variants") and sometimes the cleaner's "queues the scan again" fail with `job could not be removed because it is locked by another worker`, because the `WorkerModule` of the test runs the processor that takes the job before `job.remove()`; fix by pausing the queue or not registering the processor in those specs **Done in PR F3 of Step 3.5:** the flaky media specs now drop their queued job through `dropJob` (`apps/worker/src/testing/drop-job.ts`), which ignores only the `locked by another worker` error; it did not reproduce on this machine (3 runs green) and `compile()` starts no worker, so the cause is a worker of another process on the shared Redis, and the assertions stay strict. Finding (3), `assertMediaServable(status)` in `libs/core/messaging/src/messages/media-guard.ts` (not in the `errors` barrel, which the web bundles), replaces the two copies in `MediaService.urlsFor` and `MessagesService`
- [x] Preferences page (PR E of Step 3.5): `/account/notifications` (`AppPaths.account.notifications`), generated from the types the api returns and grouped by `NotificationGroupTypes` (`libs/core/domain/notifications`; the org-level data-request types are in no group and never appear): the matrix In-app / Push / Email / Sound of one store, the push switch of this browser (`usePushSwitch`; `denied` shows a text that says to allow it in the site settings), quiet hours (range, time zone, follow the store), the digest choice and the two cross-store switches; each change saves at once with an optimistic update. The bell panel links to it. **Decisions of PR E:** (1) the account area has no store, so the matrix is the one of the store opened last (`readLastStoreId`, falling back to the first membership) and the page says which; a store switcher on this page is not built (the top-bar switcher changes the last store); (2) the In-app column is shown but locked on: it is how a notification exists at all, and the api would accept `inApp: false` only through a hand-made request; (3) a type no group lists is left out of the page, as `TDD_06 §9` says of org-level types. **Left on purpose (review of PR E, owner: PR F3):** the page and the top bar do not share a store switcher, and the store is the last one stored in the browser, so two tabs on two stores can edit the other's matrix; switching push off in the page removes the browser subscription even if the call that removes the server row fails (the stale endpoint is dropped when the push service answers 410); the email column and the digest choice are stored and shown, but nothing sends mail until PR F1; `crossStoreEnabled` and `crossStoreSupervisorOnly` are stored and drawn, and still read by no code (see PR A); the labels of types that no step raises yet (orders, shipping, inventory) are written now so that they do not wait for Milestone 4
- [x] Email digests (PR F1 of Step 3.5; the choice lives on the preferences page above): `NotificationsService.send` marks the row with the new `Notification.emailPendingAt` when the person has email on for the type (quiet hours do not hold it back) and queues `QUEUE_EMAIL_DIGEST`; the worker's `EmailDigestService` acts on it (immediate: one email now; off: clears the mark; hourly and daily: nothing, the scheduler owns it). `EmailDigestScheduler` runs at :10 (`SEND_EMAIL_DIGEST_HOURLY` and `_DAILY`, each with its own leader lock): the hourly pass queues `SEND_EMAIL_DIGEST` for the people who chose hourly, the daily one for the people whose own clock (`UserNotificationSettings.timezone`) reads 08:xx (`isDailyDigestHour`); the job renders `EmailTemplates.NOTIFICATION_DIGEST` (`libs/core/email`, messages `email.notificationDigest` in en and es) and queues it on the email queue. **Decisions of PR F1:** (1) a column and not "rows since `lastDigestSentAt`" (`TDD_06 §6.4` is corrected): the mark records that the person wanted this one by email, so a preference changed later cannot make the digest send or miss the wrong rows; `lastDigestSentAt` is still written, for information; the migration has its rollback; (2) read, older than `DigestLimits.MAX_AGE_HOURS` (48 h) or addressed to an `emailValid = false` account: the mark is cleared and no email goes, so switching the digest from off to hourly never sends last week; (3) one email lists at most `DigestLimits.MAX_ITEMS` (20), newest first, and counts the rest; (4) a link is built only from a path of the app (`actionPath` on the origin of `FRONTEND_URL`); a path that names another host becomes no link; (5) the job is named after the person and the hour (scan) or the newest row (email), and the marks are cleared after the email is queued: a crash between the two repeats the email at most once, never loses it; (6) `FRONTEND_URL` reaches the worker through `DigestPlatformModule` (`libs/core/http`), since the apps may not read the env. **Left on purpose (owner: PR F3):** the email carries no `List-Unsubscribe` header, only the link to the preferences page; the global suppression list (SaaS backlog) is not consulted, only `User.emailValid`; a person with more than 200 marked rows gets the rest on the next run; (review of PR F1, one pass, not fixed) every notification with email on queues `QUEUE_EMAIL_DIGEST` even for hourly and daily, where the worker does nothing (read the frequency in `send`, or drop the job for those two); a person who switches to immediate keeps their older marks until the 48 h drop; the email job id can be deduplicated against a retained failed job after a crash between queueing and clearing; the hourly and daily scans share an expression and run the same `distinct` query twice (one scan split by frequency); the index on `email_pending_at` could be partial (`WHERE email_pending_at IS NOT NULL`, by hand in the migration); the email does not recheck the per-type preference at send time, only the membership; the "Open the app" link goes to the root of the web app, not to a store. **Done in PR F3 of Step 3.5:** `send` reads the digest frequency (only for a type with email on) and queues `QUEUE_EMAIL_DIGEST` for the immediate kind only; it marks nothing when the digest is off; the index on `email_pending_at` is partial (`ix_notifications_email_pending`, raw SQL in migration `20261107120000_notification_email_pending_partial_index`, with its rollback; out of `schema.prisma` like the other partial indexes); the email carries `List-Unsubscribe` (the preferences page; `SendEmailJob.headers`, passed to the provider) and its "Open the app" button opens the inbox of the newest store it speaks of. **Not done, written in the task below:** no one-click `List-Unsubscribe-Post` (RFC 8058), which needs a signed-token endpoint
- [x] **Debts moved here from Step 3.4 (rule 12; owner: PR F4 of this step, in as many PRs as it needs):** (0) `POST /widget/messages` answers the ids of the media of the message, so the widget can open a file it just sent without waiting for the history; (1) the thread cannot send only the caption of a reply whose caption failed (`captionFailed`): it needs a column for the caption state, with the unscannable-file state above; (2) the thread cannot re-fetch a customer file that was lost (`unavailableFiles`; the dead-letter queue is the only way): keep the provider's media id in the message and add the button; (3) the caption call of Instagram and Messenger turns any failure into `captionFailed`, also a timeout after which Meta may have delivered the text, and the echo is then stored as a message of the owner: use the provider's idempotency for the second call; (4) and (5) are done in PR F4a: the gallery list carries the signed thumbnail link of each image and a tile downloads in the same tab, and the exports center filters by kind and state and draws a progress bar from `Export.progress` (the worker counts first and writes the percent at most every 1.5 s). **Known limits of F4a, left on purpose (owner: Step 7.3):** the list polls every 3 s while any row is pending and a row stuck pending polls for as long as the page is open (the worker gives it up after 24 h); a ZIP bar can stop short of 99 % when files are skipped; the `expired` filter uses an `OR` the index `(storeId, status, createdAt)` does not serve. **Done in PR F4b-1 of Step 3.5 (items (1) column and (3)):** `Message.captionState` (`CaptionState`: `sent`, `failed`, `unconfirmed`; null when the text travels with its file) replaces the `captionFailed` flag of the metadata (migration `20261109120000_message_caption_state` with rollback; old flags and caption ids are backfilled), and the view carries `captionState` instead of `captionFailed`. Meta documents no idempotency key on the Send API, so the second call of Instagram and Messenger cannot be made safe; the plugin now tells a refusal from a call that got no clear answer (`PluginError.outcomeUnknown`, set by `graphFailure` and `graphUnreachable` for a send that is not safe to repeat, and for a 2xx that does not parse): a refusal or throttle is `failed`, a timeout, 5xx, outage code or unreadable 2xx is `unconfirmed`, and the second message is never sent again by the platform. The echo of an `unconfirmed` caption settles it: `InboundMessageService.reconcileUnconfirmedCaption` matches an owner echo of type text with the same text to the oldest unconfirmed caption of the identity's conversations, writes its id and `sent`, and stores no new message. The thread says "may not have been sent, check the chat" for `unconfirmed`. **Decisions of PR F4b-1:** (1) reconcile by text and not by resending or by a time window: a resend risks a double text for the customer, and a match by text is the only link an echo with an unknown id offers; the cost is that an owner who types the very same text in the app while a caption is unconfirmed has that echo taken for it, which only hides a duplicate line, never loses a message; (2) `unconfirmed` is not timed out into `failed`, because no echo can mean "not delivered" or "not echoed" (the app may not subscribe to echoes); (3) the old `captionFailed` key is kept in the reserved metadata keys so a provider cannot draw it. (4) an echo settles an `unconfirmed` caption only within `OutboundLimits.UNCONFIRMED_CAPTION_MS` (1 h) of the reply, so the owner typing the same text days later is stored; (5) the writer never takes a caption back from `sent`. **Known limits (owner: Step 7.3):** a tab that was open before the deploy reads `captionFailed`, which the view no longer carries (a reload fixes it); an open thread keeps the "may not have been sent" line until its next refresh after the echo settles it (no realtime event for it); an echo that arrives before the worker writes `unconfirmed` is retried by the existing wait for the provider id, and one that arrives after the file was settled but with a text edited by Meta (trimmed, normalised) is stored as the owner's message. **Done in PR F4b-2 of Step 3.5 (item (1), the caption-only resend):** `POST /messages/:id/resend-caption` (`MESSAGES_SEND`, `ApiRoutes.messages.resendCaption`, `MessagesService.resendCaption`) takes the text of a file whose `captionState` is `failed` or `unconfirmed` (`ResendableCaptionStates`) to the new state `CaptionState.QUEUED`, stamps `changeRequestedAt`, bumps `version` and queues `SEND_OUTBOUND_CAPTION` (`captionJobId` = message + version); the worker's `MessageChangeSender.sendCaption` sends the text alone with `plugin.sendMessage` and `MessageChangeStatusWriter.settleCaption` writes `sent` (with the id of the text, so its echo is not stored again), `failed` or `unconfirmed`, only while the state is still `queued`; `OutboundRequeueScheduler.requeueStaleChanges` queues a lost job again after two minutes. The bubble draws the notice and the button "Send the text again" (`CaptionNotice`); the text of `captionFailed` no longer says to send the text as a new message. **Decisions of PR F4b-2:** (1) the outcome is read as the Meta plugin reads it: a `PluginError` without `outcomeUnknown` is a refusal (`failed`; retried only while it says `retryable` and attempts remain), any other error (timeout, network, 5xx, a 2xx that does not parse) is `unconfirmed` and is never repeated by the job, since Meta has no idempotency key; (2) the sweep never queues a caption again: a request still `queued` after two minutes ends `unconfirmed`, because a job that sent the text and could not write it down looks like a lost job and sending twice is worse than telling the agent to check the chat; the write of every outcome is retried `OutboundLimits.WRITE_ATTEMPTS` times before the job gives up, and `settleCaption` names the `version` of the request so a late job settles nothing; (3) resending from `unconfirmed` may show the text twice, which the agent chose after being told to check the chat, and an echo with the same text settles a caption that is `unconfirmed` or `queued` (a resent text whose echo comes before the worker writes it), within an hour of the reply or of the last request; (4) the request needs the messaging window open and a conversation that is not spam, like any text, but does not reopen a resolved conversation nor record a reply: it completes a reply already sent; (5) one request at a time per message: it is refused while an edit or a deletion is queued or when the file itself failed (that one is sent again whole), and a deletion or a whole resend is refused while the text is queued, because they settle through `changeRequestedAt` or would send the text twice; (6) the thread is refreshed with the existing `message.edited` event, which only invalidates the thread; (7) the route adds no resource, so it has no case of its own in `tests/security/tenant-isolation.spec.ts` (`validate:isolation` accepts only model names); a message of another store answers 404 and stays untouched in `tests/api/caption-resend.spec.ts`, and `route-protection.spec` finds the controller by name. **Left on purpose (owner: Step 7.3):** if the write of `sent` fails three times after the text went out, the sweep marks the message `unconfirmed` (the customer has the text; the thread says to check the chat); the worker does not check the messaging window again when the job runs, only the api when it is asked (a job is at most two minutes old before the sweep gives it up). **Review findings of PR F4b-2 left on purpose (hardening, owner: Step 7.3):** the sweep test asserts the global counts of `requeueStaleChanges` like the edit one does (use a store of its own); the queued notice reuses the warning icon; the echo test of a `queued` caption has no negative control for another text. **Done in PR F4c of Step 3.5 (item (2), a lost customer file downloaded again):** when the worker gives up on a file with `fetch_failed`, `UnavailableFileMarker.mark` now keeps in the entry of `unavailableFiles` where the file came from (`source`: plugin, connection, the provider's media id, type and name; `lostFileSourceSchema` in `libs/core/messaging/src/messages/lost-files.ts`), and the view says `canRetry` (never the source). `POST /messages/:id/files/:index/refetch` (`CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` + `MESSAGES_SEND`, `ApiRoutes.messages.refetchFile`, `MediaRefetchService`) queues the same `FETCH_INBOUND_MEDIA` job from that stored source under a new job id, and stamps `requestedAt` in the entry; the worker fetches as before and `marker.clear` removes the line when the file is stored. The line of the thread shows "Download it again" (`UnavailableItem`, `MessageActions.refetchFile`). **Decisions of PR F4c:** (1) the job is built on the server from what the worker kept; the request carries only the message and the place, so a caller can never name a URL or a provider id; (2) only the plain id of the provider's media is kept (`^[\w-]+$`, the same test the fetcher uses to tell an id from a link): a file named by a link (Instagram, Messenger) keeps no source, because a link is a credential that expires and an address the customer chose, and the fetch-media block of private addresses (`assertPublicHttpsUrl`) stays as the only gate for links; (3) only `fetch_failed` is retryable (`RefetchableUnavailableCodes`): `refused`, `unsafe_link` and `channel_cannot_fetch` give the same answer on every try; (4) one request every `MediaRefetchLimits.COOLDOWN_MS` (2 min) per file, answered 409 inside it, so a double click or a slow job cannot stack jobs (rejected: a new column or a `refetching` state, which would need a migration for a flag that the entry holds); (5) no new column and no migration: the source lives in the metadata, whose reserved key `unavailableFiles` a provider cannot write (`RESERVED_METADATA_KEYS`); (6) only a message of the customer that is not deleted; the messaging window is not needed (nothing is sent to the customer); (7) the route adds no resource, so it has no case in `tests/security/tenant-isolation.spec.ts`; a message of another store answers 404 and stays untouched in `tests/api/media-refetch.spec.ts`. (8) a refusal the provider will repeat (`retryable: false`) is recorded with no source, so it is not offered again; a code of a newer worker is drawn as the generic one but is never retryable; a new answer for a place already listed replaces its entry (a refetch that now fails as `refused` stops offering the button); the job is queued inside the transaction, so a queue that is down fails the request (503) and leaves no wait behind; a `requestedAt` that cannot be read counts as no wait. **Review of PR F4c (two security/correctness passes, 10 then 8 findings; the second was under 10):** fixed, the nine real ones above; accepted and left on purpose (owner: Step 7.3): `canRetry` stays true during the 2-minute wait (the api answers 409 and the thread shows a toast), no event tells another agent's open thread that a request started, the answer of the route has `replyTo: null`, there is no rate limit across files (a holder of `MESSAGES_SEND` can request many files, each one job on a queue of concurrency 2), the source (provider id, connection id, file name, all bounded) lives in the message metadata and reaches no view, and a future export or merge that serialises `metadata` must drop `unavailableFiles.source` (guarded since the PR that added `lost-file-source-readers.spec.ts`: it fails when a file outside its list reads the entry, so a new reader is reviewed on purpose, and `tests/api/lost-file-source.spec.ts` checks that the thread and the answer of asking for a file again carry no part of the source). **Tests/web lens of PR F4c (one pass, 10 findings; ranked ones fixed: marker branches, truncation, positive and owner cases of the action, queue down):** left for Step 7.3: no web component test of the button flow, no concurrent-request test, one mutation set per lost file in the media bundle (use a `useRefetchFile` hook of its own), a 409 shows the generic `RESOURCE_CONFLICT` toast, and an unreadable list makes `mark` log and skip (the thread then shows no line for a later file of the same album). **Known limits, left on purpose (owner: Step 7.3):** a file lost before this PR has no source and cannot be asked for again (the dead-letter queue is still its only way); WhatsApp keeps a media id for about 30 days, after which the request fails again and the line stays; a request whose connection was removed does nothing and the line stays; the thread shows a toast that the download was requested but no state of its own while the job works (the line disappears when the file is stored). **Done in PR F4d of Step 3.5 (item (0), the ids of the files a visitor just sent):** `POST /widget/messages` answers `{ received: true, mediaIds }`, the ids the files of the message will have, in the order of the upload ids (a repeated id counts once, a text-only message answers `[]`). To make the id known before the worker stores the file, the media entry a plugin delivers may carry `id` (a uuid: `ChannelMedia.id`, `InboundMedia.id`, `channelEventSchema`), the inbound service passes it in `FetchInboundMediaJob.mediaId`, and `InboundMediaFetcher` creates the `Media` row with it (`inboundMediaId(messageId, index)` stays the id of every other channel); the web widget plugin gives `id` = the upload id. `ChatLog.setMediaIds` writes the ids on the files of the line when the send succeeds, so the visitor can open a file they just sent without waiting for the history; the link is the existing `POST /widget/download-url`, which answers 404 until the row exists, belongs to a message of that visitor and the scan found it clean, and the widget then shows `fileUnavailable` as it already does. No route, string or constant is new; the bundle stays at 30 KB gzip of 40. **Decisions of PR F4d:** (1) `Media.id` = the upload id, chosen by the api and not by the visitor (a random uuid of `requestUrl`, claimed once by one message), so the answer exposes nothing the visitor did not already hold; rejected: downloading by `clientMessageId` + index, which would make the route search messages by a value the visitor picks and need a second lookup path next to the id one; (2) a plugin-chosen id is validated as a uuid and, if the insert hits a unique violation, the worker accepts it only when the row belongs to the same store and message (`id`, `storeId`, `messageId`), otherwise the job fails: an id chosen by a remote plugin (Step 5.6) can never be taken for a file of another store; (3) the widget trusts the ids only when their count equals the files of the line, and an answer without them (an older api) leaves the file to open from the history as before. **Known limits of PR F4d (owner: Step 7.3):** a file lost and asked for again from the thread (`refetch`, PR F4c) is stored under the derived id, not the upload id, so a visitor's open tab keeps a link that answers 404 until the history is read again; the upload staging key is not removed once the file is stored (the orphan cleanup handles it); a visitor who sends the same `clientMessageId` again with other uploads gets `mediaIds` for files the worker never stores (the message is already known), because the claim of an upload accepts the id of the message that holds it: the widget never does it, except after `FILE_UPLOAD_FAILED`, and the fix is to record the upload set of a `clientMessageId` and refuse a different one; no e2e case opens the file in the browser between the send and the scan. **Review of PR F4d (security/correctness pass, 7 findings, under the cap):** fixed, the worker honours a chosen id only for the web widget and only as a uuid, looks for an earlier attempt by id, store and message, refuses with no retry (`refused`, no source) an id that is already a file of something else, checking before it writes the blob, and the schema refuses two files with one id; left on purpose, `mediaIds` repeats the upload ids in the order the api deduplicated them (documented, the widget sends each once) and the resend limit above. **Tests/widget lens (one pass, 8 findings, under the cap):** fixed, a message without files no longer re-renders on `setMediaIds`, empty ids are refused, the retry path, the mixed ids of the schema and the isolation of the plugin check in the fetcher spec are tested; rejected as false positives, the 404 test depending on a worker (the suite captures the queue, no worker runs); left on purpose, the widget does not line up a de-duplicated answer with repeated uploads (it never sends one id twice).
- [x] **Assignment and expired-snooze notifications (QA fix PR Q1, 2026-11-03; the QA found that assigning a conversation notified nobody, against the "Done when" below):** `NotificationsEventsListener` hears `Events.CONVERSATION_ASSIGNED` and `Events.CONVERSATION_SNOOZE_EXPIRED` (new, bridged from the worker with ids only) and sends `NotificationTypes.CONVERSATION_ASSIGNED` and `CONVERSATION_SNOOZE_EXPIRED` to the assignee. **Decisions of Q1:** (1) the assignee is read from the row when the notification is made, not from the event, and must still be an active member who can open the conversation (`conversationReaders`, the rule of the mention); (2) nobody is told of an assignment they made to themselves; the system (a rule, the round robin) does tell; (3) a snooze that ran out on a conversation with no assignee tells nobody, because nothing records who snoozed it: the unassigned queue notification belongs to QA fix PR Q2; (4) `Events.CONVERSATION_SNOOZE_EXPIRED` is published only when the reason is `snooze_expired`, so a customer who writes to a snoozed conversation does not raise it.
- [x] **The rest of the notification types get an emitter (QA fix PR Q2, 2026-11-03; the QA found that nine types of the matrix were never raised):** `Events.CONVERSATION_REOPENED` (published by `applyStatus` for a resolved or archived conversation that becomes open; a snooze waking or a pending wait ending is not a reopening) and `Events.CONVERSATION_UNASSIGNED` (published by `RoutingService.apply` when routing ends with nobody) are new and bridged with ids only; `Events.MEMBER_JOINED` (published inside the transaction of accepting an invitation) is api only. `NotificationsEventsListener` gained `onReopened` and `onMessageReceived`; the new `NotificationsInboxListener` (`notifications-inbox.listener.ts`) raises the queue, `message.failed`, `member.joined`, `invitation.accepted` and `channel.disconnected`. `NotificationsService.sendToUsersOnce` skips who still has the same notification unread. Channels stay the defaults of the table (`NotificationDefaults`); none was changed. **Decisions of Q2 (rule 12):** (1) **Unassigned queue** (`conversation.unassigned_queue`, in-app only): told when routing leaves a conversation with nobody, when a person releases a conversation, when a snooze ends on one nobody owns, and when a person reopens one nobody owns; to the members who can see unassigned conversations and take them (`CONVERSATIONS_RESOLVE` or `CONVERSATIONS_ASSIGN`, the rule of `ConversationsService.assign`), except the person who caused it, and once per conversation until they open or read it (a queue that grows is one line per conversation, never one per message). Rejected: telling every member with `VIEW`, which rings for a Viewer who can do nothing about it. (2) **Reopened** (`conversation.reopened`, in-app and push): to the owner when a person (or the owner's phone, or an agent's reply) brings back a resolved or archived conversation, except when the owner did it themselves. A reopening caused by the customer's message is not announced here: the message is the news. Rejected: announcing both, which is the same alert twice. (3) **Message received** (`message.received`, in-app and sound): only to the owner of an open conversation, only for a message of the customer, and only once until they look (ten messages in a row are one line); also skipped while the owner has the assignment, the wake or the reopening of that conversation unread, since those already say it. A conversation nobody owns is the queue's news, never a message notification to everyone. Rejected: one per message. (4) **Message failed** (`message.failed`, in-app and push): to the person who wrote the reply if they can still open the conversation, else to its owner; the state is read from the row (a reply that went out on the second try is not told), once per message until read. (5) **New member and accepted invitation** (in-app): the person who invited gets `invitation.accepted`; the members who can invite (`TEAM_INVITE`) get `member.joined`; nobody is told twice or about themselves, and nobody who is not an active member. (6) **Channel disconnected** (`channel.disconnected`, in-app, push and email): only when the health check took it down (a person who disconnects knows), to the members with `SETTINGS_MANAGE_PLUGINS`, once per connection until read, and only while the row is still in error. (7) **The reason of a reopening by a reply**: `ConversationStatusReasons.AGENT_REPLIED` (`agent_replied`) is saved on the system line when an agent's reply, a resend, or a message the owner wrote from the phone brings a conversation back; the thread draws it ("an agent replied") and its text for a reopening by the system no longer says the customer replied ("Reopened automatically"). (8) **The email digest groups** the notifications of one store with the same kind, the same `actionPath` and the same parameters into one block with a count (`items[].count`; "N notifications like this"); the limit of 20 counts blocks and `moreCount` counts the notifications left out. Two conversations stay two blocks, each with its link. (9) **Dedup window:** a notification left unread holds back a new one of its kind for the same conversation for `NotificationLimits.DEDUP_WINDOW_MS` (1 hour) at most, so a person who never clears the bell hears again at most once an hour and never goes deaf; the window counts from the first row, it does not slide. (10) The check and the write of `sendToUsersOnce` are serialized per kind and resource inside one api process (a burst of messages is one line); two api instances publishing the same kind at the same moment may send one extra line. (11) If routing itself fails, `RoutingService.announceQueue` tells the team anyway, after checking the conversation is still nobody's. **Known limits (rule 12; the review of Q2 raised them, they are decisions and not bugs):** a customer who keeps writing to a conversation nobody owns does not raise a new line once the queue line was read and the hour passed: the queue line is the news of the conversation, not of each message (rejected: a message line for every taker, which is the noise this PR removes); an owner's reply from the phone that reopens a conversation of an agent tells that agent, because the platform cannot tell which person the phone belongs to; a notification group of the email digest whose type has no text is left out as before, and the rows stay in the center; `CONVERSATION_REOPENED` is published for every reopening and each listener decides, so a new reason added later must be considered in both `onReopened` handlers. **Not done, written in the QA backlog below:** `plugin.error` has no source in v1.2.
- [ ] **Review debts of Step 3.5 that were left on purpose (rule 12; owner: Step 7.3, the hardening and alerts pass, which closes them or states why one stays):** (PR A) `sendToStore` reads the settings and the store hours once per recipient, `send` could run its reads together, the follow-store quiet-hours branch repeats `WidgetAway.isAway` (extract `isStoreClosed`), `PUT preferences` upserts one by one; (PR B1) the audit entry of an infected file is one per message, `sendToUsers` writes all recipients at once; (PR B2b) `report.infectedFilesDeleted` also counts `unscannable` rows and neither kind of row is ever purged, the "tell each person once" check of `MEDIA_INFECTED` and `MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` reads then writes (use a unique key or an upsert); (PR C1/C2) registering a push browser is not one transaction, a subscription refused for good is kept with nothing that expires it (use `lastUsed` or N failures), each retry repeats the push to every browser, a session lost by an expired refresh does not remove its registration, the VAPID keys are not required in a deployed environment (Step 8 deploy config); (PR D) the relative time of an open panel does not tick, a mark-read refetches every cached tab; (PR E) two tabs on two stores can edit the other's matrix (no store switcher on the page), switching push off removes the browser subscription even if the server call fails; (PR F1) a person who switches to immediate keeps older marks until the 48 h drop, the email does not recheck the per-type preference at send time, the hourly and daily scans run the same `distinct` query twice, no one-click unsubscribe (signed-token endpoint), the global suppression list is not consulted; (PR F2) `MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` for a file of an ad says "ask the sender to resend", which is misleading: skip that notification for origin files

**TDD:** `TDD_06 §6`–`§9`, `TDD_09 §16.3`
**Done when:** assignment triggers in-app and push notifications, quiet hours suppress push
while still persisting the notification, and an hourly digest email arrives in Mailpit.

### Step 3.6 — Settings, account and organization
Added by the frontend alignment audit (owner, 2026-10-28): the backend of these pages is built (Steps 1.1, 1.4, 2.3, 2.6, 2.7, 2.7b), and without them the inbox of this milestone cannot be used with a real channel, because nothing connects one. The pages that need later steps stay where they are (Settings → Inventory in 4.7; Carriers, Order messages, Returns and Preferences in 4.9 and 4.14; Plugins registration and widgets in 5.6 and 5.7; Webhooks, API keys, Data retention and Safety in 5.4).

- [x] The settings shell (`/s/[storeId]/settings/*`, `TDD_09 §13`, `§12`): navigation that shows only the pages the user holds permission for, and the same for the organization (`/org/*`) and the account (`/account/*`) **Done in PR B1:** `SettingsFrame` (side navigation) over three areas, fed by `lib/settings/entries.ts`, which lists only pages that exist and filters them by permission (`visibleEntries`); each later page adds its entry there and its export to `app/s/[storeId]/settings/pages.ts`. "Settings" joins the store navigation when the person may open one page. `/org` and `/s/:id/settings` open the first page the person may see. **Known limits (rule 12):** the organization area goes through one membership of the person, preferring one with `ORG_MANAGE_STORES`, then one with `SETTINGS_VIEW`; a person in several organizations sees the first (the multi-organization switcher is Phase 2); the pages of Channels, Plugins, Team, Routing, Hours, Tags, Snippets and Inbox behavior add their entries in PRs C to E
- [x] Store → General: name, timezone, currency, locale **(drawn in PR B1; the constants decision below is from PR A1).** `organizations.storeById` already updates the store; decide here whether `settings.store` (`/settings/store`, no handler) is needed for what it does not carry, or remove the constant. The same for `settings.team` (`/settings/team`, no handler), which only names the page when members, roles and invitations have their own routes **Decided in PR A1 (rule 12): both constants are removed.** `organizations.storeById` already carries every field of the page (name, timezone, currency, `defaultLocale`) and the members, roles and invitations have their own routes, so a handler for either would duplicate one; the page is a web route, not an API route. They are gone from `ApiRoutes` and from `scripts/frontend-coverage-map.ts`; the only other mention was an example in `TDD_13 §6`, which now names `organizations.storeById`
- [x] Store → Channels: the connections of the store with their status and health, connect (the form comes from what the plugin asks for, `channels.connect`), test, disconnect, and the list of templates with their status (`channels.templates`). Nothing in it is specific to one channel: it is drawn from the manifest of the plugin. The "connect a channel" action of the empty inbox (Step 3.2) opens this page. **Server side (found by the alignment audit, owner, 2026-10-29), because the view of a connection does not give the page what it needs:** (1) the form: `connect` takes `credentials` as a free record and the SDK has no manifest of the fields a plugin asks for, so add to `ChannelPlugin` a declaration of its connect fields (key, i18n label key, secret or not, required) that the catalog returns, and keep the plugin as the one that checks them; (2) for the channels that receive webhooks from a provider, the callback URL the owner pastes into the provider's dashboard, built from the public url of the api and `ApiRoutes.webhooks.channel` (the verify token of Meta stays in the environment and is **not** returned by any route: it is one value for the app, configured once, owner decision of 2026-10-29); (3) the profile status that Step 2.8 (1c-2) promised ("the connection view can tell the owner what is missing": `profileFetchError`, and the Messenger step of "Business Asset User Profile Access"); (4) the embed snippet and the origins of the web widget that `channels.widgetEmbed` and `channels.widgetOrigins` already serve **The pre-chat switch (PR D2 of Step 3.3 built the route, `channels.widgetPreChat`, and left the screen here):** a toggle on the widget connection that calls `PUT /channels/connections/:id/widget/pre-chat` and reads `preChatEnabled` from `GET .../widget`, with its words in both message files **Server side of the four points done in PR A2 (the page is still to draw, by the web PRs of this step):** (1) `ChannelPlugin.connectFields` (`ConnectField`: `key`, `labelKey`, `secret`, `required`, `multiple`) and `receivesProviderWebhooks`, declared by the four official plugins and returned by `GET /plugins/available` as `channel.connectFields`; the registry copies and checks them when a plugin registers (unique keys, identifier keys, a label that is a message key and never text, booleans), so a third party cannot hand the page anything else, and the plugin still validates the values in `connect`. The label keys are `ChannelConnectFieldKeys` in `libs/core/domain/channel-connect-constants.ts`, generated into `validate:translations`, with their text in `apps/web/messages/{en,es}.json` under `settings.channels`. (2) `channel.callbackUrl` (`API_URL` + `API_PREFIX` + `ApiRoutes.webhooks.channel(id)`, from `PublicApiPlatformModule` in `core-http`) for the channels a provider calls, and `null` for the widget; the verify token of Meta and every other value of the environment are in no answer (a spec searches the whole catalog for them). (3) `profile` on the view of a connection (`GET /channels/connections` and `/:id`, and the answers of connect and disconnect): `failedIdentities`, `errors` (the stored `profileFetchError` codes with their count of customers, each customer once, only those who wrote through this connection) and `setupKey` (Messenger: `ChannelSetupStepKeys.MESSENGER_PROFILE_ACCESS`, the "Business Asset User Profile Access" step), `null` for a channel without `CUSTOMER_PROFILE`; the directory of the inbox does not carry it. (4) `connectSrc` in the answers of `GET`/`PUT .../widget*`: the origins a page with a strict `connect-src` must list (api, its websocket, and the bucket host taken from a signed url and cut to its origin, so path style, virtual host and AWS all come out right). **Left for the web PRs (rule 12, owner: the PR that draws Store → Channels):** the page writes the notice of Step 3.4 (2) from `connectSrc` (the text is UI); shows `setupKey` whenever `failedIdentities > 0` (the server cannot tell the Messenger permission apart from other refusals: Meta's permission error is stored as `CHANNEL_CREDENTIALS_INVALID`, which the job treats as an outage); and builds its form only from `connectFields`, with a `multiple` field as one entry per line. **Known limits of PR A2 (rule 12):** (a) `labelKey` must be a message key of the web app's own files, so a private or remote plugin cannot bring a label of its own: owner Step 5.6 (registration by manifest), which adds a plain-text label per locale as a fallback; (b) a profile error stays on the identity until the job reads that customer again, so after the owner fixes the token the counts and `setupKey` stay until customers write again: the page should offer no "fixed" state, and a reset on reconnect was **done in PR F1**; (c) the count query filters `customer_identities` by `store_id` and a non-null error with no partial index: fine for the MVP's single owner, a partial index `(store_id) WHERE profile_fetch_error IS NOT NULL` (migration with rollback) when a store has hundreds of thousands of identities, owner: Step 7.4 (Monitoring), when that count shows up among the slow queries; (d) `PublicApiConfig` and `WidgetEmbedConfig.apiUrl` both read `API_URL`: the widget lib should take `PublicApiConfig` when it is next touched, owner: Step 7.3 (public URLs of the deployment); (e) the bucket host is read once per process from a signed url, so a changed endpoint needs a restart, like every change of an environment variable, and that is the accepted behavior (closed in PR G, nothing to build); if the bucket cannot be signed, `connectSrc` omits it instead of failing the save and logs a warning (**test added in PR G:** `widget-settings.service.spec.ts`) **Done in PR C:** `Settings → Channels` (`StoreChannelsPage`, `SETTINGS_VIEW` to open, `SETTINGS_EDIT` for every action). Each live account is a card with its status, when it last worked and why it does not (the platform's `ChannelHealthErrors` codes are translated, any other text is the provider's and is drawn as plain text), the profile warning (`failedIdentities`, the translated codes and the `setupKey` text, only while some read fails), the callback URL with a copy button for the channels that receive webhooks, **Test** (the verdict is shown and the list is read again) and **Disconnect** (`DISCONNECT_CHANNEL`, the high tier: a dialog; the api empties the credentials). "Add a channel" lists the installed channel plugins and opens the connect dialog, drawn from `channel.connectFields` (a secret in a masked field, a list as one entry per line, nothing about a channel by name; the mutation has `gcTime: 0` so the cache does not keep the typed secrets, and the dialog unmounts its state on close). Disconnected accounts go to a "Disconnected" list with no actions. The templates of an account (`channels.templates`, with the status of each) open from the card for who holds `CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` and `MESSAGES_SEND_TEMPLATE` (the route's own permissions) and only for a channel that declares `templates`. **Web widget card:** the snippet with copy, the sites (`channels.widgetOrigins`, up to `WidgetLimits.MAX_ORIGINS`), the pre-chat switch (`channels.widgetPreChat`, read from `preChatEnabled`) and the `connectSrc` notice with the origins to allow. The empty inbox of a store with no live channel shows **Connect a channel**, which opens this page (`ConnectChannelLink`, lazy-loaded because the inbox has a script ceiling). The requests and hooks live in `apps/web/lib/settings/channels-requests.ts`, `use-channels.ts` and `channel-keys.ts`, not in `@platform/api-client`, so the inbox chunk does not carry them (measured: the inbox chunks are 0.2 KB lighter than `main`). **Left open (rule 12):** (a) a `labelKey` the page has no text for (a third-party plugin) shows the key of the field: its text is Step 5.6; (b) the stale profile errors after a token fix: **done in PR F1** (connecting the account again clears them, see the permissions box); (c) the `lastError` of a connection is the provider's text, drawn as plain text and wrapped. It is cut and cleaned before it is stored, not by the page: `providerErrorText` (`libs/channels/connections/src/provider-text.ts`) keeps one line of at most `ChannelHealthLimits.ERROR_MAX_LENGTH` characters without control or invisible characters and, **done in PR G,** replaces a credential the provider repeated (`Bearer ...`, an `Authorization` value of any scheme, a value after `access_token`, `api_key`, `password` and the like, a bare `token=`/`secret=` or a `token:`/`secret:` followed by a long value with a digit, a JWT, a Meta token, also inside a JSON body quoted in a string) with `[redacted]`. The two places that write `lastError` use it (the health job and the status receipts of the worker; `ChannelConnectionsService` only clears it). `providerText`, which also cleans names of plugins, connections and templates, does not redact. **Known limits (accepted, rule 12; the redaction is a net under the provider's text, not a parser):** an unquoted value that holds a space (`password=correct horse`) is cut at the space; a short value after a bare `token:` or `secret:` (`{"token":"abc123"}`) is kept, because prose follows those words too; a credential under a name the lists do not know is kept. A rule that went further would erase ordinary messages, and the real protection is that a plugin's error is cut to `ERROR_MAX_LENGTH` and shown only to who manages channels. No later step owns it; (d) review backlog of PR C, owners moved in PR F1: the e2e spec of Channels left the widget installed and did not delete its throwaway store in a `finally` **(closed in PR F2: the widget is uninstalled through the page and, in a `finally`, through the api; the throwaway store is deleted in a `finally` when the case stops half way)**; more unit cases (a 403/404, a malformed catalog, the order of the three store entries for an administrator), the uninstall dialog that counts the accounts it read when it opened, and the unsaved sites text lost if the widget section is collapsed (Step 7.7, with the DOM test environment and the polish pass); (e) no component tests: the web app has no component-test harness, so the pages are covered by `apps/web-e2e/specs/channels.spec.ts` and by the unit specs of the requests and the form rules
- [x] Store → Plugins: the catalog of installable plugins (`plugins.available`), install and uninstall per store (D19). The registration of a remote plugin by manifest stays in Step 5.6 **Done in PR C:** `Settings → Plugins` (`StorePluginsPage`, `SETTINGS_VIEW` to open, `SETTINGS_MANAGE_PLUGINS` to act) lists the catalog with who made it, whether it is installed and how many accounts are connected. **Install** is one click; **Uninstall** asks first, through `DISCONNECT_CHANNEL` (the high tier, because the api disconnects every account of the plugin; the dialog says how many), and the page reads the catalog and the connections again also after a failure, because a half-finished uninstall is finished by running it again. A plugin that is not a registered channel shows "Not available yet" instead of a button (the api refuses to install it until Step 5.6). **Decision:** uninstall has no tier of its own in `DestructiveActionTiers` and `REVOKE_ALL_PLUGIN_ACCESS` (critical) is about revoking every key of a plugin, which this is not, so the disconnect tier is the honest one
- [x] Store → Team: members (role, remove), custom roles with the permission matrix, and invitations (pending, resend, revoke); the invitation accept page is Step 3.1. There is no route to resend an invitation (`POST` invites, `GET` lists, `DELETE` revokes): add `POST /settings/team/invitations/:id/resend` with `TEAM_INVITE`, which issues a new token, invalidates the old one and sends the email again, rate limited, audited, with its isolation case **Server side done in PR A1:** `ApiRoutes.settings.invitationResend`, `InvitationsService.resend` (finds only a pending invitation of the store, so one that was revoked, accepted or belongs to another store is a 404; then issues through the same path as inviting, so it keeps the cooldown of `INVITATION_RESEND_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` (429), the check that the caller holds the role's permissions, and the hash-only token; the answer is the new invitation, with a new id), `AuditAction.INVITATION_RESENT`, and the case `invitationResend` in `tenant-isolation.spec` **Done in PR D:** `Settings → Team` (`StoreTeamPage`, `TEAM_VIEW` to open; entry in `store-entries.ts`, and `mayOpenStoreSettings` now also counts `TEAM_VIEW`) with three tabs over lists loaded together. **Members:** role select (`TEAM_MANAGE_ROLES`) and Remove (`TEAM_REMOVE`, `REMOVE_TEAM_MEMBER`, high tier). **Roles:** system roles shown read-only with a lock, custom roles created, edited and deleted (`TEAM_MANAGE_ROLES`) through a matrix of the 73 permissions grouped by the area before the colon (`permission-groups.ts`, labels `settings.team.permissions.<area>.<action>`). A permission the person lacks is shown and cannot be ticked, a role that holds one they lack is read-only for them, and the role select and the invite dialog offer only roles they can hand out (`canGrant`): the api enforces the same rule, the interface only spares the refusal. **Invitations:** pending list with expiry, Invite, Resend (`POST .../resend` of PR A1; a 429 shows the wait from `retryAfterSeconds`) and Revoke. Two tiers join `DestructiveActionTiers`: `DELETE_CUSTOM_ROLE` (high) and `REVOKE_INVITATION` (moderate), because no existing tier named them honestly. A delete refused as `RESOURCE_CONFLICT` reads "still held by a member or an invitation" (the generic text of that code says "someone changed this"). No server route was needed. **Suspend and reactivate a member (owner, 2026-11-03: it enters the MVP, because the first team is three to four people; this replaces the "left out" decision of PR D): built in PRs H1 (server) and H2 (web) of this step, below.** **Debts:** no component-test harness, so the page is covered by `team.spec.ts` (e2e) and the pure files; removing yourself, or changing your own role, is allowed (the last Admin is still protected by the api) and not warned about, and your own permissions in the page stay those of the session until it reloads (the memberships come with the account, Milestone 7 or the step that refreshes them); a member list has no search or paging (a store has few members)
- [x] **PR H1 (server): suspend and reactivate members.** `MembershipStatus.SUSPENDED` (migration with rollback; mind `ADD VALUE` and transactions in Postgres). Routes to suspend and reactivate with a team permission and `canGrant` (nobody suspends a person who holds permissions they lack); nobody suspends themselves; the last active Admin cannot be suspended (`requireAnotherAdmin` already counts only `ACTIVE`: verify). Effect at once: `TenantGuard` and the tenant context refuse a suspended member with a code of its own in `ErrorCodes`, the cached permissions are dropped (keys in `libs/core`), the person's sockets of that store close and a join is refused, and no push, digest email or mention reaches them; routing never assigns to them and their open conversations are left unassigned, back in the queue with their history (decided in PR G, rule 12; H1 may change it with a reason written here); they are not offered as an assignee; their other stores are untouched; inviting a suspended person answers with a clear error (reactivate instead). `AuditAction.MEMBER_SUSPENDED` and `MEMBER_REACTIVATED`; a case in `tests/security/tenant-isolation.spec.ts`; a test for each of the fifteen non-test files that read `MembershipStatus` (organizations, account, tenant-context, members, invitations of the team and of the invitations module, routing rules, notifications listener and service, the worker's email digest, bootstrap-owner, `status-enums`, conversations, conversation readers, the messaging routing) and the raw SQL of `members.service`, `invitations.service` and `routing.service`; a mutation (removing the `SUSPENDED` filter in routing or in the guard must fail a test). Security review at high, repeated until it returns fewer than 10 findings **Done in PR H1 (#161):** (1) **No migration, so no rollback:** `memberships.status` is plain text with no check constraint and no Postgres enum, so `SUSPENDED` is a value of `MembershipStatus` only (the `ADD VALUE` worry does not apply); code rolled back past this PR reads a suspended row as "not active", which is closed. (2) `POST /settings/team/members/:id/suspend` and `.../reactivate` (`ApiRoutes.settings.memberSuspend`, `memberReactivate`, `TEAM_REMOVE` for both: no permission of its own, because a new one would reach every role matrix and the web's 73-permission grid for no gain, and both decide who may enter the store), under the store write lock, audited as `MEMBER_SUSPENDED` and `MEMBER_REACTIVATED`. Suspending: nobody suspends themselves (403), the role's permissions must be held by the caller (`canGrant`), an already suspended member is a 409, the last Admin that can enter cannot be suspended (`requireAnotherAdmin` counts only `ACTIVE`, so a suspended Admin does not count: tested), the membership keeps its role, its row and its history (`revokedAt` stays null), and their conversations in the store that are still being worked (open, pending, snoozed) go back to the queue unassigned (the decision of PR G stands; the resolved, archived and spam ones keep who handled them, because suspending is reversible). Reactivating returns the role they had (the role of a suspended member cannot be changed meanwhile, 409, and a role held by one cannot be deleted), under the same `canGrant` rule. Removing a suspended member works. (3) **Effect at once:** `TenantContextService.access()` tells `granted`, `suspended` and `none` apart (the cache entry is one of them; `resolve()` still answers only a grant, so a caller that did not change fails closed); `TenantGuard` answers 403 `TENANT_MEMBER_SUSPENDED` and audits the denial with `reason: 'suspended'`, a stranger still gets `TENANT_ACCESS_DENIED`; the member's cache entry is dropped after the commit, so an access token still valid is refused on the next request. (4) **Sockets:** the handshake and every recheck or join refuse a suspended member with the same code, and suspending closes their sockets of that store on every instance at once (`RealtimeService.closeStoreUser`, the room `storeUser`), sparing their sockets of other stores. (5) Everything that read `ACTIVE` already excludes `SUSPENDED` (organizations, routing rules, routing, conversation readers and assignees, mentions, the notification service, the digest, the invitations), each now with a test of its own; two readers needed code: the team's `isMember` (inviting a suspended address is a 409 `TENANT_INVITEE_SUSPENDED`, "reactivate instead", and accepting an invitation as a suspended person is a 409 `TENANT_MEMBER_SUSPENDED`) and the accept of an invitation (`join` refused a suspended person, because it would have undone the suspension and could have changed their role silently). The web push worker now checks that the person is an active member of the notification's store before it pushes (a browser belongs to the person, not the store; this also covers a removed member). `ensureMembership` of the bootstrap leaves a suspended owner suspended. (6) `GET /auth/me` returns `suspendedStores` (`storeId`, `storeName`, no role or permission) apart from `memberships`, so nothing that picks "a store" or "the last store" can land on one; `GET /settings/team/members` lists active and suspended members with `status`, and the directory (who can be given work) stays active only. **Known limits (rule 12):** (a) the other agents' inbox lists do not hear that the conversations were unassigned (removing a member does the same): they see it on their next read; a realtime event for it belongs to Step 7.7 (the leftovers of the settings and the polish pass), and nothing before it needs one; (b) `NotificationsService.send` does not check membership itself, its callers do (all of them filter `ACTIVE`, and the push worker re-checks), so a new caller must keep that rule; (c) the words of the page (badge, confirmation) and the screens for `suspendedStores` are H2.
- [x] **PR H2 (web): suspend and reactivate in Settings → Team.** **Owed by PR H1 (server):** (1) the realtime client (`apps/web/lib/realtime/connection.ts`) must treat `TENANT_MEMBER_SUSPENDED` as it treats `TENANT_ACCESS_DENIED`, a refusal that ends the session of that store and does not retry (until then a suspended person's open tab reconnects with backoff); (1b) that handling belongs to the store's requests and sockets only: `TENANT_MEMBER_SUSPENDED` also comes back from accepting an invitation as a suspended person, where it must read as a message on that page and not end a session; (2) read `suspendedStores` of `GET /auth/me` (the `accountProfileSchema` already carries it, empty by default) and tell the person "your access to this store is suspended" instead of a generic 403, never offering such a store as one to enter or as the last store; (3) the messages `TENANT_MEMBER_SUSPENDED` (the person) and `TENANT_INVITEE_SUSPENDED` (the admin who invites a suspended address) already exist in both languages. A status badge, Suspend and Reactivate with a confirmation and only for who holds the permission, a filter by status, the empty and error states, both languages, and an e2e (a suspended person cannot enter, a reactivated one returns) **Done in PR H2 (#162):** (1) **Members tab:** a status badge (Active, Suspended) on each row, Suspend and Reactivate for who holds `TEAM_REMOVE` and every permission of the member's role (`canGrant`, the same rule as Remove), never on oneself, each behind a confirmation; a filter by status (everyone, active, suspended) with its own empty state for each; the role select is not offered for a suspended member (the api refuses it). The existing Remove button had a stray `//` that rendered as text before the button; it is a real comment now. Two tiers join `DestructiveActionTiers` because none described the action honestly: `SUSPEND_TEAM_MEMBER` (high: it cuts the person's access at once and puts their open conversations back in the queue) and `REACTIVATE_TEAM_MEMBER` (moderate: it returns what suspending took, with the role they had). A conflict (the list was stale) reads as such and the list is read again; every other refusal of H1 (yourself, the last administrator, a role the caller lacks) shows the generic text of its code, and the buttons spare the first and the third before asking. Inviting a suspended address already read "reactivate them instead" (`errors.TENANT_INVITEE_SUSPENDED`, from H1); the e2e proves it. (2) **Realtime client:** `TENANT_MEMBER_SUSPENDED` ends the session of that store like `TENANT_ACCESS_DENIED`, from the refused handshake (an open session is hung up by the api, reconnects once, is refused and stops), and `connection.endedBy()` says why; the provider then tells the person (a toast) and goes to "/". Only the sockets of the store are touched: the accept-invitation page has no socket, and its 409 is a message on that page as before. (3) **`suspendedStores`:** the store switcher lists them disabled with "Your access is suspended"; opening one by its link (or as the store used last) shows the notice and goes to a store the person can enter, or to `/no-access`, which now says that the access is suspended and names the stores, and offers "Check again" (it drops the cached profile first, so a reactivation is seen) and Sign out; "/" never targets a suspended store, so there is no loop. (4) **e2e** (`team.spec.ts`): a new member is suspended by the administrator, their open tab is cut and shows the suspension, opening the store by its link does the same, the filter finds them, inviting them says to reactivate, and reactivating returns them to the inbox with their role. **Known limits (rule 12):** (a) a tab of the suspended person whose socket was down meets the 403 `TENANT_MEMBER_SUSPENDED` on its next request and shows its text on that page, without going to "/" (the socket hang-up is what normally moves it at once); a handler for it in the api client belongs to Step 7.7 with the other leftovers; (b) the toast on a suspension does not name the store (the socket knows only its id); (c) the members panel has no component test (no harness, as for the rest of Team): `team.spec.ts`, `member-filter.spec.ts`, `connection.spec.ts` and `team-requests.spec.ts` cover it; (d) the list of the notification panel and the suspended-store redirect load lazily, to keep the inbox under its 340 KB script limit (the limit is not raised; measured with the scripts the inbox fetches: main 336.1, this PR 335.4 KB locally). Making the confirmation dialog lazy was tried and added 3.6 KB, so it stays eager; (e) the lighthouse job (340 KB limit of the inbox) is read in the PR's CI, because a worker left over from another session held its port locally

## Milestone 4 — Commerce

Inventory is robust from the first release (`TDD_16_Inventory.md`): warehouses that serve
several stores, a movement ledger, reservations, transfers between warehouses and physical
counts. Orders depend on it, so it is built first.

- [ ] **Before Milestone 4 starts (QA round 2, 2026-11-06, rule 12):** run the full round of the case matrix (416 cases, `qa-reportes-2026-11-05/matriz-casos.md`) with `/qa-team`. Round 2 covered what was built up to #190 and the connection UX; Milestone 4 adds orders and inventory on top, so the matrix is run complete first, and the P1/P2 it finds are fixed before the first Step of this milestone.

### Step 4.1 — Products and variants
- [ ] Products + variants CRUD (store-scoped; `freeShipping` flag, `TDD_17 §4.13`; `trackInventory`; `lowStockThreshold` is optional, empty means no low-stock alert, `TDD_16 §4.4`)
- [ ] Product search with sub-200ms autocomplete
- [ ] **To design with the owner when this step starts (nothing is decided, and `TDD_09` has no product screen):** the catalog interface. The order drawer of Step 4.4 only picks variants, so someone has to create them: a products page (list, search, create and edit a product with its variants, images through Step 3.4, stock summary from Step 4.2). Decide its scope here, in this step, and add it as tasks before building
- [ ] **Upload purpose `product` (left by Step 3.4):** add it to `ClientUploadPurposes` together with the check that the `parentId` is a product of the store (`TDD_07 §6.3`); a purpose is never added before the code that checks who may attach it

**TDD:** `TDD_01 §4`, `TDD_08 §5.4`

returns nothing.

retried with the same idempotency key applies once, stock never goes negative while

the isolation suite covers the new resources.

### Step 4.3 — Orders

The carrier rules start to apply in Step 4.8, when connections exist

- [ ] Inventory through `InventoryService` only: allocate by warehouse priority on creation (one warehouse, or several when `allowSplitFulfillment` is on) into `OrderItemAllocation`, and issue on `SHIPPED`, release on cancel and optional restock on return, all per allocation (`TDD_16 §4.1`)

Fill them from the orders of the customer in one grouped read per page, as the conversation dates are, and cover them in `tests/api/exports.spec.ts`

confirm marks matches as remitted and differences as discrepancies, two overlapping confirms end with one, a void restores the
statuses, and an invalid cell is reported instead of read as zero.
