Make the search of customers (and the inbox search that shares the service) accent-insensitive, with a test for names with accents next to the `%` and cross-tenant cases

The "Search analytics" task of this step queues `TRACK_SEARCH`, so it also teaches the processor that job, with its spec (the unique key on events is Step 5.2's)

**TDD:** `TDD_08 §5`
**Done when:** global search returns results under 300ms and leaks nothing across tenants.

### Step 5.2 — Analytics
- [ ] Rollup workers, hourly and daily (2-period recompute)
- [ ] Human and automated response (owner, 2026-10-29): the first response time reads `firstResponseAt` only (a person); `firstAutomatedResponseAt` is reported apart. A conversation is in one of three states for the reports: **waiting for a person** (no human reply and not resolved, even if a plugin answered), **answered by automation** (a plugin answered and no person did) and **resolved by automation** (a plugin resolved it with no human reply). The first two can both be true of one conversation. A response SLA or the pending queue changes only by an explicit resolve, never because a plugin answered
- [ ] Metrics query service
- [ ] Dashboard UI, and the My performance widget of the inbox sidebar (`TDD_09 §8`: resolved today, open, average response time), read from the metrics service and added to the widgets that Step 3.2 builds
- [ ] The View analytics action of the Quick actions widget (left by PR B6a of Step 3.2; Find customer in Step 3.3 and Create order in Step 4.4 join the same widget): a link to `AppPaths.store.analytics`, shown with the permission that opens the dashboard
- [ ] Scheduled reports (PDF/CSV/Excel), and `GET /analytics/export` (`analytics.export`) as an export of the exports center
- [ ] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), the rollups in the `analytics` processor (left by Q4, (c)):** the processor writes only `TRACK_EVENT`; the "Rollup workers" task above adds `ROLLUP_HOURLY` and `ROLLUP_DAILY` to it (it refuses any job it does not know). Also add a unique key on the event, so that a job redelivered after a stalled worker cannot write twice (the producer uses the Svix event id as job id and the queue allows one attempt, so a webhook delivered twice already queues one job). `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` does not defer the rollups: they are built here

**TDD:** `TDD_11 §7`, `TDD_08 §6`
**Done when:** the dashboard loads under 1s reading only rollup tables.

### Step 5.6 — Integration plugins (runtime)

In the v1.2 MVP the platform runs third-party and private integration plugins (for example
the owner's own POS or inventory app, or a Shopify connector later). The marketplace, its
review queue and revenue sharing stay deferred (`TDD_10 §8`). Builds on Step 5.4 (webhook
delivery, SSRF protection, key hashing) and Step 1.0 (queues).
- [ ] **Private registration** instead of a marketplace: the owner registers a plugin by pasting its manifest in Settings → Plugins; it is validated by `pluginManifestSchema` and stored as a `Plugin` row (`status = approved`, `isOfficial = false`, `pricingModel = free`; `paid` and `plan_gated` are rejected) (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7, D10)
- [ ] Q6: **`POST /plugin-api/conversations`** (scope `conversations:write`, which `plugin-scopes.ts` maps to `CONVERSATIONS_CREATE`): the first route that starts a conversation, so the first reader of that permission; it removes it from `ReservedPermissions`. A person starting a conversation from the inbox is not planned, and needs the template and window design first.
- [ ] Channel plugins by manifest (`type: "channel"`), the whole of `TDD_10 §6.7`, so that a channel can be added without touching this code: the migration (`commandSecret`, `commandSecretPrevious`, `commandSecretRotatedAt`, `Plugin.manifest`, `Plugin.iconKey`, with rollback), the manifest `channel` block and its validator (ids against `ChannelIds` and the table, the capabilities, the window bounds), the icon fetched once and stored as a 128 px PNG, the registry refresh and `list(storeId)`, `RemoteChannelPlugin` (all its methods, §6.7.4), signed commands with the secret rotation, `PUT/DELETE /api/v1/plugin-api/channel/connections/:connectionId` and `POST /api/v1/plugin-api/channel/events` (scopes `channel:connections`, `channel:events`, the caps and the rate ceilings), the hooks that send `typing.send` (from the agent typing events of Step 2.4) and `message.markRead` (from the read state), inbound media by URL with pinned addresses, the health job per connection with its counter, and `tests/contracts/remote-channel.contract.ts` with the reference plugin
- [ ] Manifest, scopes and scope-to-permission mapping (`PluginScopes`, `PluginScopePermissions`, including the inventory scopes of `TDD_16 §3`)
- [ ] Install flow: consent screen, webhook-endpoint challenge with `assertPublicHttpsUrl`, `PluginInstallation` in `PENDING_SETUP`, scoped API key stored as prefix and sha256 hash only, one-time setup code (5 minutes, Redis) exchanged for the key, then `ACTIVE`
- [ ] `PluginApiKeyGuard` on `/api/v1/plugin-api/*`: scope check, per-installation rate limit, bound to exactly one store; thin controllers over the same services for conversations, messages, customers, orders, products and inventory
- [ ] **Messages from a plugin, with the author and the quiet mode chosen by the plugin (owner, 2026-10-29; Crisp's plugin API lets the plugin choose how a message looks):** `POST /plugin-api/messages` (`messages:write`) sends a reply or an internal note to a conversation. It takes: `type` (`text`, `note` or a file type with the media of Step 3.4: uploaded through the API, scanned, and sent with its text as the caption; a note carries no files in the MVP); `authorType`, `system` (the default) or `agent`; `userId`, required with `agent` (an active member of the store; the message is theirs, with their own name and avatar); `quiet` (default false); an optional `nickname` (cut to a length, control characters removed; the plugin's name by default) and an optional avatar (never a remote url: the icon of the plugin, and the plugin's own image once Step 3.4 stores it), both only for `system`. **The author and `quiet` are separate choices** (`authorType` is its own column), so a plugin can send as `system` quietly, as `system` and raise the chat, or as an agent in any of the two ways. **Author:** `system` never counts as a human answer: `recordReply` does not set `firstResponseAt` for it (that date feeds the first response time of `TDD_11 §7.2`, and an automatic answer would make it look instant) and it sets `Conversation.firstAutomatedResponseAt` instead (new nullable column, once; migration with its rollback script), so that automation is measured on its own and never hides how long the team takes. `agent` counts as that person's answer, as if they had written it. Answering does not take a conversation out of the pending queue or stop a response SLA: only an explicit resolve does, and a plugin with `conversations:write` can resolve its own. **`quiet`** (core: `CreateMessageBody.quiet`, `MessagesService.create` and `ConversationsService.recordReply`): the customer gets the message and the open thread and the preview of the list show it in real time, but the conversation does not change at all: `lastMessageAt` stays (the order of the list stays), it is not reopened, and `lastOutboundAt`, `firstResponseAt` and `firstAutomatedResponseAt` are not touched. Without `quiet` a reply moves and reopens the conversation like an agent's. A note changes nothing in the conversation, as today. `quiet` is for plugins only in the MVP (no checkbox for agents) and the send is subject to the messaging window of the channel like any other. **Trace:** two new columns of `Message`, in one migration with its rollback script: `authorPluginId` (the plugin that wrote it, always, whichever the author) and `authorNickname`; `MessageView` returns both, and the "automatic" mark and "sent through <plugin>" of Step 3.2 are drawn here, once a message can carry them (decided 2026-10-30). Every plugin message is audited with the plugin as the actor. There is no `from: user`: a plugin cannot write as the customer. Tests: a quiet reply leaves `lastMessageAt`, status, `lastOutboundAt`, `firstResponseAt` and `firstAutomatedResponseAt` as they were; a `system` reply never sets `firstResponseAt`; an `agent` reply does; `agent` with a user of another store or with no `userId` is refused; a plugin without `messages:write` gets 403; isolation case
- [ ] Outbound plugin webhooks (`TRIGGER_PLUGIN_WEBHOOK`): signed, payload redacted to the granted scopes, same delivery log and auto-disable as tenant webhooks
- [ ] Revocation with typed confirmation, final `plugin.revoked` webhook, audit
- [ ] The shipment routes of `TDD_17 §4.1` under `PluginApiKeyGuard` (`shipments:read`, `shipments:write`), over the same `ShippingService` as the UI
- [ ] Settings → Plugins UI: register, list installations with their scopes, revoke (`SETTINGS_MANAGE_PLUGINS`). `plugins.list`, `byId`, `enable`, `disable` and `config` have no handler and no task: build them here with their screens, or remove the constants if the install and uninstall of Step 2.7b made them redundant
- [ ] Text for the connection fields of a third-party channel plugin (debt of PR A2 (a) and PR C (a) of Step 3.6): `labelKey` must today be a key of the web app's own messages, so a plugin that is not official shows the key; the manifest brings a plain-text label per locale, the registry checks it (length, no markup) and `Settings → Channels` draws it when the web has no message for the key
- [ ] The routing rule editor offers the channels of the installed channel plugins (debt of PR E1 (4) of Step 3.6; PR F1 lists the channels of `GET /channels/directory`, so this is checked with a remote channel plugin and an e2e case)
- [ ] Tests: a plugin key for store A cannot read store B, scope escalation is denied, the key never appears in logs or in any response after setup, webhook signature; `PluginInstallation` in the isolation suite
- [ ] Registration by pasted JSON only (importing a manifest by URL is a later improvement, `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §6). Installed scopes are fixed at install: when a registered plugin's manifest later asks for more scopes, the existing installation keeps its old scopes until the owner approves the new ones in a new consent screen
- [ ] **Show the notification switch of this step (QA fix PR Q7):** a plugin that fails while it runs raises `plugin.error` (`NotificationService.notify`, with its `messageKey` texts in `es` and `en`); remove `NotificationTypes.PLUGIN_ERROR` from `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` (`libs/core/domain/notifications/notification-constants.ts`) in the same PR, and add the case to the unit spec of that constant
- [ ] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), names of plugins that are not official (Q7a, (e)):** only the official channel plugins are named from the message files (`settings.routing.channelNames`); any other plugin keeps the name its author gave it. Private registration is what creates the first plugin that is not official: either the manifest it validates carries the name per language (checked like the field labels above), or the interface says plainly that the name is the author's

**TDD:** `TDD_10 §6`, `§9`, `TDD_05 §9`
**Done when:** a sample plugin (a test fixture app) is registered and installed, calls the API
inside its scopes and gets 403 outside them, receives a redacted signed webhook, and after
revocation its key stops working immediately; and a channel plugin of the suite (the reference plugin of `tests/contracts/remote-channel.contract.ts`) appears in the inbox with its own name and icon, a message it posts reaches the inbox, an agent's reply reaches it as a signed command and its status comes back, and a status event for a message of another channel is dropped.

## Milestone 7 — Production readiness

### Step 7.1 — Security hardening
- [ ] Helmet on the API, CSP in Next with per-store plugin origins
- [ ] Rate limiting: application (Redis), edge (Cloudflare rules)
- [ ] **Row-Level Security** (`TDD_01 §3.2` layer 3; owner decision D16, `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7):
  - The roles `platform_app` (subject to RLS, used by `apps/api`) and `platform_worker` (`BYPASSRLS`, used by `apps/worker`, which also processes every inbound channel event) already exist since Step 0.4, and the services already connect with them; this step only turns the policies on
  - `scripts/rls.sql`, generated from the schema, with `ENABLE` + `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and one policy per table: tables with `store_id` use `app.store_ids`; the organization-scoped inventory tables (`warehouses`, `stock_transfers`, `stock_counts`) use `app.organization_ids`; `store_warehouses` uses its `store_id`; child tables (messages, order items, transfer and count lines, allocations, movements) follow their parent. The `audit_logs` grants already exist since Step 0.4
  - Identity and bootstrap tables are NOT under RLS, because the guards read them before any tenant context exists: `users`, `sessions`, `verification_tokens`, `invitations`, `organizations`, `stores`, `memberships` and `roles`. They stay protected by code (user id or token) and by the isolation suite
  - **The mechanism is already chosen and was verified by a spike** (2026-10-24, `TDD_01 §3.2`): one interactive transaction per unit of work with `set_config(..., true)` as its first statement, kept in an `AsyncLocalStorage`, with `PrismaService.client` returning that transaction and `PrismaService.transaction(fn)` joining it. The per-operation extension was rejected because it breaks atomicity. This step implements it and re-runs the same checks as tests: atomic rollback, no cross-tenant leak under concurrency through PgBouncer, raw SQL (`SearchService`), parallel queries on one transaction, and row locks (`TDD_16 §4.2`)
  - **Tenant interceptor:** after `TenantGuard`, a `TenantContextInterceptor` runs the whole handler inside `PrismaService.tenantTransaction(ctx, fn)`, which sets `app.store_ids` and `app.organization_ids` with `set_config(..., true)` (never the session-level form; a lint rule forbids `false`) and applies explicit `maxWait` and `timeout` limits. The `await` of every query stays inside the scope, and **no external I/O runs inside it** (carrier, channel, S3 and e-mail calls are queued and run in the worker). `TenantGuard` fills the context from the caller's memberships; plugin API requests set only the installation's store; widget requests set only the store of the site key's connection; requests with no context see no rows. Streaming endpoints and the WebSocket gateway use an explicit `TenantContext` instead of the interceptor
  - **API code that runs outside a tenant request fails loudly instead of returning empty results:** the WebSocket gateway, domain-event listeners fed by the events bridge (notifications, plugin webhooks), and the public endpoints must either run inside an explicit `TenantContext.run({ storeIds, organizationIds }, fn)` taken from the event payload or the verified credential, or hand the work to the worker. In the `api` process a query on a store-scoped table with no context throws in dev, test and CI
  - **Pre-context lookups** (widget site key to store, plugin API key hash to installation, tenant API key hash to store, a carrier webhook's connection id to its store) use small `SECURITY DEFINER` SQL functions that `platform_app` may call and that return only the store id. A globally unique value that belongs to another store (a `ChannelConnection` `(pluginId, connectionId)`) is detected from the unique violation, never by reading the other store's row
  - Add the policies for the Step 4.2 to 4.12 tables here; the isolation suite and the integration, security and e2e CI jobs run as `platform_app` from this step on, and a test proves that a query without `storeId` still returns nothing from another store
- [ ] **Outbox for the invitation email (review backlog of PR A1, Step 3.6):** inviting and resending revoke or replace the old invitation before the email is queued, so a queue that is down leaves the invitee with no live link until the cooldown passes. Write the email in the same transaction as the invitation (an outbox row the worker sends) on both routes, and test that a failed queue leaves the old invitation alive
- [ ] **One helper for the sweeps of the orphan cleaner (review backlog of PR A1, Step 3.6):** `deleteStrayAvatarUploads` copies the paging loop of `deleteStrayObjects`; extract one helper (root, cursor key, classifier) when a third sweep is added, or here if none was
- [x] **Dependency audit gate (dependencies lane, PR A, 2026-11-07):** `lint-and-types` runs `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level=high`. It audits the `dependencies` of every workspace package (api, worker, web and widget alike, not only what an image carries), never the `devDependencies`. A new high or critical advisory fails every pull request until it is fixed: on purpose, and the same severity bar as Trivy. Unlike Trivy it does not skip advisories without a fix (`pnpm audit` has no such option, and a registry outage also turns the step red: rerun it; the step has a 3 minute limit); one that has no fix goes to `auditConfig.ignoreGhsas` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, one entry per GHSA with the reason written beside it, and is listed here. `zod` of `@serwist/build` and `@serwist/turbopack` is pinned to 4.6.7 by override (GHSA-mrhx-c7xc-m3w5) and `fflate` is 0.8.3 (GHSA-px8p-9vwx-vf98; a root devDependency, so the gate does not cover it, bumped because it is a patch). The lockfile also re-resolved `ts-jest` to the root `esbuild` 0.28.2 (it kept 0.25.12 before); unit, integration and security suites pass with it.
- [ ] **Production advisories left open on purpose (dependencies lane, PR A):** `deepmerge` 4.3.1 under `react-email > html-to-text` (GHSA-gcqx-w48m-w2h4, low, no patched version exists; its input is our own email templates, not user data) and `glob` / `minimatch` under `@sentry/nestjs > @sentry/node > @sentry/bundler-plugins` (GHSA-3g3m-2fjr-6pw4, GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249, moderate; a build-time plugin that does not run in the api, and forcing glob 14 over a major would break it). None blocks the gate (it stops at high). Closes in Step 7.3, before the api is exposed: re-run `pnpm audit --prod`, bump `@sentry/*` and `react-email` if they have published a release, and write the reason for whatever stays. The development-only advisories (axios, follow-redirects, brace-expansion, smol-toml under `nx`) close with the Nx 24 upgrade, a task of this step: **[ ] Upgrade Nx to 24 (`nx`, `@nx/js`, `@nx/eslint`, `@nx/eslint-plugin`, `@nx/jest` together, with `nx migrate`; replaces Dependabot #93 to #95, which are closed only when it merges)**, which is the dependencies lane's next PR.
- [ ] Secrets as Railway variables (no Secret Manager in the MVP), rotated with the procedure of `TDD_05 §1.1` adapted to Railway
- [ ] Weekly ZAP baseline against staging (the other scanning gates exist since Steps 0.5 and 0.6)
- [ ] Full internal pentest checklist executed (skip password, staff and billing items; magic-link token reuse and prediction do apply)
- [ ] **Exports leave the platform with personal data (debt of PRs D1 and E3c of Step 3.4):** the conversations export reads the assignment of each conversation when the file is made, not when it was asked (the requester's permissions are frozen in the row, the assignment is not). Decide with the pentest whether to freeze it: a conversation reassigned in the seconds between the request and the file could be included for a member who no longer sees it. If frozen, store the visible ids at request time for filter exports as the bulk export already does, and add the case to `tests/api/exports.spec.ts`.
- [ ] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), Business hours start in the browser's time zone for a role without `CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` (Q3, B-06):** the empty state of Settings → Business hours reads the zone of the store from `GET /settings/inbox/behavior` (`CONVERSATIONS_VIEW`), so a custom role that edits settings but cannot see conversations starts in the browser's zone. Give `GET /settings/business-hours` the zone of the store when the hours are empty and read it from there
- [ ] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), the hourly avatar limit has no code of its own (Q7a, (b)):** the avatar refusal for too many pictures cannot tell "five held" from "twenty signed urls in the last hour" (both are `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`), so its text says "several pictures in progress" for both. Give the hourly limit its own error code and text with the upload quota work of this step
- [ ] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), currency validation:** the currency `ZZZ` is accepted and the list holds obsolete currencies. Validate against the current list in the input validation pass and remove the obsolete ones

**Done when:** securityheaders.com grade A, the pentest checklist is clean, and the whole isolation suite passes as `platform_app` with RLS forced, including a deliberately unfiltered query that returns zero rows from another store.

tax-retention legal hold on orders, and the day-25 alert fires in a test.

### Step 7.3 — Deployment
- [ ] **MVP platform: Railway** (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7, D9). The Dockerfiles for api and worker (`docker/node-service.Dockerfile`, Step 0.5) and web (Step 3.1) already exist; this step adds PgBouncer and ClamAV as Docker services in the same Railway project (`admin` image and service DEFERRED). Google Cloud (Cloud Run, Cloud Build, Cloud SQL, Memorystore, Secret Manager) is the Phase 2 target
- [ ] **The websocket is public with the api** (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §10 item 34): set `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` to the proxies in front on Railway (the limit of handshakes by address reads the client from it), and add an edge rule that caps connections and handshakes per address (Cloudflare in front, `TDD_05 §3.3`, or the platform's own)
- [ ] **`/metrics` and the probes share the port of the service**, and a Railway public domain exposes every path of it: decide here how `/metrics` stays private (scrape over private networking and keep the public domain off the api, or a `METRICS_TOKEN` checked by the metrics controller) before the first deploy; `TDD_04 §2.2` assumes a network rule that Railway does not offer
- [ ] Railway project with staging and production environments; Postgres and Redis as Railway services; the worker always running (one instance minimum); secrets as Railway variables; private networking between services
- [ ] Release flow (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7, D13): merges to `main` deploy to staging automatically; production deploys only from a version tag with manual approval, as `TDD_03 §4` says. Migrations run before each deploy. Verify with Context7 how Railway deploys from a tag, how to require approval and how to run migrations before a deploy, and write that into `TDD_03 §4`
- [ ] Postgres on Railway's official image, pinned by **major** tag (`:18`; Railway supports 14 to 18 and does not support minor tags such as 18.6; PITR also requires the official image), so production runs Postgres 18 at whatever minor Railway publishes while local development keeps `postgres:18.6-alpine`; record this in `TDD_01 §2.4`. Backups: volume backups and PITR enabled from day one (`railway postgres pitr`), plus periodic `pg_dump` copies stored outside Railway
- [ ] Media stays on AWS S3 (versioning and replication per `TDD_03 §7.3`; the IAM role of the api and the worker holds `s3:ListBucket` besides get, put, copy and delete: without it S3 answers 403 for a missing key and `confirm-upload` fails with a 500 instead of `FILE_UPLOAD_FAILED`, and `HeadBucket` in `/health/ready` needs it too; only `S3_*` and `AWS_*` variables change). Railway buckets lack versioning and lifecycle rules
- [ ] Backup restore drill executed and documented (PITR restore into a new sibling service)
- [x] **`pnpm dev`, `dev:api`, `dev:web`, `dev:worker` and `dev:all` (debt of PR D2 of Step 3.3, owner 2026-11-01):** `TDD_14 §2` promises them and they do not exist. The api, web and worker have no `serve` target (only lint, type-check, test and build), so a laptop runs the `nx build` bundles by hand with no hot reload. Add the targets and the root scripts, and document them in the README **Done in QA fix PR Q8:** `pnpm dev` (api, worker and web), `dev:api`, `dev:worker` and `dev:web` exist as `serve` targets (`nest build --watch` plus `scripts/dev-service.ts` for the two Nest apps, `next dev` for the web); there is no `dev:all` or `dev:admin` because the MVP has no admin app. Documented in `TDD_14 §2` and `§3`. `pnpm infra:reset` is not part of it: see "Left by QA fix PR Q8" in the QA backlog.
- [ ] **Media memory and cleanup at scale (debt of PR B2 of Step 3.4):** (1) `InboundMediaFetcher` holds the whole file in memory (up to 25 MB, and about twice that at the peak while the chunks are joined, two jobs at a time): store it by streaming (multipart upload) if the worker memory budget measured here needs it; (2) `OrphanMediaCleaner` lists 30 pages of 1000 objects per run and keeps its cursor in Redis, so a large bucket is covered over several hours: measure how long a full turn takes and move to `DeleteObjects` batches or a per-store prefix if it is too slow
- [ ] **Backfill of ad pictures (debt of PR E4 of Step 3.4; owner agreed 2026-11-01):** origins recorded before PR E4 have no `Media` row for their picture. A one-off script queues `FETCH_ORIGIN_MEDIA` (job id `originMediaJobId`, deterministic, so it is safe to repeat) for each origin that has a link and no `Media` row; skip it when the database holds only test data at go-live
- [ ] **Old export rows (debt of PR D1 of Step 3.4):** `ExpiredExportsCleaner` deletes the file of an export and marks the row `expired`, but the row of a `failed` or `expired` export stays for ever. Delete them after a period (they hold no personal data, only the filters), in the same cleanup, with `ExportLimits` constants
- [ ] **A stalled bucket read hangs a ZIP job (debt of PR D2 of Step 3.4; closed in this step, 7.3):** `ZipExportWriter` waits for each file with no idle timeout; a connection that goes silent holds the job until its lock expires and the retry may write the same key. Destroy the file stream after an idle period so the stall is an ordinary retryable failure
- [ ] **Export generation at scale and scope (debts of PR D1 of Step 3.4, from its review):** (1) the conversations source runs one message query per conversation; read the messages of a page of conversations in one keyset query ordered by `(conversationId, createdAt, id)`. (2) `ExpiredExportsCleaner` reads the expired rows oldest first and a row whose file keeps failing to delete is read first on every run, so a hundred of them would starve the rest: move failed rows behind (a retry-after column or a cursor in Redis). (3) The conversations export applies the assignment of each conversation when the file is made, not when it was asked for, and the worker does not check that the requester is still a member with the permissions; freeze the set of conversation ids at the request (or check the membership and permissions in `ExportGenerator` before reading), and cover a reassignment and a removed member in `tests/api/exports.spec.ts`
- [ ] Cursor pagination, with a hard page size, on the lists of members, roles, pending invitations and stores (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §10 item 20)
- [ ] **The default rate limit counts per IP, not per person (QA FA-6, decided in QA fix PR Q4, rule 12):** `ThrottleProfiles.DEFAULT` (100 per minute) keys on the client address, so several agents behind one office address share it. It stays per IP in v1.2 on purpose: the throttler guard runs before `AuthGuard`, so the person is not known yet, and keying on the token without verifying it would let a caller choose a fresh key per request and never be counted. The fix is a second throttler, registered after the auth guard, keyed on `request.user.id` for authenticated routes, with the IP one kept for public routes; measure first with the real office address once the demo has users (`rate_limit_exceeded` per route in Grafana), then set the per-person number. Closes in this step with `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`, because the IP key is only right once the proxies are counted.
- [ ] **One config for the public API URL (debt (d) of PR A2 of Step 3.6, see the note of that step):** `PublicApiConfig` (`libs/core/http/src/public-api-platform.module.ts`) and `WidgetEmbedConfig.apiUrl` (`libs/channels/web-widget/src/widget-config.ts`, provided in `web-widget-platform.module.ts`) both read `API_URL`. The widget lib takes `PublicApiConfig` and `WidgetEmbedConfig` keeps only `scriptUrl`, so the public URLs of the deployment have one source. The only consumer of `apiUrl` is `apps/api/src/modules/widget/widget-settings.service.ts` (the connect-src origin and the embed snippet): `widget-settings.service.spec.ts` must still show the same snippet and origin.

If an event exists, it enters as `message_deleted` and the core already applies it. Until then the README lists edits and deletions by the customer as known limits (WhatsApp in particular)

A distinct code and message (`CHANNEL_ACCOUNT_AMBIGUOUS`) needs that service to let a `PluginError` code through, owned by the next PR that touches `ChannelConnectionsService`; (e) the token is the business's: after the owner assigns a second account to the system user, the stored token of a connection reaches both, and nothing re-checks it (a connection only checks that its own account is still among them); a check that no token reaches another connected account, and the pages after the first 100 accounts, wait for the next PR that touches the Instagram plugin; (g) re-subscribe `message_edit` on Instagram if the documentation of the unified login backs it (Meta's permission table for it does not list the field, and a name Meta refuses fails the connect): until then Instagram does not receive the customer's edits and the inbox keeps the old text; the parser is ready (`meta-messaging-events.ts`) and `MetaSubscribableFields.INSTAGRAM` still holds the name; (f) `MetaGraphRequest.origin` has a single value now: remove the parameter in the next PR that touches `MetaGraphClient`.
- [ ] **Instagram on the token of a Page (PR `feat/instagram-page-token`, owner, 2026-11-05) replaces the unified login for the connection:** no token of the unified login (system user) ever saw the owner's account, so `InstagramPlugin` now takes the token of the Facebook Page linked to the account (Meta: "Get started with Instagram Messaging", Messenger API support for Instagram). The account is read from `me?fields=id,instagram_business_account{id,username}`; the connection id stays the account's (`entry.id` of the webhooks) and the Page id is kept in `ChannelConnection.metadata.pageId`, which `healthCheck` compares (`PluginChecks.PAGE_ID_MISMATCH`); the app is subscribed on `me/subscribed_apps` with `messages,message_reactions,messaging_referrals`. The base plugin gained three hooks (`subscribeOwner`, `sendOwner`, `verifyBinding`) with the old behavior as default, so Messenger is unchanged. `PluginChecks.ACCOUNT_AMBIGUOUS`, `ACCOUNTS_EDGE` and the reading of `assigned_instagram_accounts` are gone (a Page links one account). **Needs the owner's real token before the live demo is announced (checks (a), (b) and (c) of the entry above now apply to this variant):** (a) `me/messages` with the Page token sends on Instagram, reacts, shows typing and marks seen (`InstagramApi.SEND_OWNER` is NOT verified against Meta's pages, the send guide of this variant could not be read; the alternative is `<pageId>/messages`, change that one constant); (b) `entry.id` of a real message webhook is the account's id; (c) the app in development mode delivers the webhooks to an account with a role (Meta's page asks for the app to be published; unconfirmed); (d) the token must come from a long-lived user token or the Page token expires in one hour; (e) the User Profile read (`<igsid>?fields=name,username,profile_pic`) with a Page token is unverified (a null profile is tolerated); (f) CLOSED by the subscription union PR (`feat/meta-subscription-union`, 2026-11-05): Messenger and Instagram share the Page and the app, and Meta does not say whether a POST to `subscribed_apps` replaces the fields or adds to them. A connect now reads what the app has on the node (`GET <node>/subscribed_apps`, our entry picked by the app id of `GET app?fields=id`), posts the union with the channel's own fields, and refuses without subscribing when it cannot read (`SUBSCRIPTION_READ_FAILED`, `SUBSCRIPTION_SHAPE_INVALID`, `SUBSCRIPTION_APP_UNKNOWN`); the order of connection no longer matters. Messenger's connect makes the same two reads and has the same refusals (f1 hits it too). Left, owner Step 7.3: (f1) `GET app?fields=id` with a Page token is NOT verified; if Meta refuses it the connect fails with `subscription_app_unknown` and the fix is an optional `META_APP_ID` (use it only when defined, never required); (f2) `disconnect` still removes nothing (the fields of a disconnected channel stay subscribed and the core drops those webhooks); removing only its own fields once no connection of that Page is left is not done; (f3) two connections of the same Page made at the same moment can overwrite each other's POST (one owner, accepted); (f4) CLOSED by the page field names PR (`fix/instagram-page-field-names`, 2026-11-05): Instagram subscribed the Page to `messaging_referral`, a name of the `instagram` object that the enum of the Page edge does not have (Meta's reference of `subscribed_apps`), and Meta answered error 100 on the POST of the connect; it now asks for `messages,message_reactions,messaging_referrals`, the specs and the fake Graph validate against `MetaSubscribableFields.PAGE` (the list the call reaches), and the log of a refused connect names the call (`account_read_failed`, `subscribe_failed`) beside the provider's numbers. Left, owner Step 7.3: (f5) NOT verified: that the `messages` of the Page is what delivers Instagram DMs, or the `instagram` topic of the app dashboard (Messenger > Instagram settings, Webhooks of the object `instagram`) is also needed; the owner's real test says; (f6) Meta's error text lists the valid names and the index of the refused one; it is deliberately not kept (only the numbers and the check name are), so the next diagnosis is the call name plus the Page enum in the code; (g) a user token pasted instead of a Page token makes Meta answer error 100 (\"nonexisting field\") on `me?fields=instagram_business_account`: the log shows `providerCode` 100, not `account_not_found`. Debts (rule 12): a message for the owner that tells a Page without a linked account from a token that lacks `instagram_basic` or is a user's (today both log `account_not_found` and the owner sees the generic credentials text; same owner as the clearer-error task below); `message_edit` is not a name of the Page edge, so Instagram does not receive the customer's edits.
- [ ] **A second Meta app for Instagram (PR `feat/instagram-own-app-secret`, owner, 2026-11-05):** Meta's Facebook Login for Business needs the portfolio that owns the app to differ from the one that owns the Instagram account, and `omnichannel-dev` and `@smartgadgetsv_` are both in the portfolio "Erick Martinez". The owner chose a new app owned by "Erick Ads", for Instagram only (WhatsApp and Messenger stay on `omnichannel-dev`, the account does not move). Built: `META_INSTAGRAM_APP_SECRET` (optional; the same length and not-the-verify-token rules as `META_APP_SECRET` in a deployed environment; an empty value is unset); `MetaAppConfig.instagramAppSecret`; `MetaGraphClient.isValidSignature(rawBody, headers, app)` with `MetaWebhookApps`: Instagram's webhooks are signed by the Instagram app's secret ALONE when it is set (the main secret no longer passes there) and by the main secret when it is not; WhatsApp and Messenger always answer to the main secret (the contract suite proves both). The verify token stays one, shared: the owner sets the same value in both apps. Owner steps: `~/qa-reportes-2026-11-04/instagram-app-nueva.md`. **Debt, consolidation later (one app for the three):** if Meta changes the rule or the app becomes a Tech Provider, delete `META_INSTAGRAM_APP_SECRET`, restart the api and the worker, generate the token with the main app and connect Instagram again: no code change. A connection made with the first app keeps working only while the variable matches the app that sends its webhooks; changing one without the other makes the webhooks of Instagram fail the signature check (HTTP 401 in the api log, no message reaches the inbox). The same happens to any Instagram webhook the MAIN app still delivers once the variable is set (the Instagram endpoint then accepts only the second app's key). Cutover order: the second app's Instagram webhook must be live before the variable is set (the main app's deliveries to the Instagram route are refused from then on). Accepted trade-offs (security review): the new variable is checked only in a deployed environment, like `META_APP_SECRET`, and neither secret is trimmed (a trailing space in a secrets store makes a different key); the verify token is shared by both apps (an administrator of the second portfolio learns it; it only answers the GET challenge and does not sign anything), and nothing at boot reports which key protects Instagram (the log of a refused webhook is the signal). A per-app verify token and a boot log of the mode wait for the next PR that touches the Meta webhook controller.
- [x] **Connecting a channel says why it failed (PR `feat/meta-connect-reasons`, owner, 2026-11-05; design in `~/qa-reportes-2026-11-04/meta-conexion-ux-diseno.md`):** every failure of `ChannelConnectionsService.connect` was `CHANNEL_CREDENTIALS_INVALID` ("the channel credentials are not valid") whatever the cause, and the real reason was only in the api log (hours of diagnosis for the owner). Now the code stays one and `metadata.reason` carries a name of the closed list `ChannelConnectFailureReasons` (`libs/core/domain/errors/channel-connect-failures.ts`: `token_malformed`, `token_rejected`, `permission_missing`, `request_rejected`, `account_not_linked`, `other_account`, `provider_answer_unexpected`, `subscription_not_confirmed`, `account_unreadable`, `subscription_failed`, `subscription_unreadable`, `provider_busy`, `provider_unavailable`). The plugin says it: `PluginError.reason` (optional, so third-party plugins still compile), derived by `connectReasonOfCheck` (an exhaustive `switch` over `PluginChecks`: a check added without a reason does not compile, and a test walks the list) or set by `graph-failure.ts` from Meta's numeric code (190 or 401 = token refused; 10, 200 to 299 or 403 = permission; 100 = request refused; throttles = busy; 5xx and outages = unavailable). The api sends only a name of the list (`connectFailureReasonOf` checks it again), never Meta's words, a value or an id; a page older than the server shows the generic message. The web reads it in `connectFailureReasonOf` (`error-message.ts`) and `useErrorText` shows `settings.channels.connectFailure.<reason>` (es and en); the connect form goes back to the token field (`aria-invalid`, the `role="alert"` of `MutationError`) when the reason is about the token. The health check keeps what the owner can fix (`token_rejected`, `permission_missing`, `other_account`, `account_not_linked`) in `lastError` (they are in `ChannelHealthErrors`) and the card offers "Connect again"; a busy or unavailable Meta stays `check_failed`.
- [x] **Update the token of a connection in its own card, and the connect dialog fits its box (PR `feat/channel-connection-ux`, PR A, owner, 2026-11-05):** a connection whose token expired had to be deleted and made again. `POST /channels/connections/:id/token` (`SETTINGS_EDIT`, audit `channel.token_updated`) takes only the secret fields; what the connection holds that is not secret stays, so the account cannot be changed through it. Order: (1) WHO the token belongs to is READ with the plugin's own `healthCheck` run on the credentials the row would have (the account id, and for Instagram the Page id, are compared there), so a token of another account or Page answers `other_account` with no subscription, no write and nothing of the other account in the body or the log; (2) only for the same account `connect` runs (it subscribes the app again, merging fields) and the sealed credentials of THAT row are replaced under `lockStoreForWrite`, conditional on the row still being active or in error (a disconnect that wins stays disconnected). The row, its name, its account id and its conversations are the ones it had; the old token is gone from the database. Another store's connection is the same 404 as one that does not exist. Web: "Update token" in the card (main button when the state says a new token fixes it), `UpdateTokenDialog` reuses `ConnectForm` with the secret fields only; it replaces "Connect again", which opened a blank form that created a second connection for another account. The dialog overflow of the owner's screenshot was measured on the real CSS (scrollWidth 545 over 510 at 1280 px): a grid column is `auto`, and the truncated address in a flex row sets its minimum width, so every `grid` wrapper in a dialog is now `grid-cols-1` (dialog and the settings and org components); an e2e checks 390 and 1280 px. Polish of #190: "expiró" and no "caduca", "venc", "webhook", "enlazada" in the failure texts (spec), a 403 with no Meta code gives no reason (the generic text shows), one `isPluginCheck` for the log and the error, the throttle codes 80007 and 80010-80013, an invented `FAKE_APP_ID`, and the CI trace of a failed e2e explained in `playwright.config.ts`.
- [x] **An editable name for each connection (PR `feat/channel-connection-label`, PR B, owner, 2026-11-05):** the owner wants "Instagram principal" and not the name Meta gave. New nullable column `channel_connections.label` (migration `20261110120000_channel_connection_label`, rollback `rollbacks/20261110120000_channel_connection_label.rollback.sql`, which drops it and loses the names). `displayName` of a view is now `label ?? the account's name`, so the list, the card, the toasts and the inbox directory all say the same; the view also carries `label` and `accountName` so that the form says what an empty name goes back to. `PUT /channels/connections/:id/label` (`SETTINGS_EDIT`, audit `channel.renamed`) takes only `{ label: string | null }` (strict: no other field), one visible line of 1 to 80 characters (`singleLineText`: trimmed, NFC, no control or invisible character; markup is kept as text and drawn escaped by React), `null` clears it; only the `label` column is written, for a connection of the store that is not disconnected, so the account id (`connectionId`), the credentials and the status are out of reach. A connect of the same account, a reconnect and a new token never write it (tested). Another store's connection is the same 404 as one that does not exist. The name is kept when a connection is disconnected and connected again (the row is reused), and is written to the audit trail with the one it replaced (a change only, never a repeat). Known limit, owner Step 7.3: `singleLineText` checks the length before the NFC normalization, so a label made of characters that expand under NFC can be stored a little over 80 characters (the column is text and nothing breaks); the fix belongs in the shared helper, which every name field uses. A public base url variable for the api (`env-schema.ts`, `.env.example`) would give the right address; no such variable exists today; owner Step 7.3; (c) the e2e of this PR (dialog size at 390 and 1280 px, update token, token of another Page) could not run on the author's laptop (ports 3000, 8080 and 8081 were the owner's dev servers) and was verified by CI; the CSS was measured in Chromium on the built stylesheet; (d) a plugin that has no secret field has no "Update token" (the web widget), and a third-party plugin whose `healthCheck` is not read-only would act on the account before the comparison: the contract of `healthCheck` is read-only and is stated in `channel-plugin.interface.ts` for that reason. (e) a refused token update is logged (`token update refused`, the closed reason, never the token) but not audited: only the success has an audit row, so an owner probing tokens of other accounts leaves no trail in `audit_log` and the route has no limit of its own; audit the refusal with its reason and rate-limit the route, owner Step 7.3; (f) when the saved credentials of a WhatsApp connection cannot be read, the number id and business account id they held are lost, so "Update token" cannot repair it (it answers `token_malformed`) and the owner disconnects and connects again; Meta and Instagram keep their identity in the row (`connectionId`, `metadata`) and are repaired, owner Step 7.3; (g) a 403 from Meta with no code of its own no longer says `permission_missing` (it was a guess): the connection is still put in error, with the generic text; the codes 80007 and 80010 to 80013 were added to the throttle list as the review of #190 asked, from Meta's list of business-use-case limits, and were not exercised against Meta.

- [ ] **Response headers of the bucket (QA fix PR Q6, SEC-3414):** the files the api signs are served by the bucket, not by the api, and S3 lets a signed URL set the type and the disposition only (`ResponseContentType` and `ResponseContentDisposition`, which the api fixes from the row: an uploaded file is always an attachment and the type is the one the bytes were checked against). It cannot set `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` or a `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox`. Put a response-headers policy (CloudFront, or the bucket's own) in front of the production bucket that adds `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` to every object, and check it with `curl -I` on a signed URL. The development bucket (SeaweedFS) does not add them; the check of the first bytes at the confirmation (`matchesDeclaredType`) is what keeps a page out of the bucket there.
- [ ] **A wrong credentials key flips every connection to error (left by QA fix PR Q13):** a connection whose saved credentials cannot be opened now gets the health verdict `credentials_unreadable` (status `error`, a `channel.disconnected` event and the notification of each store), and the next health round restores it once the key opens it again. A deploy with the wrong `CREDENTIALS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` therefore flips and notifies every store at once. Decide here whether the api and the worker check at start-up that a sample connection opens and refuse to boot otherwise, and put it in the deployment checklist next to the key rotation. Also: an outbound send with unreadable credentials is retried like any failure and its last attempt fails the message with `CHANNEL_CREDENTIALS_INVALID` (a worker with another key for a while loses nothing), and it does not touch the connection; the health round is what marks it, so the state follows within one round
- [x] **Messenger and Instagram sent files by a public https link (left by the WhatsApp media upload PR; closed by the Meta media upload PR, 2026-11-07):** the cause of "El canal no aceptó el mensaje" on every picture was local, not Meta: the development signed link is `http://localhost:8333`, and `meta-channel.plugin.ts` refused anything that was not a public https url before calling Meta (so the log has no `providerCode`). `sendMedia` now uploads the stored file and sends it in one call (`POST /<owner>/messages`, multipart, `recipient`, `message` and the file in `filedata`, no `attachment_id` and no `is_reusable`, as Meta says for upload-and-send), reading the bytes with `openFile` (checked again to be clean when opened) and never giving Meta a link. The content type is the one the platform recorded when it checked the file, never the client's; the type and the size are checked against a table per channel before the file is opened (`MessengerMediaRules`, `InstagramMediaRules` in `libs/core/domain/meta-media-rules.ts`: Messenger 8 MB for an image and 25 MB for the rest, no list of formats because Meta names none; Instagram png/jpeg/gif up to 8 MB, aac/m4a/wav/mp4 audio, mp4/ogg/avi/mov/webm video and **pdf only** as a file, 25 MB each), with `CHANNEL_FILE_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED` and the new `CHANNEL_FILE_TOO_LARGE`. The send is not repeated after a failure that may have reached Meta (`MetaGraphClient.uploadMedia` takes `fileField` and `safeToRepeat`). Instagram now declares `AUDIO` and `DOCUMENT`, which Meta's page supports. **Left, none blocking:** (1) not proved against a real Messenger or Instagram account (the `filedata` form against `/me/messages` with a Page token is as Meta's page shows it; the Graph fake of the specs is strict about the form): the owner's next real test of a picture, a PDF and a voice note decides it, and the documented formats may differ from what the account accepts; (2) **done in the capability map PR (2026-11-07):** the formats and sizes are the `media` declaration of each plugin (`ChannelPlugin.media`, `libs/core/domain/channel-media-rules.ts`), copied and frozen by the registry (`PluginRegistry.mediaOf`) and required for exactly the kinds a channel declares as capabilities; Messenger, Instagram, WhatsApp and the web widget declare it and the Meta plugins hold a file to it, but nothing in the API reads it yet (the PR of several files per message makes the API refuse a file before queueing it, and the composer offer only what the channel takes); until then the composer offers files by capability, so an agent can attach a docx or an mp3 on Instagram and gets a failed bubble with `CHANNEL_FILE_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED` after the message was queued: the API must refuse it before queueing once the map exists; (3) **done in the same PR:** `SendMediaParams.mediaUrl` is gone and the worker signs no link (no plugin read it; the SDK is in-process only until remote plugins exist in Phase 2, so no third-party plugin breaks, and that Phase must version this contract); (4) Messenger documents list no formats, so a type Meta refuses (100/2018047) arrives as a generic refusal; (5) the worker budget for a media send is now 205 s (upload 180 s, the caption post 15 s, a margin); a document is named by the plain-character name of its file, and a name with other characters is shown with underscores (a name with accents is not kept); (6) stickers and GIFs (WhatsApp) are not sent yet either

(b) An animation over the caps (`AnimatedImage`: 100 frames, 16 million pixels in all, sized for the two jobs the heavy queue runs at once), one whose bytes are not a gif or a webp, and one with an EXIF orientation (the encoder cannot rotate the frames of an animation by a quarter turn) is drawn still from its first frame and the original downloads as it came; a transcode that thins the frames or turns them is not built (no customer has asked, and decoding more is the risk the caps exist for). (c) A picture the worker never settles (the job died in the dead-letter queue) reads as a skeleton until its links were read eight more times (`VariantWait`: the poll of 15 seconds, so about two minutes, or sooner if events read them) and then as a download; the count lives in the query, so drawing the picture again does not start the wait over, and an event that does bring the variants later still repairs it. The same is true of the pictures the decoder refused before this PR, which have no `processedAt`. `orphan-media-cleaner.service.ts` (`settleStuckScans`) requeues only scans that are still `pending`: make it also queue the variants (`JobNames.PROCESS_IMAGE`, same job id) of a clean image with no `processedAt` older than an hour, so those rows settle by themselves; a test in `orphan-media-cleaner.integration.spec.ts`. (d) Stickers are not sent to a customer yet (WhatsApp accepts them by media id), nor does the composer offer them. Owner: Step 7.3.
- [ ] **Hardening of the media pipeline found by the review of the media PR, in code that PR did not change (rule 12):** (a) `HEAVY_PROCESSING` has `attempts: 2` with a 5 s backoff (`queue-job-options.ts`), so a scanner outage of about ten seconds makes the last attempt of `scan` run `giveUp`, which marks the file `UNSCANNABLE` and deletes it from the bucket; a customer file the provider will not hand over again is lost. Give the scan more attempts with a backoff of minutes, or give up by age of the row. (b) A store image is decoded three times (once per variant) with the 100 million pixel ceiling, against 25 million for an avatar, and the heavy queue runs two jobs at once: decode once to the big variant and derive the other two from it, lower `MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS`, and set sharp's processing timeout. (c) Every sharp error is final (`UnrecoverableError` for a message image; for an avatar `UNSCANNABLE` and the file deleted), including a resource error such as running out of memory, which the queue should retry; and a message image is decoded with sharp's default `failOn` while the avatar uses `failOn: 'error'`, so a photo with a flaw in its tail is refused. (d) `processAvatar`, unlike `processImage` (fixed in the media PR: guarded `updateMany` and the variant keys deleted when the row is gone), still writes with no guard on a row that may have been removed. (e) `processAvatar` deletes its deterministic final key when its update matches no row, even if a second run already finished and the row points at it: delete only when the row is gone. (f) In `media.service.ts` the confirmation drops the staging object and the requester key for any error of the signature check, including a transient bucket failure, and `dropStaging` logs the raw error and the key: clean up only for a refusal, and log `errorName`. (g) Tests: the pixel limit is tested only for avatars (add a store image), the give-up case does not assert that `MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` is announced, `signVariant` has no case for a key outside the store prefix, and `tests/api/media.spec.ts` writes `3_600_000` and status codes as literals (`QUOTA_WINDOW_MS`, `HttpStatusCodes`). Owner: Step 7.3.

(SEC-R1-03, a suspicion not run) an incoming text or place name from a Meta channel with a NUL (`libs/channels/whatsapp/src/whatsapp-events.ts`, `placeTextOf`) is not rejected by `channelEventSchema`, and Postgres refuses NUL in `text` and `jsonb`, so the job would retry until the dead letter and the message would be lost: confirm with an integration test that feeds a signed event, and then use `safeText` (`libs/core/domain/text.ts`) at the boundary. (a) NOT-06, **done (PR `fix/server-robustness`)**: the notification `message.failed` carries `params.reason` (the error code of the event) and the page draws `notifications.message.failed.reason.<CODE>` for the seven reasons that have a sentence (window expired, credentials, capability, file type, template, not connected, rate limit); any other code keeps the plain text. (b) MED-11, **done (same PR)**: `export.failed` carries `params.reason` from the `error` of the export row, with a sentence for `EXPORT_TOO_LARGE` and `EXPORT_EMPTY`; the generic `EXPORT_FAILED` keeps the plain text. The bell, the email digest and the push read the same helper (`reasonNameOf`), and the reasons with a sentence are listed in `NotificationFailureReasons` (generated keys, so `validate:translations` fails if one lacks its text). Old notifications saved without a reason keep the plain text. Accepted limit: an identical rule asked on purpose inside the window (a rule deleted and made again within a minute) returns the old one, and after the window the same request makes a second rule, as it always did; a client `Idempotency-Key` with a unique index would be exact, and needs a migration with its rollback (Step 7.1). (d) the store `PATCH` and `DELETE` (`/organizations/current/stores/:id`) **done (same PR)**: a cache that cannot be dropped is logged and the answer stays 200/204; other call sites of `invalidateStore` were not changed. The tenant cache expires on its own (`CacheTTL.VERY_SHORT`), so a deleted or renamed store can still be resolved by the guards for that time when Redis is down; nothing retries the invalidation. (e) The original of an image keeps its EXIF and GPS data (only the variants are re-encoded): decide with the owner whether the original is served at all or stripped on upload, a privacy decision; until then the download of an image is the original. (f) Names already stored with a line break or an invisible character (before this fix) are shown as stored; a one-off cleanup of `customers.name` and `customer_identities.display_name` with the `cleanText` rule, and the search by name that ignores accents ("nandu" finds "Ñandú"). (g) The limit of a name counts UTF-16 units when a person types it (`singleLineText`) and characters when a channel reports it (`cleanText`), so a name of 70 emoji can arrive from a channel and be refused on the next edit; count both by characters. (h) Other names that are drawn in lists still take any text: the organization name, the invitation's name, the address label, line and city, the display name of a connection and the name and address of a location a channel sends; give them `singleLineText` or `cleanName`. (a) IG-07, **link done (PR `fix/channels-p3`)**: the story an Instagram customer replied to (`reply_to.story.url`) is kept in `metadata.attachmentUrl` (type `story_reply`) and drawn by `SharedLink`, as a shared post is; what the customer shared wins over it. **Still open:** the picture of the story is not fetched (the link expires within a day, and Meta's reference does not say the CDN allows a server to read it), and the story is not drawn as a quote (`replyTo`), because a story is not a message of the thread. (b) MS-04, **done (same PR)**: the cause of the silent `[image]` was the webhook parser, which dropped any attachment whose link was not a public web name (`httpUrl` refuses a literal IP); it now hands the raw link to the worker, which refuses it with `unsafe_link` and the notice on the message, like `http://` or a port; a link to a host that is not a Meta CDN (`downloadMediaUrl`) raises `UnsafeMediaLinkError` (plugin sdk) and the worker marks it `unsafe_link` instead of `fetch_failed`. The raw link of the customer (bounded, not blank, never shaped like a plain id of the provider) travels in the job and is refused by the worker before anything is fetched; it is not stored on the message. A link that is only a bare id is still dropped, as before. Left: `UnsafeMediaLinkError` is an `Error`, not a `PluginError`, so only the inbound fetcher classifies it (the other callers of `fetchMedia` see an unknown error and retry); a story reply that carries a file keeps the file and not the story link. (c) WW-23: under a CSP that blocks the widget's API (`connect-src 'self'`) the launcher never appears and the page says nothing; the bootstrap could log one `console.warn` naming `connect-src` and the API origin when its first request is blocked. (d) The `[type]` marker still reaches the quote of a reply, the notifications and the digest as English text (they read `content`, not the message view), and a message stored before the mark `metadata.unsupportedType` existed still shows its marker; give those readers the same rule as the view (the mark plus `content` equal to `[type]`) or store the marker as `null` content with the type in metadata, which the core refuses today (a text with no text). The download name (`downloadFilename`) is applied where a person downloads from the inbox (`media.service`), not in `StorageService`, because the links the worker hands to the providers carry their own filename; the outbound document names a customer receives (`a.pdf.exe` sent by an agent) are not renamed. Owner: Step 7.3 for (a) and (b), Step 7.7 for (c) and (d).

(b) CAN-13, **decided, nothing to change (PR `fix/channels-p3`)**: a store may hold several connections of the same channel (one per Page), so connecting another Page does not invalidate the old one and it stays `active`; the matrix expected `error`, which is the discrepancy of the matrix and not a failure. The only case that replaces a Page is the token update of a connection, which refuses another Page (`other_account`). (c) ORG-03, **done (PR `fix/settings-robustness`)**: `DELETE /organizations/current/stores/:id` takes `{ confirmation }` and answers 400 unless it equals the name of the store (the rule of the screen, `phraseMatches` in core, now shared); a store the caller cannot see is still a 404 before the phrase is read. The web and the e2e helper send it. The confirmation travels as a JSON body of the `DELETE`: a gateway that drops bodies of a `DELETE`, or a web bundle cached from before the deploy, gets a 400 (a deploy-time risk for Step 7.3); it only guards against an accidental delete, since a script can read the name and send it back. The permission, the last-store rule and the data check stay the real guards. (d) WA-22: the 100 MB limit of a WhatsApp video is proved only by the spec of the limits, not with a real file (needs the streaming of the body above).
- [ ] **A member who leaves with many conversations (from the review of PR `fix/channels-notices`, rule 12; owner Step 7.1/7.3):** suspending or removing a member publishes one `CONVERSATION_ASSIGNED` event per worked conversation it frees, inside the transaction (`MembersService.backToTheQueue`), so a person with hundreds of open conversations makes the removal longer and fans out as many events after the commit. Put a cap or a batch event (a payload with a list of ids) on it, with a test that measures the number of events for a large set.

Real network noise covers it almost entirely, so nothing changed. If it ever matters, make the unknown-account branch do the same work (a dummy link and a no-op enqueue) or move both to the queue and answer first. ### Step 7.4 — Monitoring
- [ ] Sentry for errors, an external uptime check on `/health/ready` and Railway's own service metrics, plus **Grafana Cloud** (free plan, `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7, D12): a Grafana agent runs as a Docker service in the Railway project, scrapes `/metrics`, and the alert rules of `TDD_04 §6` (queue depth, dead letter, `TenantViolation`, error rate, latency) load there. Verify the free plan's limits with its documentation when building this step **Web image (left by PR E1):** `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` is likewise not a build argument of `apps/web/Dockerfile` (nothing reads it yet): when the browser reports to Sentry, add it as an `ARG`/`ENV` there, because the CSP of `proxy.ts` only allows the endpoint the image was built with
- [ ] Alerts go to one Slack channel with push notifications on the owner's phone (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7, D6); no PagerDuty; no AI alert or dashboard panels

When that count appears among the slow queries, or a store passes a few hundred thousand identities, add `(store_id) WHERE profile_fetch_error IS NOT NULL` as raw SQL in a migration with its rollback (rule 4; out of `schema.prisma` like the other partial indexes).
- [ ] Status page on separate infrastructure
- [ ] Runbooks written in `docs/runbooks/` (no `ai-kill-switch.md`)

Nothing makes a row fail for good today. Add an alert on the warn line of these two jobs and, if it ever fires, a retry counter or a skip list
- [ ] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), `media.processed` arrives twice and the dead-letter queue holds jobs with no consumer:** the QA found `media.processed` delivered twice and 37 jobs in the dead-letter queue. Find why the event is emitted twice, and look into the 37 dead-lettered jobs with the dead-letter alert of this step (the dead-letter queue has no processor by design: `apps/worker/src/processors/queue-coverage.spec.ts` names Step 7.4 as what watches it)

Each mismatch is fixed or recorded as a decision of the owner before any visual change starts; the plan is read after this walk, not before
- [ ] **Settings leftovers of Step 3.6 (rule 12, from PRs B2, C, E1, E2 and F1):** a test of the drag and drop reorder of routing rules (a component test with the DOM environment above, or a Playwright drag that is reliable on native HTML5 events; today only the buttons are in the e2e, PR E1 limit (1)); component tests, once the DOM environment above exists, for `AvatarPicker` (processing, refused and polling states), the uninstall dialog (it counts the accounts it read when it opened) and the widget card (the sites text is lost if the section is collapsed); cancel an avatar upload when the person leaves the page, and free the row of an upload whose attach failed; the picture of a teammate in the team list and the inbox (a signed link per teammate or a copy to a public-read key); keep the previous profile picture until the new one is ready (the pointer of the profile would hold two ids; today the old one is deleted when the new one is attached, so for the seconds the worker takes the person has initials); the unsaved-changes warning on the browser's back button, on the store navigation, on the brand link of the top bar (it leaves a dirty form with no warning, as the store switcher does) and on the editors that are dialogs (rule, tag, snippet, role); Team: warn before a person removes themselves or changes their own role, refresh the permissions of the page when the person's own role changes (today they stay those of the session until it reloads), and search and paging for the member list; Profile: stop the two-minute limit of the picture polling from leaving "Getting your picture ready" for good (a retry button) and use the density that is saved and read by nothing. The e2e specs sign in as the same seeded addresses (`admin@test.com`, `agent@test.com`), and the magic link is limited to `MagicLinkThrottle` (5 per minute per address), so on a laptop a fast batch or a quick rerun leaves a test with "no email for admin@test.com in Mailpit" (measured in PR G: seven reruns of one spec in 70 s fail from the third; CI is slower and retries once); a `signIn` helper that gives each spec its own address, or waits out the window, closes it. The e2e of the Done when leaves one store per run on a laptop database, because the product will not delete a store that holds a conversation (a delete that removes what it holds, or a reset of the e2e data, closes it). Suspending and reactivating members is built in PRs H1 and H2 of this step, not here (see Team above) (From PR H2) A request of a suspended person that meets 403 `TENANT_MEMBER_SUSPENDED` with no socket to move them (the api client) should send them to "/" like the socket does.
- [x] **Realtime event when a member's conversations are unassigned (debt (a) of PR H1 of Step 3.6, see the note of that step) — done (PR `fix/channels-notices`, QA EQ-14/EQ-15): `MembersService.suspend` and `remove` now publish `CONVERSATION_ASSIGNED` (`assignedTo: null`, `previousAssignee`) for each conversation they free, inside the transaction, so the other agents' sockets learn it and the queue notification is raised for who can take it (not for the person who left, nor for the one who did it); tests in `team.spec.ts` for both paths. Only the rows that update really changed are announced, and only the worked ones (open, pending, snoozed); the finished ones also leave with the member but without an event. The tests listen to the events on the in-process emitter and read the notifications; the existing realtime listener tests cover the fan-out to sockets, so there is no new test with a real socket. Left: the unassignment leaves no system line in the thread (a manual unassign writes one), and the departing member's still-open socket may receive the event in the instant before it is closed (the payload carries ids, not text); the notification is raised only for OPEN conversations, as the queue rule always was. Original text:** suspending a member (and removing one) leaves their open, pending and snoozed conversations unassigned and back in the queue in the database, but the inbox lists of the other agents do not hear it and show the old assignee until their next read. Emit the reassignment event (`CONVERSATION_ASSIGNED`, `libs/core/domain/realtime/realtime-events.ts`) for each conversation the suspend and remove paths unassign, with a test that a second agent's socket receives it. The same event also closes the second half of this debt (QA round 2, EQ-14 and EQ-15): the notification `conversation.unassigned_queue` ("a conversation went back to the queue", `notifications-inbox.listener.ts`, which listens to `CONVERSATION_ASSIGNED` with `assignedTo: null`) is not raised either when a member is suspended (`members.service.ts`, `updateMany` of the suspend path) or removed (the same call in the remove path), so the team is not told that those conversations are waiting. The test must also assert that notification for the other members who can take them, and that the person who is suspended or removed does not get it.

(b) `originOf` now refuses a `*` in the host, but a connection saved before with `https://*.shop.test` keeps it in `metadata` (it never matched anything, so it is harmless): the form shows the generic "not an origin" error on the next save, so add a wildcard-specific message to `validation` and drop the old rows when the owner saves. The example env stays a bare address. **Still open of this item:** The invitation to an address with no account is written in the language of the store, not of the inviter; the decision of 2026-11-05 says it must be documented: say it in `TDD_09` and in the invitation email test. - [ ] **Room under the script ceiling of the inbox (from the QA round 2 PRs `fix/text-pass-p3` and `perf/inbox-bundle-room`; owner: this step, linked to the item above):** lighthouse runs on pull requests and not on `main`, so the inbox crept up to 340.00 KB of its 340 KB ceiling (`ScriptCeilingsKb`) across five small PRs and every web change after that fought for tenths. The first real saving is done: the browser no longer ships the full `ErrorCodes` table (about 120 codes, one binding for the bundler) but `ClientErrorCodes` (22 codes, drift-checked by a spec in `libs/core/domain`), and the page asks the messages which codes it knows (`useCodeText`); a spec fails if browser code imports the full table again. Measured as the sum of the gzip of the chunks the inbox loads, same base: 196.185 KB before, 195.175 KB after (-1.01 KB; the error table is gone from those chunks). The CI number of the inbox (the one against the ceiling) goes with it: 340.00 before; the first run of this change gives the new one, which this item records. **Permissions, done (PR `perf/permissions-client-table`):** the browser reads `ClientPermissions` (the 30 permissions the web checks, drift-checked against `Permissions` by a spec in `libs/core/domain/auth`), a spec in `apps/web/lib` fails if browser code reads the full table anywhere but `permission-groups.ts`, and the role dialog, the only reader of the full table (the matrix of a role), is a lazy chunk (`next/dynamic` in `team-roles-panel.tsx`). Measured by the lighthouse job (the line it now prints on every run): the inbox was at 339.84 KB of 340 on `main` before the change and is at 339.14 KB with it (room 0.86 KB; login 252.60 of 260). Accepted and left: the guard spec looks for the name `Permissions` only (a namespace import or another library that re-exports the table would pass it), the lazy role dialog has no loading fallback and no error boundary for a chunk that fails to load, and the drift between the tables is checked in one direction (a key missing from the client table is a compile error). **Still to do:** code-splitting what is not on the critical path of the inbox (the settings and customers code that rides in shared chunks), a single source for `phraseMatches` (the web keeps a local copy of the rule of `libs/core/domain/text.ts` because the domain barrel cost bytes of the inbox: bring them back to one when there is room, with a test that runs both with the same cases), and a lighthouse run on `main` (or a nightly one) so a creep is seen when it happens. **NOT-21** (the notification tone) is built, +0.10 KB measured (see the text pass item above). The lighthouse job used to print the number of the inbox only when it failed, so the CI number of #201 was not recorded (it passed, under 340). **Done (PR `ci/lighthouse-print-numbers`):** every run prints one line per page (`[lighthouse] inbox: scripts X KB of 340 KB (room Y KB), FCP, LCP, TTI, TBT, CLS`) to the log of the job (the `line` reporter is on in CI; whether the `github` reporter alone would show it was not checked) and to the summary of the run, so the room is seen on every pull request. It also lists the 25 heaviest scripts of the load, one line each (`[lighthouse] inbox script 36.20 KB chunks/...js`), and how much of the figure the listing explains, so the ceiling can be read by file (PR `ci/lighthouse-script-breakdown`). Limits: log only (not in the run summary), taken from the run that is the median of the scripts, silent (an empty listing) when the audit is missing from the report, and the format of the lines was not checked against a real run when it was written. Still open: that number is on pull requests only; the lighthouse run on `main` or nightly (below) is what shows a creep between them.
